# TinapayanRMS — Project Guide & Build Plan

Product recipe management & costing app for Tinapayan Festival Bakeshoppe. Tracks ingredient expenses, recipe/product margins, and cascades ingredient and packaging price changes up through sub-assemblies and finished products automatically.

## Tech Stack

- **Language / Runtime**: C# 14, .NET 10 (`net10.0`; `net10.0-windows10.0.19041.0` for the UI and Tests projects — ReactiveUI.WPF requires at least that Windows SDK version, and the bare `net10.0-windows` moniker resolves to `windows7.0`, which is too low)
- **UI**: WPF, styled with **Syncfusion Essential Studio for WPF 34.2.7** and Syncfusion's Windows11Light theme (the official `Syncfusion.Themes.Windows11Light.WPF` package), accent-recoloured at runtime
- **MVVM**: ReactiveUI (24.x). This version requires explicit builder-pattern initialization before any `ReactiveObject` is touched — `RxAppBuilder.CreateReactiveUIBuilder().WithCoreServices().WithWpf().BuildApp()` — done once per process by `src/TinapayanRMS.UI/ReactiveUIBootstrapper.cs`. It also dropped `System.Reactive` as a dependency, so that package is referenced directly wherever Rx LINQ operators (`.Select`, `.Where`, …) are used on an `IObservable<T>`.
  - **`ReactiveCommand.Create`/`CreateFromTask`'s parameterless overloads return `ReactiveCommand<RxVoid, RxVoid>`** (`ReactiveUI.Primitives.RxVoid`), not `ReactiveCommand<Unit, Unit>` — this fork doesn't use `System.Reactive.Unit` for that. Import the type via a using-alias (`using RxVoid = ReactiveUI.Primitives.RxVoid;`), never `using ReactiveUI.Primitives;` wholesale — that namespace also carries its own C# 14 extension-member `Select`/`Where`/`Subscribe`, which collide (CS0121, ambiguous call) with `System.Reactive.Linq`'s the moment both are in scope over the same observable.
- **Reactive collections**: DynamicData
- **Persistence**: EF Core 10.0.12 → SQL Server (db name `TinapayanRMS`) for writes, the schema and migrations; **Dapper** (2.1.x) for reads (see Conventions, "Reads through Dapper, writes through EF Core")
- **DI / Hosting**: `Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting` + `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection`, wired via `HostBuilder` in `src/TinapayanRMS.UI/CompositionRoot.cs`, called from `App.xaml.cs`
- **Validation**: FluentValidation
- **Logging**: Serilog (rolling file sink)
- **Charts**: Syncfusion SfChart (`Syncfusion.SfChart.WPF`) for margin/price-trend reporting — replaces the originally-planned LiveCharts2
- **Testing**: xUnit v3, AwesomeAssertions, Respawn. Integration tests run against a locally installed SQL Server instance (such as LocalDB or SQL Server Express), and Respawn resets that database between tests. `AwesomeAssertions` replaces `FluentAssertions`: FluentAssertions 8+ requires a paid commercial license for use in a for-profit product, and AwesomeAssertions is a same-API, MIT-licensed fork of the last free FluentAssertions 7 release, created for exactly this situation. `NSubstitute` was referenced but never used — the suite exercises real repositories against a real Respawn-reset database rather than mocking — and its package reference was removed during a code-quality pass.
- **Solution format**: `.slnx`
- **Build settings** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "centralize my build settings"): `Directory.Build.props` at the repo root sets `TargetFramework` (`net10.0`), `ImplicitUsings` and `Nullable` for every project, plus a `WindowsTargetFramework` property that the UI and Tests projects use as their `TargetFramework`. `Directory.Packages.props` enables Central Package Management: every package version is a `PackageVersion` there (EF Core and Syncfusion through the `EFCoreVersion`/`SyncfusionVersion` properties), and a project's `PackageReference` has no `Version`. A `.csproj` only holds what is unique to it. **Code analysis**: `AnalysisLevel` is `latest-recommended`, the SDK's built-in .NET analyzers (no NuGet package) at their Recommended rule set; the Default set reported nothing and All reported about 1,700 warnings, mostly style (`ConfigureAwait`, argument null checks, `internal` types). The root `.editorconfig` marks `Migrations/` as generated code (they can't be hand-edited) and turns off CA1707 in `tests/`, whose method names use underscores, and CA1716 everywhere (it flags `IPriceHistoryEntry.Date` for being a Visual Basic keyword; the app is C# only and the name is a database column). Warnings are not errors, but the build had none once they were resolved (2026-09-17): name equality checks are `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, sorts for display stay `StringComparer.CurrentCultureIgnoreCase`, display dates format with `CultureInfo.CurrentCulture` and the Serilog file sink with `InvariantCulture`, and the six ViewModels that own a DynamicData `SourceCache` are sealed and `IDisposable`, so the host disposes the caches with its singletons at exit.
- **`dotnet test`**: the .NET 10 SDK dropped VSTest support for `dotnet test`; running under the new Microsoft.Testing.Platform mode needs a `test.runner` setting in `global.json` at the repo root (already added). Run tests with `dotnet test --solution TinapayanRMS.slnx`.
- **Syncfusion license key**: read from the `SYNCFUSION_LICENSE_KEY` Windows user environment variable and registered in `App`'s static constructor (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/App.xaml.cs`), before anything else in the process touches a Syncfusion type. Running the app or its tests on another machine requires that variable to be set there too.

## Solution Structure

```
TinapayanRMS.slnx
├── Directory.Build.props             — shared compiler settings and target frameworks
├── Directory.Packages.props          — Central Package Management: every package version
├── src/
│   ├── TinapayanRMS.Domain            — entities and enums (no external deps)
│   │   ├── Entities/                  — the persisted domain types
│   │   ├── Enums/
│   │   └── Interfaces/                — contracts shared by entities (none since 2026-09-18, when IPriceHistoryEntry went with PriceHistoryRules)
│   ├── TinapayanRMS.ApplicationCore   — business and costing rules, repository contracts, validation
│   │   ├── Interfaces/                — repository/service contracts that Infrastructure implements
│   │   ├── Rules/                     — static business rules and calculations (*Rules, UnitConversionRules, IngredientCostGraph)
│   │   └── Validators/                — FluentValidation validators
│   ├── TinapayanRMS.Infrastructure    — EF Core; TinapayanRMSDbContext and its design-time factory at the root
│   │   ├── Configurations/            — one IEntityTypeConfiguration<T> per entity
│   │   ├── Migrations/                — generated by `dotnet ef migrations add` and never moved; since 2026-09-15 never hand-written or hand-edited either (the user's instruction; earlier ones carry hand-added data-conversion SQL)
│   │   ├── Queries/                   — Dapper reads: DapperReads (connection/transaction helpers), Columns (every entity's column list), RawMaterialReads and SubAssemblyReads (shared graph loaders)
│   │   ├── Repositories/              — one implementation per ApplicationCore repository interface
│   │   └── Services/                  — cross-entity workflows (the cost propagators, ProductCloner)
│   └── TinapayanRMS.UI                — WPF + Syncfusion + ReactiveUI; app startup (App, CompositionRoot, …) at the root
│       ├── Views/                     — MainWindow, SettingsWindow and one UserControl per screen or Settings tab (.xaml + code-behind)
│       │   └── RawMaterials/          — Raw Materials' cards, one UserControl each, inheriting the screen's DataContext
│       ├── ViewModels/                — ViewModelBase and one ViewModel per View
│       ├── Controls/                  — reusable UserControls (ErrorToast, SuccessToast, UnitConversionOverview), grid columns (NumericColumn) and attached behaviors (GridFillerRows, GridEditCommit, GridAddNewRow, GridRowReordering, AutoCompleteDropDown, NumericEditing, MouseWheelForwarding, FocusScrolling)
│       └── Converters/                — IValueConverters
└── tests/
    └── TinapayanRMS.Tests             — xUnit v3, AwesomeAssertions; CompositionRootTests at the root
        ├── Rules/                     — unit tests for ApplicationCore/Rules (no database)
        ├── Validators/                — unit tests for ApplicationCore/Validators (no database)
        ├── Persistence/               — integration tests against the Respawn-reset test database
        ├── Queries/                   — ColumnsTests: the Dapper column lists against EF's model (no database)
        └── Fixtures/                  — DatabaseFixture, DatabaseCollection, PersistenceTestBase
```

**Folder = namespace, grouped by kind.** Every folder inside a project is a sub-namespace of it (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/ViewModels/` → `TinapayanRMS.UI.ViewModels`). A new file goes in the folder for its kind, never at the project root. Only app-startup plumbing (`App`, `CompositionRoot`, `ReactiveUIBootstrapper`, `ConfirmDeleteHandlerRegistration`) and the DbContext pair live at a project root. Code in a sub-namespace sees its parent namespaces without a `using`: a View calls `RegisterNativeMessageBoxHandler` from `TinapayanRMS.UI`, and a repository uses `TinapayanRMSDbContext` from `TinapayanRMS.Infrastructure`. Sibling folders need a `using`. The solution was reorganized into this layout after the Phase 16 UI work: projects moved into `src/`/`tests/`, and each project's files went from flat to grouped by kind. Moving the entities to `TinapayanRMS.Domain.Entities` left the EF model unchanged (`dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes` reported no changes), because table names come from the DbContext, not from entity namespaces. `Migrations/` was therefore left untouched.

The Windows11Light theme is not a project in the solution: it's Syncfusion's official `Syncfusion.Themes.Windows11Light.WPF` NuGet package (see Syncfusion UI Framework below).

## Domain Model

- **UnitOfMeasure**: `Id`, `Name`, `Abbreviation` — nothing else. **No unit is seeded or protected by a fixed id** — the table starts empty on a fresh database, and the shop owner adds every unit they need (gram, piece, mm, cm, in, m, or any custom unit like a sack or roll) in Settings → Units of Measure (see Application Architecture below), the same as any other user-managed lookup. **A unit carries no conversion factor of its own.** Conversions live on each raw material instead (see Multi-unit materials below): every material has a **base unit** its quantities and costs are tracked in (always the gram for an ingredient; chosen per packaging material), plus any number of **alternative units**, each defined as a multiple of that base unit. Length units (`IsLengthUnit`/`MillimetersPerUnit`/`LengthConversion`) were removed on 2026-09-13 to make way for this.
- **RawMaterial: ingredients and packaging share one table, told apart by `Type` (`Ingredient` or `Packaging`).** Merged on 2026-09-13 at the user's request ("the difference between the two is too thin"). Until then they were two tables, `Ingredient` and `PackagingMaterial`, each with its own category, unit and price history tables, and the schema itself kept packaging out of recipes. The merge kept the three differences that are real, as rules on the type:
  - **Base unit**: an ingredient's is always the gram, so its `BaseUnitId` is null — recipes, BOMs and sub-assembly weights all add up grams. A packaging material picks its own (`BaseUnitId` required).
  - **Source**: only an ingredient can be `Made` (a BOM and a weight loss) or `Linked` (a share of another ingredient's cost); packaging is always `Purchased`.
  - **Where it can be used**: sub-assembly lines and BOM lines take ingredients only; a product's packaging lines take packaging only.
  - `RawMaterialRules.EnsureFieldsMatchType`/`EnsureFieldsMatchSource` run in `RawMaterialRepository` before every save, backed by two check constraints (`CK_RawMaterials_BaseUnitMatchesType`, `CK_RawMaterials_OnlyIngredientsAreMade`). Type and source are fixed once created (`EnsureTypeUnchanged`/`EnsureSourceUnchanged`). Each line repository checks every line's type (`EnsureUsableInRecipes`/`EnsureUsableAsPackaging`) before it replaces anything, with a message naming the material, and the pickers only offer the right type. A name is unique across both types.
  - The line entities kept their role names: `SubAssemblyLine.Ingredient`, `IngredientBomLine.Ingredient`/`ComponentIngredient` and `PackagingLine.PackagingMaterial` are all `RawMaterial`s. A packaging line's unit is `UnitId` (a `RawMaterialUnit`); recipe lines have had no unit since 2026-09-14 (they're in grams — see Ingredients). `IngredientCostPropagator` and `SubAssemblyCostPropagator` kept theirs too, since they only ever deal with ingredients. (`IngredientQuantityConverter`, which turned recipe lines entered in a unit into grams, went with those units on 2026-09-14.)
  - **`CostPerBaseUnit`** (stored, derived): the true cost of one base unit — one gram, for an ingredient (it was `Ingredient.CostPerGram`).
  - **Buffered cost** (2026-09-14, at the user's request): a per-material **`CostBufferPercent`** (0–100, default 0; `RawMaterialRules.EnsureValidCostBuffer`, the validator, and `CK_RawMaterials_CostBufferPercent`) is a cushion for price swings, waste and yield variation. **`BufferedCostPerBaseUnit`** (stored, derived) is the cost with it on top (`RawMaterialRules.BufferedCost`, the only buffer formula). **It's the value every sub-assembly line, BOM rollup and packaging line uses**, so products are costed and priced with it; `CostPerBaseUnit` stays the true cost, shown beside it on the Raw Materials screen and in Price History.
    - Purchased: **a fixed amount** (since 2026-09-19, at the user's request: "the cost buffer is a fixed amount without relying on the maximum cost of raw materials in the list. But it can be reinitialize"). Brand prices don't move it. `ApplyBrandPrices` only works it out, as `CostPerBaseUnit × (1 + buffer / 100)`, the first time the material is priced (while it's still 0), and sets it back to 0 when the last price goes. Otherwise it changes only when the screen sends a new one: typed, or from typing the Cost Buffer % (the dearest brand as typed with the % on top; until 2026-09-21 a **Recalculate** button did this, see Raw Materials). `RawMaterialRepository.UpdateAsync` writes the form's amount (0 sent means "work it out from the cost and the %", and `SaveAsync` does that from the new prices when it has them) and cascades an ingredient whose buffered cost changed. `EnsureValidBufferedCost` (and the validator) rejects a negative amount; one below the cost is allowed, and the screen warns about it. So since 2026-09-19 a price change alone reaches product costs only through the true cost, and the buffered cost everything is costed with waits for the screen to send a new one. `CostBufferPercent` is kept as the % that puts the buffer back on top. No migration: existing materials kept the buffered cost they had.
    - Made: the BOM rollup of the components' *buffered* costs, and nothing more. **Since 2026-09-21 a made ingredient adds no buffer of its own** (at the user's request: "there should never be a buffered cost % in the cost per gram because the ingredients use is already has a buffered cost"), so a buffer is counted once however deep the tree, replacing the stacking the bullet used to describe. `RawMaterialRules.StoredCostBufferPercent` is the one place that says so: `RawMaterialRepository`'s Add and Update store a made ingredient's `CostBufferPercent` as 0 whatever the form sends (rejecting it instead would leave the three rows that already had one unsaveable, since the screen has no buffer box for a made ingredient), and `IngredientCostPropagator` sets it to 0 on every recompute, which is what cleans a *dependent* made row the propagator reaches without a save. The dev database's stale percents (Butter Icing 84.4147%, Ube Filling 16.9973%) were cleared by re-saving each row, taking Butter Icing's buffered cost from ₱0.4374 to ₱0.2372/g. A linked part of a made ingredient now inherits 0%, which is right: it takes a share of an already-buffered cost.
    - Linked: the same share of the linked-to ingredient's buffered cost as its true cost is of the true cost (`LinkedCost(linkedTo.BufferedCostPerBaseUnit, …)`, since 2026-09-19, so a part follows the linked-to ingredient's fixed amount). Until then it was the part's true cost with the inherited percent on top.
    - It's stored, not computed on read, because a made ingredient's buffered cost isn't a multiple of its own true cost once its components have buffers.
- **Multi-unit materials** (built 2026-09-13, from the user's Ingredients mockup):
  - **`RawMaterialUnit`** (`RawMaterialId` Cascade, `UnitOfMeasureId` Restrict, `BaseUnitsPerUnit` > 0, `Description`; unique per material + unit; never a packaging material's own base unit). It merged `IngredientUnit` (`GramsPerUnit`) and `PackagingMaterialUnit`. The base unit never has a row: **an empty unit means the base unit** everywhere a unit is optional (price entries, packaging lines), so nothing depends on a "gram" row existing in Units of Measure. Recipe lines take no unit at all (2026-09-14): an ingredient's alternative units price it and work in the Usage Example, but a recipe is always written in grams.
  - `UnitConversionRules.ToBaseQuantity(quantity, multiplier?)` / `CostPerBaseUnit(price, multiplier?)` / `EnsureValidMultiplier` are the only conversion formulas.
  - **At most two alternative units per item** (`UnitConversionRules.MaxAlternativeUnits`, a user requirement added right after the feature shipped): `RawMaterialUnitRepository.AddAsync` counts the material's units and rejects a third (`EnsureCanAddAlternativeUnit`; `RawMaterialRepository.SaveAsync` adds units through the same check, after removing the ones a save drops), and the Raw Materials screen disables Add Alternative Unit at two (`CanAddUnit`, set when the unit rows are rebuilt). Editing an existing unit is unaffected.
  - **Every price records the unit it was priced in** (`UnitId?`), both a brand price and a price history entry, so switching from buying bags to buying packs leaves the old per-bag prices correct. The stored cost is per base unit only: `CostPerBaseUnit` = the dearest brand price ÷ its unit's multiplier. `CurrentCost`, `PurchaseUnitId` and `PurchaseUnitQty` were dropped from both material tables before the merge: a price is meaningless without its unit, and storing the unit on the material would make a material ↔ unit FK cycle.
  - **Correcting a multiplier reprices.** `RawMaterialUnitRepository.UpdateAsync` (and the screen's Save, through the same shared write method) reprices the material from its brand prices when one is in that unit (`RawMaterialBrandPriceRepository.RepriceAsync`, which also logs a new cost in price history); for an ingredient whose cost moved that way it then calls `IngredientCostPropagator.RecomputeAndPropagateAsync`, which reaches every dependent made ingredient and sub-assembly. Recipe lines are in grams, so nothing else moves (until 2026-09-14 it also rewrote the `Grams` of every recipe line entered in the unit). A packaging material needs nothing more: packaging lines are costed on read. A unit a brand price or a price history entry is in can't be deleted.
  - **A unit in use can't be deleted**: the repository counts price entries, and for a packaging material's unit its packaging lines and its packaging sets' lines, first and says which (Restrict FKs back that up). An ingredient's unit is never on a line.
  - **A packaging material's base unit is fixed once it has alternative units, prices or packaging lines** (`RawMaterialRules.EnsureBaseUnitCanChange`, checked in `RawMaterialRepository.UpdateAsync`), since all of them are measured in it.
- **RawMaterialCategory** (Flour, Dairy, Boxes, Ribbons, …): one user-editable list, merged from `IngredientCategory` and `PackagingCategory` (a packaging category named like an ingredient one joined it), **each category typed Ingredient or Packaging** (`Type`, a `RawMaterialType` stored by name; 2026-09-18, at the user's request). A material goes only in a category of its own type (`RawMaterialRules.EnsureCategoryMatchesType`, in `RawMaterialRepository`'s add and update, after the type and source checks), and a category's type can't change while any material is in it (`EnsureCategoryTypeCanChange`, in `RawMaterialCategoryRepository.UpdateAsync`). Migration `RawMaterialCategoryType` added the column with a default of Ingredient; the dev database's packaging categories (Container, Label, Wrapper, and the empty Adhesive Tape) were set to Packaging by hand afterwards, since no category held both types. Managed in Settings → Material Categories, which has a Type column and a Type picker on its form — see Application Architecture below.
- **ProductCategory** (Breads, Cakes, Pastries, …; 2026-09-17, at the user's request: "Products doesn't have categories"): a user-editable list **of its own**, not shared with `RawMaterialCategory` (the user's choice, so a Category picker never mixes Flour and Boxes with Cakes). `Product.CategoryId` is **optional** (also the user's choice): existing products needed no backfill, and a product without one is listed as Uncategorized. Its FK is **Set Null**, not Restrict like a raw material's, so deleting a category just leaves its products uncategorized (the delete confirmation says so). Managed in Settings → Product Categories, or added from the Products header's Category picker. `ProductRepository` reads it with the product (`Include`) and writes it in add, update and `SaveAsync`; `ProductCloner` gives a clone its source's category.
- **Ingredients** (`Type = Ingredient`):
  - **The base unit for every ingredient is the gram.** Every weight total is in grams: sub-assembly lines' and BOM lines' `Grams`, sub-assembly total weights, and weight-per-piece. **Recipe lines are entered in grams only** (2026-09-14, at the user's request): sub-assembly lines and BOM lines have no unit picker. Until then a line could be entered in an ingredient's alternative unit ("3 pc of egg") and stored in both; migration `ProductOwnedSubAssemblies` dropped `Quantity`/`UnitId` and kept the grams.
  - **`Source`**: `Purchased`, `Made` or `Linked`. It is set when the ingredient is created and can't change afterwards, so an ingredient's cost always comes from exactly one place.
  - **`Units`**: its alternative units (a 25 kg bag is `BaseUnitsPerUnit = 25000`, an egg piece `50`). Either source can have them; only a purchased one is priced in them.
  - **Purchased** ingredients are priced through their brand prices (`RawMaterialBrandPrice`), each in one of the ingredient's units (or per gram). `CostPerBaseUnit` = the dearest brand's price ÷ grams in its unit.
  - **Made** ingredients are produced in-house from a bill of materials (BOM):
    - They have no brand prices or price history.
    - **`WeightLossPercent`**: weight lost while making the ingredient (e.g. water boiled off syrup). At least 0 and less than 100, enforced by `RawMaterialRules.EnsureFieldsMatchSource`. **The screen defaults a new made ingredient to 3%** (2026-09-21, at the user's request: "the Weight Loss % TextBox should be preserved with a default value of 3% and should be customizable"; it was 0% before), seeded by the `EditingSource` subscription with `??=` so a saved material keeps what it was saved with, and the box caps at 0–99.99 to match the rule. A cleared box still saves 0 — a legitimate custom value.
    - Output grams = `Σ BOM line grams × (1 − WeightLossPercent / 100)`.
    - Cost per gram = `Σ (BOM line grams × component cost per gram) / output grams`. The buffered cost per gram is the same formula over the components' buffered costs, with nothing of its own on top (see `CostBufferPercent` above, 2026-09-21). The formula itself is `IngredientBomRules.CalculateCost`; a component's cost used here is whatever's currently stored on it — see the propagation note below for why that's always safe to read directly rather than walking the tree every time.
    - Example: simple syrup made from 1000g sugar (₱0.09/g, so ₱90) and 500g water (₱0, i.e. never priced: a literal ₱0 brand price is rejected, so "free" means unpriced, not a zero price), with 20% weight loss, gives 1500 × 0.8 = 1200g. It costs ₱90 / 1200 = ₱0.075/g.
  - **Linked** ingredients (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "Raw Material cost link to another raw material") are parts of another ingredient, costed as a share of its cost. An egg is bought by the 1,500 g tray at ₱135 (₱0.09/g); egg white is 70% of its weight and carries 30% of its cost, egg yolk 30% of the weight and 70% of the cost. A made ingredient couldn't do this: a BOM spreads cost by weight only.
    - **`LinkedToId`** (self-referencing FK, Restrict: an egg can't be deleted while a part links to it; the driver's `SqlException` shows as "in use elsewhere"), **`WeightSharePercent`** and **`CostSharePercent`** (each above 0, at most 100). Only a linked ingredient has them, and it has no price history, no BOM and no weight loss (`RawMaterialRules.EnsureFieldsMatchSource`, the validator, `CK_RawMaterials_LinkMatchesSource`). The link and shares can be edited after creation, like a weight loss; the source can't.
    - **Cost per gram = linked-to cost per gram × cost share ÷ weight share** (`RawMaterialRules.LinkedCost`, the only place the formula is). White: 0.09 × 30/70 = ₱0.038571; yolk: 0.09 × 70/30 = ₱0.21. 1,050 g of white and 450 g of yolk cost ₱40.50 + ₱94.50 = ₱135, the whole tray. It doesn't depend on the unit the egg was priced in. Stored costs keep 4 decimals (`decimal(18,4)`), so white is stored as ₱0.0386 — the same limit every stored cost per gram has.
    - **The cost share is of the linked-to ingredient's unbuffered cost** (the user's rule, 2026-09-16: "the Cost Share must be the percentage of the unbuffered cost not the buffered one"), **and the part inherits the linked-to ingredient's `CostBufferPercent`** (2026-09-17: "i want the linked ingredient to inherit the buffer cost %"). Egg at ₱0.09/g with a 10% buffer gives yolk (30% / 70%) ₱0.21/g, buffered ₱0.231/g. **Since 2026-09-19 the buffered cost is the same share of the linked-to ingredient's buffered cost** (the egg's being a fixed amount now), so the inherited percent is only shown; the numbers match while the egg's buffered cost is its cost with its % on top. The percent, not the buffered cost, is what's inherited: the share is still taken before any buffer, so it never counts twice, and a made linked-to ingredient's stacked buffered rollup doesn't leak in.
      - **Stored on the part, kept in step.** `RawMaterialRepository` sets a linked ingredient's `CostBufferPercent` from the linked-to ingredient's on add and update, whatever the form sent (`EnsureValidLinkAsync` returns it), and `IngredientCostPropagator` copies it again every time the part is recomputed. A buffer edit on an ingredient already cascades, so it reaches every part, down a chain of links; relinking takes the new ingredient's. Storing it keeps everything that reads `CostBufferPercent` (the Usage Example, the list) right without knowing about links.
      - The Linked Cost card shows the inherited percent read-only (bound to `EditingLinkedTo.CostBufferPercent`); it's changed on the linked-to ingredient.
      - History: it first shipped stacking the buffers the way a made ingredient's did until 2026-09-21 (the share of the egg's *buffered* cost, then the part's own buffer); on 2026-09-16 the share moved to the unbuffered cost with only the part's own buffer on top; on 2026-09-17 the part's own buffer gave way to the inherited one.
    - **The parts of one ingredient take at most 100% of its weight and at most 100% of its cost** (the user's choice; `RawMaterialRules.EnsureLinkSharesFit`, checked by `RawMaterialRepository` on add and update against the *other* parts, so a part never counts against itself). Less is fine — the shell is neither white nor yolk. The message names the other parts: "Egg Yolk (30%) already takes 30% of Egg's weight, so Egg White can take at most 70%."
    - **Ingredients only** (the user's choice): the target must be another ingredient (`EnsureValidLinkTarget`, which also refuses a link to itself), purchased, made or itself linked. Packaging stays purchased-only; `CK_RawMaterials_OnlyIngredientsAreMade` already covers it.
    - **A link is one more edge in the cost graph** (see Reactive Costing Cascade), so a cycle can't close through a mix of BOMs and links: linking egg white to a meringue base made from egg white is refused, and so is a BOM that uses a part linked to the ingredient being made. `IngredientDependencies.LoadAsync` builds the combined graph for both checks (`IngredientBomRules.EnsureNoCycle`).
    - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor, against a throwaway migrated database (dropped afterwards), with real clicks and keystrokes: a seeded Egg Yolk loaded into the Linked Cost card; Egg White was created as Linked to Egg, 75% / 30% was refused with "Egg Yolk (30%) already takes 30% of Egg's weight, so Egg White can take at most 70%.", and 70% / 30% saved at ₱0.0386 with the other-parts caption showing 100% of the weight and cost.
- **IngredientBomLine**: one component of a made ingredient. It has `IngredientId` (the made ingredient), `ComponentIngredientId` (a raw material of the Ingredient type, purchased or made), and `Grams`, the amount. `IngredientBomRules.EnsureValidLines` rejects an empty set, non-positive grams, or the same component twice (combine it into one line instead).
  - BOMs can nest to any depth: caramel can be made from syrup, and syrup from sugar. `IngredientBomRules.EnsureNoCycle` blocks an ingredient from appearing anywhere in its own BOM tree, checked at save time by walking the *other* made ingredients' current BOM graph.
  - `IngredientId`'s FK is Cascade (deleting the made ingredient takes its own BOM lines with it); `ComponentIngredientId`'s FK is Restrict (deleting an ingredient that's in use as a component is blocked — remove it from the BOM first). Both can't be Cascade: SQL Server rejects two cascade paths from the same table to the same parent table.
  - BOM lines use ingredients only, never packaging (`RawMaterialRules.EnsureUsableInRecipes`, checked by `IngredientBomLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`).
- **Packaging materials** (`Type = Packaging`):
  - `BaseUnitId` (FK to UnitOfMeasure; `StandardUnitId` until 2026-09-13) is its base unit, set per material: typically a count unit (piece) for boxes and wrappers, a length unit (cm) for ribbon. No rule restricts which unit that can be — the shop owner is trusted to pick a sensible one — but it's fixed once anything is measured in it (see Multi-unit materials).
  - `Units` are its alternative units (a pack of 100 boxes is `BaseUnitsPerUnit = 100`, a 1000 cm ribbon roll `1000`), and `CostPerBaseUnit` its stored cost.
  - Always purchased, and priced through brand prices like a purchased ingredient.
  - Packaging lines can be entered in the base unit or any alternative unit (see PackagingLine).
- **RawMaterialBrandPrice** (2026-09-18, at the user's request: "convert the textboxes to a datagrid" with Brand, Purchase Unit, Quantity per Purchase Unit and Cost Buffer columns): what each brand of a purchased material costs now. `Brand` is optional free text (trimmed, max 100, `null` when blank); `UnitId` is one of the material's units or `null` for per base unit; `Price` is per unit and positive (`CK_RawMaterialBrandPrices_Price`). **The material is costed at the dearest brand per base unit** (`RawMaterialRules.ApplyBrandPrices`/`FindDearestBrandPrice`; the user's call: "always use the max of the purchase price of different brands"), so costing stays conservative. A brand is priced once per unit, whatever its case (`EnsureDistinctBrandPrices`); the same brand in two units is fine. There is no default-brand flag: the user dropped it once the max rule decided the cost. `RawMaterialBrandPriceRepository.SetAsync` (and the screen's Save) replaces them all, reprices and cascades.
- **RawMaterialPriceHistory** (merged from `IngredientPriceHistory` and `PackagingPriceHistory`): **a log of the prices a purchased material has been costed at** since 2026-09-18, at the user's request. Before, it was the price timeline the cost came from (latest by date, then id, via `PriceHistoryRules`, now removed); entries could be added, edited and deleted, each repricing the material. Now `RawMaterialBrandPriceRepository.RepriceAsync` adds an entry, dated today, with the dearest brand's price, unit and `Brand`, whenever brand prices move the cost; a Save that leaves the cost as it was logs nothing, and one that leaves no prices logs nothing either. **Deleting an entry only tidies the log** and never reprices. Made ingredients have none. The migration `RawMaterialBrandPrices` added the table and `Brand`; each purchased material's latest entry was carried over as one unnamed brand by a one-off SQL script run against the local database (a migration is never hand-edited, so the data step couldn't live in it).
- **SubAssemblyRole**: an editable lookup table (`Id`, `Name`, `SortOrder`, `IsPackaging`), seeded with Dough, Filling, Topping, Inclusion and Finishing. More roles, such as Glaze, can be added in Settings → Roles. `SortOrder` sets the order of a recipe's sections. A role can't be deleted while any sub-assembly uses it.
  - **The Packaging role** (2026-09-18, at the user's request: "Sub-Assemblies can contain a subassembly for packaging"): seeded at **Id 6** with `IsPackaging = true`, the only role with the flag. A standalone sub-assembly in it is a **packaging set** (see Packaging sets below). The code checks the flag, never the name, so the role can be renamed; `SubAssemblyRoleRepository.DeleteAsync` refuses to delete it, and nothing in Settings can clear the flag (`UpdateAsync` writes only `Name` and `SortOrder`).
    - Id 6 was freed by hand first. The dev database's roles had drifted from the seeds (Inclusion, Id 4, deleted; Finishing, Id 5, renamed Pan Greasing; Sponge added at 6; an unused "Packaging" role at 7), so a `HasData` row would have clashed on the key or the unique name. The user deleted the old Packaging row, and a one-off script moved Pan Greasing to 4 and Sponge to 5 (neither was used by a sub-assembly) and reseeded the identity to 5; the migration `PackagingSubAssemblies` then inserted Id 6. A fresh database gets 1–5 as seeded plus 6.
  - **Product-owned roles: `IsProductOwned`** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "allow products to own Filling, Pan Greasing, and Topping subassembly … add an option in the Roles setting which can set a role if it can be product owned"). A checkbox on Settings → Roles (and a read-only Product-owned column in its grid); never on the Packaging role (`SubAssemblyRoleValidator`, `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureProductOwnedAllowed`, in `AddAsync`/`UpdateAsync`, and the checkbox is disabled for it). See "Product-owned sub-assemblies" under SubAssembly. Not seeded: a seeded flag would skip the conversion below, and seed Id 4 is Pan Greasing in the dev database, not Inclusion. The dev database's Filling, Topping and Pan Greasing were ticked in the app.
- **SubAssembly** + **SubAssemblyLine**: a sub-assembly is an in-house recipe that products use directly, such as a dough, a filling or a topping. Its lines are the ingredients that make it up.
  - **Every sub-assembly is kept on the Sub-Assemblies screen, and every product links the ones it uses** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "after the subassembly is added to the product and save, that subassembly which is unique to the product itself is stored automatically to the Subassembly with product name as the name … it will automatically use by the product … as link subassembly"). **Nothing was owned by a product from then until later that day** (see Product-owned sub-assemblies, next): `OwnerProductId` and `OwnerProduct` are gone (migration `SubAssembliesAllStandalone`), `Name` is required, and the unique index is on `Name` alone. The ownership bullets below record how it worked from 2026-09-14 to 2026-09-23.
    - **A new section becomes a sub-assembly on Save.** A section typed in the Products screen's Recipe Structure (New, Copy an existing one, or Make a Copy of a linked one) goes to `SetRecipeAsync` as a `NewSubAssemblyDraft(RoleId, Lines, AuthoredField, AuthoredValue, SectionName)` (it replaced `OwnedSubAssemblyDraft`, which carried an id; `RecipeDraft` is `(New, Linked)`). Save inserts a sub-assembly with its lines and links it: the piece size and shrinkage go on the usage, as for any link, and it has no add-ins. From then on the product shows it like any link: read-only lines, the badge "Cheese Roll Dough, from Sub-Assemblies" that opens it there, Make a Copy. **Its lines are edited only on the Sub-Assemblies screen** (the user's choice, over the product that made it still editing them).
    - **Its name is the product's and the role's** (`SubAssemblyRules.StandaloneNameFor`): "Cheese Roll Dough", and with the section's own name after them when it has one that isn't just the role's, "Cheese Roll Filling: Cheese" (the user's choice, over the product's name alone, which would have needed names unique per role). The product's name is read inside the save's transaction, so a new or renamed product saves under its new name. **A name already taken gets the next number**, "Cheese Roll Dough 2" (`UniqueStandaloneName`, case- and space-insensitively, against every sub-assembly and the ones the same save just made), cut short to fit 100 characters with its number. Renaming the product later leaves its sub-assemblies' names alone.
    - **Removing a section, or deleting the product, keeps the sub-assembly** (the user's choice, over deleting it when nothing else links it): `SetRecipeAsync` only replaces the usages, and a product's usages cascade while the usage's sub-assembly FK is Restrict. So nothing refuses a product's Delete or a section's removal because another product links it any more. What's left unused stays on the Sub-Assemblies screen until it's deleted there, which is still refused while a product uses it.
    - **How a linked section goes by its name in a recipe** (`SubAssemblyRules.SectionLabel(name, productName, roleName)`, the user's choice of "section name only"): less the "Cheese Roll Filling: " it was saved under from that product, so the tab reads "Filling: Cheese"; and nothing at all when it's exactly "Cheese Roll Filling", saved from there without a name, so it goes by its role alone, as it did before it was saved, and still counts as the one without a name in its role. Another product's goes by its whole name ("Filling: Ube Bun Filling"). `SetRecipeAsync`, `EnsureDistinctWhereLinkedAsync` (a rename or new role on the Sub-Assemblies screen) and the Products form all read it the same way.
    - **Existing data**: the 11 sub-assemblies products owned in the dev database were converted by a one-off SQL script before the migration (a migration is never hand-edited, so the data step couldn't live in it), after a `COPY_ONLY` backup (`C:\Users\infotech\TinapayanRMS_before_AllStandalone_20260923.bak`). Each was named as above, numbered where taken, and released (`OwnerProductId` null); its usages were left exactly as they were, so its product and any other linking it link it now ("Milky Bread x 12 Topping: Milk Streusel" is used by both Milky Breads). The script ran with `QUOTED_IDENTIFIER ON`, which `sqlcmd` leaves off and the filtered index needs. Tried first on a restored copy: the usages were identical before and after, and the migration went up, down and up. `TinapayanRMS_Render` and `TinapayanRMS_Demo` predate owned sub-assemblies and were left alone.
  - **Product-owned sub-assemblies** (2026-09-23, later the same day, at the user's request). `SubAssembly.OwnerProductId` is back (nullable, Cascade from `Product`; migration `ProductOwnedRoles`, with `SubAssemblyRole.IsProductOwned`), set only in a product-owned role. The user's choices, over the alternatives offered:
    - **A section made in a product in a product-owned role is that product's own** (new, Copy an existing one, or Make a Copy): `NewSubAssemblyDraft` gained an `Id` init property (0 new, else the product's own saved one, rewritten in place; the ~50 call sites that make new ones didn't change). In any other role a new one is kept on the Sub-Assemblies screen and linked, as before; one of its own moved to such a role is released there (`OwnerProductId` null) and linked. Its lines are edited in the product (the Products form's third state, `ProductUsageEdit.IsOwned`, badge "This product's own"), it isn't on the Sub-Assemblies screen (`GetStandaloneAsync` filters it out, and `SaveStandaloneAsync`/`DeleteStandaloneAsync` refuse it), and taking it out of the recipe or deleting the product deletes it.
    - **Copy only**: another product can copy one (the Copy picker lists it as "Cheese Roll Filling: Cheese — Cheese Roll (Filling)", `GetLinkableAsync` now bringing each one's owner) but never link it (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureLinkable` in `SetRecipeAsync`; the Link picker leaves it out). So nothing but its product ever uses it, and it can be changed or removed freely. A product-owned role can still link one from the Sub-Assemblies screen.
    - **Named like any other** ("Cheese Roll Filling: Cheese", unique, `StandaloneNameFor`/`UniqueStandaloneName`), and kept so: renaming its product (`ProductRepository.UpdateAsync`, which `SaveAsync` runs even without a recipe) or its role (`SubAssemblyRoleRepository.UpdateAsync`) renames it (`SubAssemblyRules.OwnedNameAfterRename`: one not named after them, like an adopted "Pan de Coco", keeps its name). Renames go through `SubAssemblyRepository.RenameAsync` (a throwaway name first, then the new ones, so two can swap).
    - **Turning a role product-owned** (`UpdateAsync`, one transaction, after a confirmation saying how many move, `CountOwnershipChangesAsync`): each of its sub-assemblies on the Sub-Assemblies screen that exactly one product's recipe uses becomes that product's own, keeping its name (`AdoptSingleUseAsync`); that product's add-ins on it are merged into its lines, after its own. Used by several, or none, it stays. **Turning it off** releases every one products own in it to the Sub-Assemblies screen, still used (linked) by the same product (`ReleaseOwnedAsync`); merged add-ins stay merged, which the confirmation says. The Products screen picks either up on its next refresh: with nothing typed it reads the product again, so what Save compares against is how it's saved now.
    - **Clone and Create Variant give the new product its own copy of each owned section** (named after the new product; lines, piece size, shrinkage and what it went by), and link what the source links (`ProductCloner.DerivedRecipe`, which replaced `LinkedRecipe`).
    - **Existing data**: after a `COPY_ONLY` backup (`C:\Users\infotech\TinapayanRMS_before_ProductOwnedRoles_20260923.bak`) and the migration, Filling, Topping and Pan Greasing were ticked in Settings → Roles on the running app. 4 Fillings and 3 Toppings became their products' own; "Milky Bread x 12 Topping: Milk Streusel", used by both Milky Breads, stayed on the Sub-Assemblies screen; Pan Greasing had none. Every sub-assembly's totals and every usage were identical before and after, so no cost moved.
  - **Superseded (2026-09-23):** **Owned by the product it's made in** (`OwnerProductId`, Cascade from `Product`; 2026-09-14, at the user's request — until then sub-assemblies were a library of their own, on a Sub-Assemblies screen, and any product picking one shared it without anything saying so). A product's own sub-assembly is made and edited only inside its recipe on the Products screen; the owner always uses it. Another product can **link** it (a usage of it — live, with read-only lines and its own piece size) or **copy** it (an independent sub-assembly of its own). See Derived products below.
  - **Superseded (2026-09-23):** every sub-assembly is standalone now. **Or standalone: `OwnerProductId` null** (2026-09-15, at the user's request: "a Subassembly not owned by any product but can use for linking or cloning"). It's made and edited on the restored **Sub-Assemblies screen** (see Application Architecture), and every product using it links it, since none owns it. Products link or copy it exactly as they do another product's. `ISubAssemblyRepository.SaveStandaloneAsync`/`DeleteStandaloneAsync` write it (one transaction: name, role, then the lines through `SubAssemblyLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`), and refuse a product's own; `SetRecipeAsync` refuses a standalone one listed as a product's own.
  - **Superseded (2026-09-23):** a new section's optional name now only tells repeats apart and goes into the name it's saved under; `SectionLabel`, `DisplayName` and `EnsureValidOwnedNames` (now `EnsureValidSectionName`, a length check) changed or went, as above. **A product's own sub-assembly goes by its role, and by an optional name when its role repeats** (2026-09-18, at the user's request: "Give repeats a name again: the tab reads 'Filling: Ube' and 'Filling: Cheese'"). From 2026-09-17 to 2026-09-18 a product's own had no name and a product had one sub-assembly in each role (`EnsureOnePerRole`, the user's choice then over numbering repeats "Filling 2"). Now **a product can have several sub-assemblies in one role, its own and linked together, as long as they can be told apart** (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureSectionsDistinct`, in `SetRecipeAsync` and on the Products form's Apply): within a role no two go by the same name (case- and space-insensitively), and at most one goes without, so an existing recipe stays valid and a second Filling only needs a name of its own. What a section goes by (`SubAssemblyRules.SectionLabel`): a product's own by its `Name`, which it needn't have; a linked one by its name, or else its owner product's ("Filling: Cream Loaf"). A product's own names are optional, trimmed (blank is null, `NormalizeName`), at most 100 characters, and unique within the product whatever their roles (`EnsureValidOwnedNames`, as the `(OwnerProductId, Name)` index filtered on `[Name] IS NOT NULL` has it; the schema already allowed this, so no migration). `SetRecipeAsync` saves the names (it used to clear them), letting renamed ones' names go first so a swap doesn't trip the index halfway. Where it isn't plain whose it is — messages, the link picker — one is called by its product and role, with its name when it has one: "Cream Loaf's Dough", "Cream Loaf's Filling: Ube" (`SubAssemblyRules.DisplayName`). A clone keeps each one's name; in a role the source has more than one in, a linked one without a name of its own takes its owner's, which is what told it apart there.
    - **A new role or name can't leave a linking product unable to tell its sections apart**: giving an owned one (in `SetRecipeAsync`) or a standalone one (`SaveStandaloneAsync`) another role or name is refused when a product linking it would then have two in a role by one name, naming that product (`SubAssemblyRepository.EnsureDistinctWhereLinkedAsync`, which replaced `EnsureRoleFreeWhereLinkedAsync`).
    - **Existing data**: migration `ProductSubAssembliesGoByRole` made `Name` nullable and filtered the unique `(OwnerProductId, Name)` index to `[Name] IS NOT NULL`; nothing was renamed or deleted (a data step would have meant hand-editing the migration). Nothing shows an old owned name, and `SetRecipeAsync` clears it the next time its product's recipe is saved. A product saved with two sub-assemblies in one role still loads (two tabs of that role, Almond Cheese Bread's two Doughs among them), and can still save its name, packaging and costing; changing its recipe, or cloning it, is refused until one of them is given another role or taken out. Its unchanged tabs can still be looked through: `ApplyUsageForm` only checks the role of a section that changed.
    - **Superseded (2026-09-23):** every name is required and unique, on a plain unique index on `Name`. **Standalone names are unique among themselves** (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureStandaloneNameAvailable`, case- and space-insensitively). The same index enforces it: its filter is `[Name] IS NOT NULL` (`HasFilter(null)` until 2026-09-17), which keeps the ownerless rows in, where SQL Server's unique index treats their NULL owners as equal, and leaves the nameless owned ones out. EF's SQL Server convention would otherwise add `[OwnerProductId] IS NOT NULL` too, and drop the standalone rows from the index.
  - `RoleId` (FK to `SubAssemblyRole`, required): each sub-assembly has exactly one role, so custard is always a Filling. The owner can change it at any time; every recipe using the sub-assembly then shows it in the new role's place. (Until 2026-09-14 a role could only change while no product used the sub-assembly, since that broke role-tab links; those links are gone.)
  - Each line has an FK to an ingredient (a `RawMaterial` of the Ingredient type — rejected otherwise) and `Grams`. Line cost = grams × the ingredient's `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit`, its buffered cost per gram. `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureValidLines` rejects no lines, non-positive grams or an ingredient twice, so a sub-assembly always has a batch.
  - **Baker's percentage: `SubAssemblyLine.IsFormulaBase`** (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "a checkbox column to mark the ingredient as a reference, then find the total of all ingredients with check and use that as the divisor"). Each line is read as a percentage of the ticked lines' total weight — the formula's 100% base, usually the flour. `SubAssemblyRules.FormulaBaseWeight` sums the ticked lines and `CalculateBakersPercent(grams, baseWeight)` gives the figure, **null when nothing is ticked**: a glaze or a filling needn't have a base, and a blank is the honest answer, not a nought. The sum of every line's percentage — "whole formula 201.5%" in the caption under the table — is the number a batch is scaled by (`flour = target ÷ (total% ÷ 100)`).
    - **Per line, not per ingredient** (the user's design, over an `IsFlour` flag on the raw material). Sugar is the base of a syrup and an extra in a dough; two flours tick together and make 100% between them; and it never depends on the `Flour` category, which is user-editable free text and was already drifting (Cornstarch was filed under Butter). Named `IsFormulaBase` because "base" already means a *unit of measure* throughout this codebase (`BaseUnitId`, `CostPerBaseUnit`); the column header is "Base".
    - **Nothing about cost changes.** Grams stay the source of truth; the tick is a reading of the batch, never stored as a figure, and `TotalWeight`/`TotalCost` don't move.
    - **Lines travel as `RecipeLine`** (`ApplicationCore/Interfaces/RecipeLine.cs`), a `readonly record struct (IngredientId, Grams, IsFormulaBase = false)` that replaced the `(int IngredientId, decimal Grams)` tuple in `OwnedSubAssemblyDraft.Lines`, `ISubAssemblyRepository.SaveStandaloneAsync`, `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureValidLines` and `SubAssemblyLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`. It has an **implicit conversion from the old tuple**, so the ~120 call sites writing `[(flourId, 1000m)]` compiled untouched (only three test helpers that declared the tuple type themselves changed). The same conversion makes a *lossy* projection compile silently — `.Select(l => (l.IngredientId, l.Grams))` drops the tick without an error — so the three places that rebuild lines from existing data name it explicitly: `ProductsViewModel.CurrentRecipe()` (miss it and ticks never save, and `IsSameRecipe` never even sees the change), `SubAssembliesViewModel`'s save, and `ProductCloner`'s own-copy branch. Anything that carries sub-assembly lines from one place to another in future has to do the same.
    - **Add-ins have no tick** and stay on the plain tuple (`LinkedUsageDraft.AddIns`, `UsageAddInLineRepository`): an add-in is never part of the base. Each still gets a percentage, **read against the linked recipe's base**, not its own weight — 100 g of buttermilk on a dough's 1,000 g of flour reads 10.0, and the whole-formula figure includes it. BOM lines are untouched: a BOM is a yield calculation with weight loss, not a baker's formula, and uses its own `(ComponentIngredientId, Grams)` tuple.
    - **Typed either way, and whichever was typed stays put** (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "make typing percentages to get grams and vice versa work two ways", and their choice of what happens after). A line's grams or its baker's percentage can be typed; the other is worked out. **`SubAssemblyLine.AuthoredBakersPercent`** (nullable) records a typed percentage, null when the grams were typed — the same idea as a usage's `AuthoredField`/`AuthoredValue`, and chosen over "percentages always win" and "grams always win". When the base weight changes, a line typed as a percentage has its grams worked out again from it (`SubAssemblyRules.GramsFromBakersPercent`), and a line typed in grams keeps them while its percentage moves: on 1,000 g of flour, water typed as 65% and eggs typed as 100 g; double the flour and the water is 1,300 g, still 65%, while the eggs stay 100 g at 5%.
      - **Grams are still stored for every line** and still all that's costed. A typed percentage is worked back into grams the moment the base changes, in the form, and saved that way; the base only ever changes by editing that same formula, so the stored grams are never stale. `GramsFromBakersPercent` rounds to the 4 decimals grams are stored in, so reopening a saved line reproduces exactly what was worked out; unrounded, a line could come back a ten-thousandth off and make a recipe nobody touched look changed.
      - **Any change to the base weight counts**: a base line's grams, a tick, or a base line added or removed. A typed percentage always stays that percentage, which is the simple rule to predict. Ticking a line into the base makes it a grams line (the base is 100% between its lines by definition), and `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureValidLines` refuses a base line carrying a percentage. With nothing ticked, a typed percentage keeps its last grams and its percentage waits: tick the flour again and the line follows it.
      - **Where it can't be typed, it won't open**: on a base line, and while nothing is ticked (`IngredientLineRow.CanTypeBakersPercent`). SfDataGrid's `AllowEditing` is one setting per column, so `controls:GridCellEditing` (a grid-level `IsEnabled`, and `AllowWhen` naming a `bool` property of the row on the column, the way `MappingName` names the value) cancels `CurrentCellBeginEdit` on the rows that say no. The row's setter ignores such a value too, as a backstop.
      - **A typed percentage is in bold** (`controls:GridCellEmphasis`, `When="IsPercentTyped"`), the way the Products screen's typed piece-size figure once was. Only the percentage is marked: grams are the usual way in, and bolding them on nearly every row would drown out the ones that follow the flour. It's a renderer (`EmphasisNumericRenderer`, replacing the grid's `"Numeric"` one) binding the text's `FontWeight`, not a `CellStyle`: a cell style replaces the theme's own `GridCell` style, and basing one on the theme's (`SyncfusionGridCellStyle`, as Syncfusion's ConditionalFormattingDemo does) needs its whole dictionary merged in, beside the accent colours the app sets at startup. `OnUpdateDisplayBinding` calls `OnInitializeDisplayElement`, so the override also covers recycled rows.
      - **Carried everywhere lines are**: `RecipeLine.AuthoredBakersPercent`, `SubAssemblyLineEdit`, `ProductUsageEdit.ShowsSameAs`, the three line-copying projections named above (`CurrentRecipe()`, Sub-Assemblies' save, `ProductCloner`), `Repriced`, and both add rows' item factories, which hand a new line the current base so a percentage can be typed into it straight away.
      - **Add-ins can be typed either way too, but only grams are kept.** An add-in's base is the linked recipe's, which belongs to another product and can change there without this form open; remembering a typed percentage would mean working its grams out on every read, in Products and Reports alike, from a base those screens don't load. So a percentage typed for an add-in fills in its grams and isn't kept, and isn't shown in bold.
    - **With nothing ticked, the column shows absolute percentage** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "if an assembly does not have a base that is checked, use the absolute percentage … the total grams/weight of assembly as the divisor. The header will become Absolute %"). This replaces the blank a base-less formula showed before. `SubAssemblyRules.CalculateAbsolutePercent(grams, batchWeight)` is a line's share of the whole batch, so the lines come to 100 between them. `IngredientLineRow.Percent` (renamed from `BakersPercent`, since it's now either figure) reads baker's percentage while `BaseWeight > 0` and absolute percentage against `BatchWeight` otherwise. `ApplyFormulaBase` hands every row both weights, and the batch counts the rows with an ingredient picked (`IngredientLineRow.BatchWeightOf`, the same count as the screens' batch weight).
      - **The header follows** (`SubAssembliesViewModel.PercentHeader`, `ProductsViewModel.UsageFormPercentHeader`, both from `IngredientLineRow.PercentHeader`), through a **`HeaderTemplate`** (`PercentHeaderTemplate` in each view) whose `TextBlock` binds the view's `DataContext` by `RelativeSource AncestorType=UserControl`.
        - **Not a bound `HeaderText`**, which was the first version. It looked right on the Sub-Assemblies screen, but the user found Products' Recipe Structure tabs showing the previous tab's header ("Baker's %" over Topping's absolute 100.0, which has no base). Every tab has its own `UsageFormPanel`, so its grid, and a changed `GridColumnBase.HeaderText` only reaches the header cell through the grid's row generator (`OnUpdateBindingInfo`, decompiled 34.2.7), which didn't redraw the header of the grid a newly shown tab brings in. A visual-tree binding in the header cell is always current. Verified in the app on Almond Cheese Bread by clicking Dough → Topping → Filling → Dough → Topping → Dough → Packaging → Topping (the header right each time), by ticking and unticking Topping's only line as its base (the header switched both ways), and on the Sub-Assemblies screen. The caption under the table says "Absolute %: each line's share of the batch. Tick the base — usually the flour — for baker's %."
      - **Read-only** (the user's choice, over typing one to work out the grams, or keeping a typed absolute percentage). `CanTypeBakersPercent` was already false without a base. The row's setter now checks that before a cleared value, so a line whose typed baker's percentage is waiting for a base to come back doesn't lose it, and `IsPercentTyped` (the bold) only holds while there's a base, since the absolute figure is never typed.
      - **On a linked recipe with add-ins, both tables divide by the whole batch** (the user's choice, over the recipe's weight alone): the recipe's lines and the add-ins together come to 100%. With a base, add-ins are still read against the recipe's base, as before.
    - Not modelled: a preferment (a sponge made as a made ingredient) ticked as the base counts its whole weight, not the flour inside it, so such a formula will disagree with a textbook — the user has a separate plan for preferments; and true hydration counting the water in milk and eggs.
  - A sub-assembly has a total weight in grams (the sum of its line grams) and a total ingredient cost, both stored and refreshed whenever its lines are saved or an ingredient's cost changes (the cascade).
  - **It can't be deleted from under a product that links it** (since 2026-09-23 that's only its Delete on the Sub-Assemblies screen: a section removed, or a product deleted, leaves it there). Until then, removing it from its owner's recipe, or deleting the owner, was refused while another product's usage points at it, and the message names those products (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureNotLinkedElsewhere`: "Cream Loaf Mini links Sweet Dough. Make a copy of it there, or remove it there, first."). Deleting a standalone one on the Sub-Assemblies screen is refused the same way while any product uses it.
- **Packaging sets: SubAssembly in the Packaging role + SubAssemblyPackagingLine** (2026-09-18, at the user's request; the columns "similar to the packaging in the Recipe" Packaging tab). A standalone sub-assembly whose role has `IsPackaging` holds packaging materials **per set** instead of ingredients in grams — a gift box with its ribbon and tag, or the 8 bags and the box that make up an "Ensaymada Box of 8".
  - **`SubAssemblyPackagingLine`** (`SubAssemblyId` Cascade, `PackagingMaterialId` Restrict to a `RawMaterial` of the Packaging type, `UnitId?` Restrict to a `RawMaterialUnit`, `QtyPerSet` > 0 in that unit; unique per set + material; `CK_SubAssemblyPackagingLines_QtyPerSet`). A table of its own, not `SubAssemblyLine`, which is ingredients in grams and feeds the cost cascade.
    - **`UnitId?`** (2026-09-20, at the user's request: "I have an idea to utilize the alternative units stored per packaging materials … I select that and it will be used"): the unit the quantity is entered in — one of the material's own alternative units, or its base unit when empty — exactly as `PackagingLine.UnitId` has always been. The quantity is stored as typed and converted at cost time, so 1 roll stays 1 roll rather than the 1,000 cm it comes to, and correcting the roll's multiplier reprices the line without rewriting it. `SubAssemblyRepository.SaveStandalonePackagingAsync` refuses a unit that isn't that material's own, with the same message `PackagingLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` uses, and `RawMaterialUnitRepository.DeleteAsync` counts set lines beside packaging lines before it lets a unit go. Migration `SetPackagingLineUnits` adds the column, nullable, so every existing line stays in its base unit with no data step.
  - **Costed on read**, like a product's packaging lines. A set's stored `TotalWeight`/`TotalCost` stay 0, so neither cost propagator knows about sets.
  - **Standalone only.** A recipe section never takes the Packaging role (a product's own sub-assembly, until 2026-09-23) (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureRecipeRole`, in `SetRecipeAsync` for owned and linked sections alike; the Products form's role picker leaves it out), and `GetLinkableAsync` leaves sets out of the recipe link/copy picker.
  - **The kind is fixed once saved** (the user's decisions were a role for the marking; fixing it follows the raw material type rule): `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureKindUnchanged` refuses moving a saved recipe to the Packaging role or a saved set to a recipe role. `SaveStandaloneAsync` refuses the Packaging role; `SaveStandalonePackagingAsync` (one transaction: name and role through the shared `StageStandaloneAsync`, then `SubAssemblyRules.EnsureValidPackagingLines` — at least one line, positive quantities, no material twice — and `RawMaterialRules.EnsureUsableAsPackaging` per material, then the lines replaced) requires it.
  - **Used by a product's packaging, one whole set per sellable unit** (the user's choices: link the Packaging tab rather than add a recipe tab, and one whole set at a time; it read "per piece" until Quantity Required arrived on 2026-09-20, which is the same thing wherever a unit is one piece), linked or copied from the Products screen's Add Sub-Assembly. See "Link the packaging" under Derived products. A packaging material's base unit is fixed once a set uses it too (`RawMaterialRepository.UpdateAsync`'s `hasLines`).
  - **Deleting a linked set is refused**, naming the products (`DeleteStandaloneAsync`, the same wording as `EnsureNotLinkedElsewhere`), and `GetStandaloneLinkedByAsync` counts packaging links, so the screen's Used By names them.
- **Product** + **ProductSubAssemblyUsage**: a finished product is built from distinct sub-assemblies, its own and linked ones. It can use several in one role, e.g. an ube filling and a cheese filling, told apart by their names (one in a role can go without; see SubAssembly above; from 2026-09-17 to 2026-09-18 it had one in each role), and each sub-assembly only once. Since 2026-09-23 every usage is a link (until then, a usage whose sub-assembly's `OwnerProductId` wasn't the usage's `ProductId` was one).
  - **`Description`** (optional, at most 500 characters: `ProductValidator.MaxDescriptionLength`, which `ProductConfiguration`'s `HasMaxLength` also uses) and **`UpdatedAt`** (`DateTime?`, local time), both added 2026-09-14 for the Products screen's header. `UpdatedAt` is stamped by `ProductRepository`'s add and update, so by the screen's Save and by a clone when it's made. Products not saved since then have null, shown as "—".
  - **`CurrentPrice`** (`decimal?`, per sellable unit, VAT included; added 2026-09-19 at the user's request, per piece until Quantity Required on 2026-09-20): what the shop actually charges now, entered in the Products header beside the computed Selling Price and saved with the product (null until entered; `ProductValidator` refuses a negative one). A clone copies it. It prices nothing: the header shows the **Markup %** it earns, `CostingRules.CalculateMarkupPercentFromPrice` — VAT taken out first, then the markup on the base cost, `(CurrentPrice ÷ (1 + VAT%) − BaseCost) ÷ BaseCost` — the same definition as the costing's Margin, so the computed Selling Price gives back exactly the Margin % (the user's choice, over a markup on the material cost alone).
  - **Each usage has its own piece size, as total weight, yield and weight per piece** (2026-09-14, at the user's request). The user types either the **weight per piece** (grams) or the **yield** (pieces per batch), and the other is worked out from the batch's total weight. A usage stores only what was typed: **`AuthoredField`** (`UsageAuthoredField.WeightPerPiece` or `Yield`) and **`AuthoredValue`** (≥ 0, `CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_AuthoredValue`). Until then it stored `WeightPerPiece` only; the migration carried every one over as a typed weight.
    - **Neither figure is required** (2026-09-20, at the user's request): a section goes into the recipe, and saves, before how much of it a piece takes is known, stored as `AuthoredValue` 0. `ProductRules.EnsureValidUsages` and the check constraint both take 0 and refuse only a negative (migration `OptionalUsagePieceSize`); `ResolvePieceSize` already had no other figure to work out from 0, so an unsized section has a batch but costs the product nothing per piece, and the form's caption says so. It was refused on Apply (`EnsurePieceSizeEntered`, now gone) and at the repository before that.
    - **Each usage has its own shrinkage too** (2026-09-22, at the user's request: "Add Shrinkage % on top of the Weight per Piece (g) in the Recipe Structure. Final Weight (g) = Total Weight (g) * (1 - Shrinkage %)"). **`ShrinkagePercent`** is what the batch loses being made — a dough baked off, a filling reduced — at least 0 and under 100 (`CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_ShrinkagePercent`, migration `UsageShrinkagePercent`, which carried every existing usage over at 0). It belongs to the *usage*, not the sub-assembly: two products can bake the same dough to different weights. **`ProductRules.CalculateFinalWeight(totalWeight, shrinkagePercent)`** is the formula, the same one `RawMaterial.WeightLossPercent` uses one layer down (`IngredientBomRules.CalculateCost`). **The final weight is what the pieces are cut from** (the user's choice, over showing it and feeding nothing): the weight per piece is the *finished* piece, the yield is the final weight over it, and the batch keeps its whole cost, so shrinking a batch leaves fewer, dearer pieces. 1,700 g at 10% → 1,530 g; a typed 60 g piece yields 25.5, not 28.33.
  - **`ProductRules.ResolvePieceSize(authoredField, authoredValue, finalWeight)`** is the one formula: weight typed → `Yield = FinalWeight / WeightPerPiece`; yield typed → `WeightPerPiece = FinalWeight / Yield`. It's worked out on every read, never stored, which is what makes **the typed figure stay put when the batch or the shrinkage changes**: a 60 g piece stays 60 g and the yield moves; a 24-piece yield stays 24 and each piece gets heavier or lighter. Being computed on read, an owner's line edit never has to update other products' rows. A batch that weighs nothing has no other figure (0). Every caller works the final weight out first — `ProductUsageEdit`'s constructor, `ProductsViewModel.RecomputeUsageForm` and `UpdateOtherPieceSizeFigure`, and `ReportsViewModel` — so the rule itself stays one line.
  - Usage cost per piece = `SubAssembly.TotalCost × WeightPerPiece / FinalWeight`, with the resolved weight per piece, which comes to the same thing as `TotalCost ÷ Yield`. For example, 100g/₱24 filling used at 10g/piece gives ₱2.40/piece, yield 10; reduce that filling 20% and the same 10 g finished piece carries ₱3.00, yield 8.
  - **Per-piece line breakdown** (2026-09-14): a piece takes `WeightPerPiece / TotalWeight` of the batch, so it takes that share of every line. `ProductRules.CalculateLineGramsPerPiece` scales a line's grams and `CalculateLineCostPerPiece` its cost (grams × buffered cost per gram), with the same share. A usage's lines add up to its cost per piece and its weight per piece. The Recipe Structure showed this per line until 2026-09-16, when a sub-assembly's tab became its form. **Nothing calls these two rules now** — the Costing tab's Cost Breakdown works off `ProductCostSummary`, a piece at a time, not line by line. They're kept, with their tests, because they're the costing rule for a line's share of a piece and a per-line view is the obvious thing to want back. **If one returns, it must divide by the usage's *effective* batch after shrinkage** (`ProductRules.CalculateEffectiveBatch`, then `CalculateFinalWeight`), not the sub-assembly's stored totals, or a linked usage's add-ins would be left out of every line's share and a shrinking batch would under-price every line; their third parameter is named `finalWeight` for that reason.
  - **UsageAddInLine: a product's own extras on top of a sub-assembly it links** (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "the first datagrid has headers containing the linked recipe and the bottom one contains some additional recipe like food colors, flavoring etc"). The same dough goes into several products, but one wants ube flavouring and another pandan colour; until then the only way was **Make a Copy**, which breaks the link, so the dough stopped being shared and the owner's changes stopped reaching it.
    - `UsageAddInLine` (`UsageId` Cascade, `IngredientId` Restrict, `Grams`; unique per usage + ingredient) hangs off the **usage**, not the sub-assembly: two products linking the same dough each keep their own set, and neither moves `SubAssembly.TotalWeight`/`TotalCost`, which stay the owner's. The only cascade path in is `Products → ProductSubAssemblyUsages → UsageAddInLines`, a chain rather than a second path, which SQL Server allows because a usage's own sub-assembly FK is Restrict.
    - **Add-ins go into the batch** (the user's choice, over "a fixed amount per piece"): the usage's **effective batch** is the sub-assembly's plus its add-ins (`ProductRules.CalculateEffectiveBatch`, over `SubAssemblyRules.CalculateTotals`), so 1,700 g of dough with 20 g of flavouring is a 1,720 g batch and a piece takes its weight-per-piece share of the whole thing. `ResolvePieceSize` and `CalculateUsageCostPerPiece` were **unchanged**: they just receive the effective figures. The typed figure still stays put, so adding a flavouring moves the other one.
    - **Linked usages only.** A new section (a sub-assembly of the product's own, until 2026-09-23) has no add-ins — its extras just go in its lines, and a second table there would be two ways to do one thing.
    - **An ingredient the linked recipe already has is refused** (`ProductRules.EnsureValidAddIns`, the user's choice over merging the grams): "Water is already in Variety Bread Dough. Add-ins are only for what its recipe doesn't have — change how much it uses where Variety Bread Dough is kept, or make this product a copy of it." Checked in the form the moment Apply is pressed, and again in `SetRecipeAsync`'s pre-flight and `UsageAddInLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` as it writes. An empty set is valid, unlike a sub-assembly's own lines: most linked usages take the recipe as it is.
      - That guard is load-bearing beyond validation: since an add-in can never duplicate a base ingredient, **Make a Copy (and `ProductCloner`'s owned branch, until Clone started linking on 2026-09-23) concatenate the base lines and the add-ins** into the new sub-assembly's lines without a merge step, and the result always passes `EnsureValidLines`. The batch it was showing is the batch it keeps. A clone of a *standalone* link stays linked, so its add-ins stay add-ins.
    - **Written with the rest of the recipe**: `LinkedUsageDraft.AddIns` (an `init` property, so the existing positional call sites are untouched). `SetRecipeAsync` adds the linked usages together, saves once so every one has the id its add-ins need, then writes each one's lines — the same "save for the id, then reline" shape the owned loop already uses. `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.GetAddInsForProductAsync` reads them back, on the interface Products and Reports already inject, so no ViewModel constructor changed.
    - **`ReportsViewModel.CalculateMarginRowsAsync` had to learn about them**: it is the only place that costs a product without going through `ProductUsageEdit`, reading a sub-assembly's stored totals as the usage's batch, so without `CalculateEffectiveBatch` the margin chart silently understated every product with add-ins.
  - A product has **no yield of its own**. Each usage row has its own yield, and those will usually differ between a product's sub-assemblies. That's expected, not an error.
  - Product material cost per piece = the sum of all its usages' cost-per-piece, plus its packaging lines' cost-per-piece.
  - Sub-assembly and product weights are raw input weights, and baking/moisture loss is not modeled for them. Made ingredients are the only place weight loss is modeled (`WeightLossPercent`).
- **PackagingLine**: one packaging material used by one whole sellable unit of a product — its wrapper, its box, its label, its ribbon. "Unit" throughout this bullet means that sellable unit, which is one finished piece unless the product's `QuantityRequired` says otherwise.
  - FK to a `RawMaterial` of the Packaging type (`PackagingLineRepository` rejects an ingredient), so packaging price changes cascade like ingredient price changes.
  - **`UnitId?`** (`PackagingMaterialUnitId` before the raw materials merge): the unit the line's quantities are entered in — one of the material's own units, or its base unit when empty. Quantities are stored as entered, in that unit. (A length-only `EntryUnitId` existed until the length units were removed on 2026-09-13; this general unit replaced it the same day.)
  - **`PackId?` is gone** (2026-09-20, with `ProductPack`; see Quantity Required below). It pointed at the `ProductPack` a line packed, null meaning a per-piece line. Its FK had to be `Restrict` rather than `Cascade`, since `PackagingLine` already cascades from `Product` and `ProductPack` did too, and a second cascade path `Products -> PackagingLines` is something SQL Server refuses — so `ProductPackRepository.SetPacksAsync` and `ProductRepository.DeleteAsync` deleted a pack's lines by hand, lines before packs. All of that went with it: `ProductRepository.DeleteAsync` now just deletes the product and lets its cascades do the rest.
  - **`QtyPerUnit`** (`QtyPerPiece` until 2026-09-18): amount of the packaging material used by one sellable unit, in the line's unit (12.7 for 12.7 cm of ribbon, `0.1667` for a 6-piece box, 1 for a label). **Typed, and never rescaled**: changing a product's `QuantityRequired` from 8 to 12 doesn't touch it, so the packaging is revisited by hand. The Packaging tab's caption says which unit the figures are for (`ProductsViewModel.CostUnitCaption`), so that stays visible rather than silent.
  - **`UnitsPerPackagingUnit`** (`PiecesPerPackagingUnit` until 2026-09-18): sellable units served by one of the line's unit of the packaging material (6 for that box). **Both were renamed when `PackId` arrived** (2026-09-18), since "piece" stopped being true for a pack's line; the names outlived packs, because "unit" is now the sellable unit. The columns were renamed by a generated migration (`PackagingLinesServeUnits`) with no data loss.
  - **`AuthoredField`**: records which of the two the user typed. The other is recalculated as its reciprocal on every edit. Ribbon is naturally entered as `QtyPerUnit`, a box as `UnitsPerPackagingUnit`. Its members are stored as ints, so they may be renamed but **never reordered**.
  - The unique index is **`{ProductId, PackagingMaterialId}`**: one line per material per product, a material a unit takes twice over belonging on a single line at the combined quantity. It was `{ProductId, PackId, PackagingMaterialId}` with `HasFilter(null)` while packs existed — the filter being required, since EF's SQL Server convention would otherwise have added `[PackId] IS NOT NULL` and dropped every per-piece line out of the index (the same trap `SubAssemblyConfiguration` documents). `ProductQuantityRequiredReplacesPacks` put the two-column index back.
  - Cost per unit = `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit × QtyPerUnit` converted to base units (`ProductRules.CalculatePackagingLineCost(costPerBaseUnit, qtyPerUnit, baseUnitsPerEntryUnit?)`, passed the buffered cost), computed on read. It is a cost per *sellable unit*, so `CalculateMaterialCost` adds it once however many pieces that unit holds.
  - Example: a ribbon costs ₱45.00 per roll. Its base unit is cm and a roll holds 1000 cm, so it costs ₱0.045 per cm. One product uses 12.7 cm per piece (₱0.5715); another is entered as 0.08 m per piece (8 cm → ₱0.36).
  - Storing both directions differs from `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` on purpose. People describe packaging quantities both ways, but they describe recipe weights only one way.
- **Quantity Required** (`Product.QuantityRequired`, 2026-09-20, at the user's request: "first let's have the product a quantity required. then we can set the necessary packaging in the subassembly"): how many finished pieces make one sellable unit. 1 for a product sold by the piece, which is nearly all of them; 8 for "Ensaymada Box of 8". `int`, defaulting to 1 in the CLR and in the database, `CK_Products_QuantityRequired` keeping it positive, and `ProductValidator` refusing anything below 1 on the screen.
  - **The one rule it establishes: the recipe is authored per piece, costing and pricing are per sellable unit, and this is the bridge.** `ProductRules.CalculateMaterialCost(usageCostsPerPiece, quantityRequired, packagingCosts)` = `usageCostsPerPiece.Sum() × quantityRequired + packagingCosts.Sum()` — only the usages are multiplied, since a packaging line is already for one whole unit. It replaced `CalculateMaterialCostPerPiece` and `CalculatePackMaterialCost`, whose bodies it merges. At a quantity of 1 it is exactly the old per-piece figure, so nothing that existed changed value.
  - **`ProductConfiguration` sets `HasDefaultValue(1).HasSentinel(-1)`.** The database default is what let the migration add the column to existing rows without failing the check constraint.
