The sentinel is what moves "leave it to the database" off 0, which EF would otherwise take it to be: without it a product explicitly saved as 0 pieces would be quietly stored as 1 on an insert while being refused on an update.
  - **It replaced `ProductPack`** (2026-09-18 to 2026-09-20), which was one sellable pack of a product — "Pack of 8", "Pack of 12" — with its own `PiecesPerPack` and its own packaging lines, shown on a **Packs** tab. Create Variant (2026-09-19) made it redundant, and a pack had turned out to be a second-class SKU: it never reached Reports (`ReportsViewModel` costs per piece, and `PackagingLineRepository` filtered `PackId IS NULL`), it had no `CurrentPrice`, Markup %, category, description or `UpdatedAt`, it had no row in the Products list, it was never linkable so two products sharing a box format each kept their own copy, and it cost the schema the `PackId` complications above. **A pack is now a product**: a variant of the single, with a Quantity Required and a packaging set of its own.
  - **The worked example**, which `ProductQuantityRequiredPersistenceTests` pins: Ensaymada takes 10 g of a 1,000 g / ₱50 dough, so ₱0.50 of recipe a piece. "Ensaymada Box of 8" is **Create Variant** of it — the recipe linked, so still 10 g a piece — with Quantity Required 8 and its packaging linked to a standalone **packaging set** holding the 8 bags (`QtyPerSet` 8) and the one box (`QtyPerSet` 1). Its material cost is `8 × ₱0.50 + 8 × ₱0.25 + ₱12 = ₱18`.
  - **The per-piece wrapper is written down twice**, which was the user's choice (2026-09-20) over giving a product a second packaging slot. A product's packaging is its own lines **or** one `PackagingLink`, never both (`CK_PackagingLinks_OneTarget`), so a boxed variant can't read the single's wrapper *and* link a box set; the set carries both instead. Prices still cascade on their own — both point at the same `RawMaterial` — and only the composition is duplicated. The other consequence of that choice was that `SubAssemblyPackagingLine` had no unit column, so a ribbon a product entered in metres had to go into a set in cm; `UnitId` (2026-09-20) put that right, and the two now hold a quantity the same way.
  - **Pricing is linear in the sub-assembly cost** (labor/overhead/administrative are percentages of it, packaging passes straight through, margin and VAT are multiplicative), so **a unit of 8 with no packaging of its own prices at exactly 8 times the single's price** — its own packaging is the whole of the difference. That's pinned by a test in `CostingRulesTests`, as it was for packs.
  - **Every figure on the Products screen is per sellable unit**: `ProductHeaderCard`'s Material Cost, Base Cost, Current Price and Markup %, and the whole of `CostingCard`'s breakdown. `ProductsViewModel.CostUnitCaption` ("Per piece", or "Per unit of 8 pieces") is bound into their tooltips, the breakdown's column header and the Packaging tab's caption, so which unit is never in doubt. The recipe tabs stay per piece: a usage's weight per piece, yield and cost per piece are untouched by this.
  - **Typed in on `ProductDetailsCard`**, beside Current Price (a Syncfusion `IntegerTextBox`, `MinValue` 1), since that card is where the product's own typed fields live and the header holds only read-only figures. Undo covers it (`ProductFormSnapshot.QuantityRequired`), a clone and a variant both copy it, and `ReportsViewModel` multiplies by it so the margin chart is per unit too.
  - Gone with the Packs tab: `ProductPack`, `IProductPackRepository`/`ProductPackRepository`, `ProductPackValidator`, `ProductPackRow`, `PacksCard`, `PackCostSummary`, `ProductRules.EnsureValidPacks` and `CalculatePackMaterialCost`, the `packs` parameter of `IProductRepository.SaveAsync`, and `PackState` in the form snapshot. `ProductsViewModel.LinksTab` went back from 3 to 2.
  - **Migration `ProductQuantityRequiredReplacesPacks`**, which has **one hand-added statement** — the second in the project, after `DropLengthUnitsAndPackagingLineEntryUnit` — `DELETE FROM PackagingLines WHERE PackId IS NOT NULL;` before the column is dropped. Without it a pack's own lines would be left behind as ordinary packaging lines of their product (a box charged on every sellable unit), and a material that wrapped the piece and packed a pack, or two packs sharing one, would break the two-column unique index as it is created. The user's call was that the few packs that existed are re-entered as variants rather than converted.
- **Line order: every line table stores each line's position** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "Sub-Assembly Lines, Product Recipe Lines, Packaging Lines, and BOM should allow reordering of rows through dragging"). `SortOrder` (an `int`, from 0) is on `SubAssemblyLine`, `SubAssemblyPackagingLine`, `IngredientBomLine`, `PackagingLine` and `UsageAddInLine`, the five tables whose lines the user types in and now drags into order (see Application Architecture: rows are dragged into order).
  - **Written from the list's order, read in it.** Every `SetLinesAsync` (and `SaveStandalonePackagingAsync`) already deletes a set's lines and inserts them again, so each line's `SortOrder` is simply its index in the list it's handed; no signature changed. Every read that returns lines in order (`SubAssemblyLineRepository.GetForSubAssembliesAsync`, `IngredientBomLineRepository.GetForIngredientAsync`, `RawMaterialReads.GetPackagingLinesAsync`/`GetSetLinesAsync`, `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.GetAddInsForProductAsync`, `ProductCloner`'s add-ins) orders by `SortOrder, Id`.
  - **Why a column, when the lines were already read `ORDER BY l.Id` and inserted in list order:** EF Core inserts several rows into an identity table with one `MERGE … OUTPUT`, and SQL Server doesn't promise to hand out the identities in the `VALUES` order, so Id order was only the insert order by habit. A dragged order is the user's, and has to survive a save for certain.
  - **Migration `LineSortOrder`** adds the five columns at 0 with no data statement: `ORDER BY SortOrder, Id` reads a set that's all 0s in Id order, which is the order its lines were shown in before, and the next save of that set numbers them. Pinned by `LineOrderPersistenceTests` (a reorder round-trips on each table; the stored order beats the Id order; all-0 rows keep their Id order).
- **CostingParameters**: labor, overhead, administrative (office, permits, bookkeeping; added 2026-09-14, at least 0, default 0), margin %, VAT. **Since 2026-09-15 labor, overhead and administrative are percentages** (`LaborPercent`, `OverheadPercent`, `AdministrativePercent`, at the user's request), not pesos per piece. **Since 2026-09-20 the base they're percentages of is the sub-assembly (ingredient) cost per piece alone, packaging left out** (`CostingRules.CalculateCostFromSubAssemblyPercent`, at the user's request: "Labor Cost is Sub-assemblies (ingredients) * Labor Cost %"): 25% labor on ₱20 of sub-assemblies is ₱5, and packaging still goes into the base cost, just not into these three — what they pay for is making the recipe, not wrapping it. They were percentages of the whole material cost (sub-assemblies **plus** packaging, `CalculateCostFromMaterialPercent`) from 2026-09-15 to then, and `LaborCost`, `OverheadCost` and `AdministrativeCost` in pesos before that. **The stored percentages are untouched by the 2026-09-20 change — no migration, no schema change** — but every product with packaging reprices: its labor, overhead and administrative costs, and so its base cost and selling price, all go down. **Always independent per product**, even for derived/linked products.
  - **Costing defaults** (`CostingDefaults`, 2026-09-19, at the user's request: "set the default value of Labor %, Overhead %, Administrative %, Markup %, and VAT % … at the Settings"): the five percentages a **new product's** costing starts with, edited on Settings' **Costing Defaults** tab (`CostingDefaultsView`/`CostingDefaultsViewModel`, checked by `CostingParametersValidator`, and `CostingRules.EnsureValidParameters` in `CostingDefaultsRepository.SetAsync`). A table of one row (`Id` 1, never an identity, `CK_CostingDefaults_SingleRow`; migration `CostingDefaults`), which reads as all zero until first saved. `ProductsViewModel` reads them with every `LoadCommand` (Settings closing reloads the screen on show) and `StartNewProduct` puts them in the Costing fields while what Save compares against stays at none, so the product's first Save writes them as its own `CostingParameters` (all-zero defaults write nothing, as before). A new product still showing the defaults it started with takes up changed ones on the next load; one whose costing was typed into keeps it. **They never reach a saved product**, not even one without a `CostingParameters` row (it still shows zeros), nor a clone or a variant, which copy their source's.

### Derived products (Phase 10; reworked 2026-09-14)

Some products derive from others (e.g. Special Ensaymada Small from Special Ensaymada Big, differing only in weight; or products sharing a dough sub-assembly but differing in toppings). Since 2026-09-14, when sub-assemblies became owned by products, deriving works like this:

- **Since 2026-09-23 every sub-assembly is kept on the Sub-Assemblies screen** (see SubAssembly above): a new section and a copy are saved there as sub-assemblies named after the product, and linked; Make a Copy gives a new section with the linked one's lines and add-ins, piece size and shrinkage, which Save keeps as "Cheese Roll Dough 2" and links instead. Copy an existing one takes the source's role and lines, not its name. The picker names each "Cheese Roll Dough — Sub-Assemblies (Dough)", by name (`GetLinkableAsync`). The bullets below are as they were written, with "another product's" meaning one a product owned.
- **Link a sub-assembly**: a product uses another product's sub-assembly, or a standalone one, as it is, through a `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` of it. The lines are the owner's (or the Sub-Assemblies screen's) and follow its every change (read-only in the linking product); the **piece size is the linking product's own**, so Small and Big can share a dough at 30 g and 60 g a piece. Links don't chain (only an owner's own or a standalone sub-assembly can be linked), so there's nothing to cycle. A linked section can be turned into the product's own with **Make a Copy**.
- **Copy a sub-assembly**: a new sub-assembly of the product's own, starting from another product's or a standalone one's lines and role (a name, made unique within the product, until 2026-09-17). If the recipe already has that role, the form starts on the first role it doesn't. Nothing connects it to its source afterward.
- The three ways in read **New sub-assembly**, **Link an existing one** and **Copy an existing one** (since 2026-09-15; "…another product's" before). They are the items on Add Sub-Assembly's drop-down since 2026-09-20 — the form's own mode picker before that — and the form's title names the one picked ("Link an existing sub-assembly"). Its picker, **Existing Sub-Assembly**, lists standalone ones first (`GetLinkableAsync`), as "Butter Glaze — Sub-Assemblies (Finishing)". A linked standalone section's badge reads "From Sub-Assemblies" (`ProductUsageEdit.SourceNameOf`, the one place that names where a linked sub-assembly comes from). A new, unsaved product is offered them too: `RefreshLinkableOptions` matches `OwnerProductId is null` first, since a null owner compared with a new product's null id would otherwise count as its own.
- **Link the packaging** (`PackagingLink`, table `PackagingLinks`, one per product): a product's packaging reads through to another product's instead of owning lines. It resolves every line the linked product has. From 2026-09-18 to 2026-09-20 it resolved the per-piece lines only (`PackId is null`), since a pack's lines pointed at rows in the *linked* product's `ProductPacks` and following them would have shown the linking product lines for packs it didn't sell and couldn't edit; with packs gone there is only one set of lines to resolve. It's saved at once, can be repointed or removed at any time, and **unlinking never loses data**: it first materializes the lines it shows (resolved through the link) as the product's own, then removes the link. Chains are allowed (C's packaging linked to B's linked to A's) and resolved recursively at read time; a cycle is rejected when a link is made (`PackagingLinkRules.EnsureNoCycle`). A product whose packaging another links can't be deleted until that link goes.
  - **Or a packaging set** (2026-09-18): `PackagingLink.LinkedProductId` became nullable beside a new `LinkedSubAssemblyId` (Restrict to `SubAssemblies`), and `CK_PackagingLinks_OneTarget` requires exactly one. `IPackagingLinkRepository.SetSubAssemblyLinkAsync` links a standalone set only; `SetLinkAsync` and it each clear the other target on a relink.
    - **One set per sellable unit**: `PackagingLineRepository.ResolveEffectiveAsync` returns the set's lines as unsaved `PackagingLine`s of the product being resolved (`UnitId` and `QtyPerUnit` the set line's own, `AuthoredField = QtyPerUnit`, the material with its units). So the Products screen, the cost summary, Reports and unlinking's copy take them unchanged, and a chain through products ends at a set (it links nothing, so there's nothing to cycle).
    - **A clone keeps a set its source links directly**, like a standalone recipe (the 2026-09-15 rule); a set reached through another product's link is copied like the rest of that product's packaging.
    - The banner reads "Linked to Gift Box Set (Sub-Assemblies)". The Products `LoadCommand` refetches linked packaging's lines while the form still shows the link as saved, so a set edited on the Sub-Assemblies screen shows on return; own lines and an unsaved link choice aren't touched there, since they may hold unsaved edits.
  - **Made in the Recipe Structure, saved with Save** (2026-09-18, at the user's request: "make the Recipe structure empty for new Products, then we can reuse the Add-Subassembly … If the Role is packaging the packaging datagrid will be use"). The **Links & Clone card is gone**, and the tab is just **Clone**; the user's choices were to save the link with Save (like a linked recipe section, so an unsaved product can link too), to drop the card, and to offer both sets and other products' packaging.
    - **Storage didn't change**: the product's packaging is still its per-piece `PackagingLines` or its one `PackagingLink`, never a `SubAssembly` row, so `EnsureRecipeRole` still keeps the Packaging role off a product's own sub-assemblies, and costing, Reports, packs and the cloner are untouched.
    - **`IProductRepository.SaveAsync` takes a `PackagingLinkDraft?`** (`LinkedProductId`/`LinkedSubAssemblyId`, or `PackagingLinkDraft.None`; null leaves the link alone), written in the same transaction just before the lines (`PackagingLinkRepository.SetAsync`, sharing the product and set checks with `SetLinkAsync`/`SetSubAssemblyLinkAsync`). `None` only drops the link; unlike `RemoveLinkAsync` it copies nothing, since the form sends the lines it holds.
    - **Unlinking is Make a Copy on the Packaging tab** (`CopyPackagingCommand`): the lines showing become the product's own, and Save writes both the unlink and the lines (`ChangedLines` writes them whenever the product was linked when loaded, even if they match).
- **Clone** (`IProductCloner.CloneAsync`, one transaction): a one-time copy of a product as saved. **Every sub-assembly it uses, its own and another product's, becomes the clone's own copy** (role, lines and piece size; a source with two in one role, saved before that was refused, can't be cloned until it's put right), plus its packaging as it shows (resolved through a packaging link — a clone never inherits a packaging link, only the data), **its packs with their own packaging lines, as new rows of the clone's own** (2026-09-18), and its `CostingParameters`. Until 2026-09-14 a clone pointed at the same sub-assemblies as its source, so editing one changed both.
  - **A standalone sub-assembly stays linked** (2026-09-15, the user's choice), at the same piece size: it's kept apart to be shared, so the clone follows it like its source does.
  - **Since 2026-09-23 a clone links every sub-assembly its source does** (the user's choice, over copying them as new ones named after the clone), at the same piece sizes and shrinkage, with the source's add-ins as its own: `ProductCloner`'s `CloneRecipe` went, and Clone and Create Variant share `LinkedRecipe`. They differ only in the packaging: Clone copies it, Create Variant links it. Make a Copy splits a section off afterward. **Later the same day** a product's own sub-assemblies (product-owned roles) came back, and a clone or a variant gets its own copy of each of those; see SubAssembly's "Product-owned sub-assemblies".
- **Create Variant** (`IProductCloner.CreateVariantAsync`, one transaction; 2026-09-19, at the user's request: "create a product variant by not only cloning the recipes but also create a linked to them automatically"): a clone whose recipe and packaging are **linked instead of copied**, for Ensaymada Small from Ensaymada Big. It's the Clone tab's second button, beside Clone, sharing its New Product Name.
  - **Every recipe section is linked, at the source's piece sizes**, which are the variant's own to change: the source's own sub-assemblies are linked from the source, and one the source links (another product's or a standalone one) is linked where it's kept, since links don't chain, with the source's add-ins as the variant's own. The variant owns no sub-assembly; Make a Copy unlinks a section as usual. Where a role repeats, one of the source's own without a name goes by the source's name there (`SectionLabel`), so the sections stay apart; a source saved with two unnamed in one role is refused, as for Clone.
  - **Its packaging links the source's** (the user's choice, over copying it), a link to the source product even when the source itself links a set or another product (a chain), and only when the source has packaging (its own lines or a link). Make a Copy on the Packaging tab unlinks it. For a variant that sells differently — a Box of 8 — the link is repointed at a **packaging set** on the Recipe Structure's Packaging tab, which is what makes a variant the way to add a pack.
  - **Its `QuantityRequired` is copied**, and so are the description, category, current price and costing parameters, as for Clone. (Its *packs* were copied until 2026-09-20, when packs became variants of this kind themselves.) `ProductCloner` shares one core between the two (`CreateFromAsync`), which differ only in the recipe (`CloneRecipe`/`VariantRecipe`) and the packaging.
  - The usual guards follow from the links: the source then can't drop a sub-assembly the variant links, or be deleted, until the variant copies or removes it, and the source's sections name the variant among the products linking them.
- **`CostingParameters`** (labor/overhead/administrative/margin/VAT) always stay independent per product: nothing links them.
- **Superseded (2026-09-14): role-tab Live Template links** (`ProductTabLink` with `TabKind = SubAssemblyRole`), which linked all of a product's sub-assemblies in one role to the same role on another product, weights and all. Sub-assembly links replaced them (the user's choice): they cover sharing a recipe, and each product keeps its own weight. What nothing does any more is follow the source's weights, or pick up sub-assemblies added to the source's role later. Migration `ProductOwnedSubAssemblies` turned every role-tab link into links to the sub-assemblies its role showed, at the same weights, and renamed what was left of `ProductTabLinks` (the Packaging-tab links) to `PackagingLinks`.

## Syncfusion UI Framework

The app is styled with **Syncfusion Essential Studio for WPF 34.2.7**, licensed via the `SYNCFUSION_LICENSE_KEY` environment variable, and Syncfusion's **Windows11Light** theme.

- **The theme is Syncfusion's official `Syncfusion.Themes.Windows11Light.WPF` package (Phase 16).** It depends only on `Syncfusion.SfSkinManager.WPF` and `Syncfusion.Licensing`, and it styles every Syncfusion control, so adopting a new control never needs theme work. It replaced a trimmed in-repo theme project (see "Why the theme is no longer trimmed" below) — briefly via a locally packed Theme Studio export, then this package, which is the same theme.
  - **Every Syncfusion package is pinned to the same version, currently `34.2.7`.** The theme package requires `SfSkinManager`/`Licensing` at its own version, NuGet rejects the mismatch as a downgrade (`NU1605`), and Syncfusion doesn't support mixing versions across its assemblies — so upgrading any Syncfusion package means upgrading all of them together: they share the `SyncfusionVersion` property in `Directory.Packages.props`. The `34.2.2` → `34.2.7` upgrade (a patch within the 34.2 release) kept the same license key: no license popup at startup. A move to a new feature release (e.g. 34.3) needs a new key from the Syncfusion account.
  - **One theme gap is patched in the app**: the theme's `SfTextBoxExt` style — unlike its `ComboBoxAdv`, `DoubleTextBox` and `DateTimeEdit` styles — never sets `VerticalContentAlignment`, so the text sits against the top of any box taller than its text (every one, at the 32px `SizeMode="Touch"` height the app used until 2026-09-15). `App`'s static constructor calls `Control.VerticalContentAlignmentProperty.OverrideMetadata(typeof(SfTextBoxExt), new FrameworkPropertyMetadata(VerticalAlignment.Center))`, changing the type's default so every instance (including future ones) is centred without a `Style` on the control; it must run before any `SfTextBoxExt` exists. Verified at runtime: `VerticalContentAlignment=Center`, value source `Default`.
  - **A second `SfTextBoxExt` theme gap: its text sat about 5px further right than the other fields.**
    - The theme's `SfTextBoxExt` template puts a fixed `Margin="10,0,0,0"` on `PART_ContentHost`. `DoubleTextBox`'s template uses 3px of padding instead.
    - Measured in an off-screen replica with the same theme and Touch size, a Name box's text started 13px from its left edge. A `DoubleTextBox` started at 8px and a `ComboBoxAdv` at 7px.
    - The template passes `Padding` (the theme sets -1) to the text presenter inside that margin. `App`'s `AlignTextBoxText` class handler, on `SfTextBoxExt.Loaded`, sets a left padding of -6, which moves the text to 8px.
    - That padding has to be a local value. The theme sets `Padding` through the control's default style (value source `DefaultStyle`), which outranks an `OverrideMetadata` default. A `SetCurrentValue` at `Loaded` was tried first and didn't survive.
    - A `Padding` set in XAML still wins.
  - **A third `SfTextBoxExt` theme gap: its watermark sat at the top of the box, 4px right of typed text.** The template shows `Watermark` in a plain `ContentControl`, whose default template honors neither `VerticalContentAlignment` nor `Padding`, so neither fix above reaches it. `App.xaml`'s `AlignedWatermarkTemplate`, set as the box's `WatermarkTemplate`, centers the hint and moves it 4px left. Measured with the same text typed and as the watermark (a true-size capture with the blinking caret filtered out by keeping each pixel's brightest value across frames), both now start 8px in and cover the same rows. Give it to any `SfTextBoxExt` with a `Watermark`; today that's the Raw Materials search box.
- **Core control set**: the app adopts `SfDataGrid` (lists/grids), `Syncfusion.SfInput.WPF`'s edit controls (`ComboBoxAdv`, `AutoComplete`, `MaskedTextBox`, `DateTimeEdit`, `CurrencyTextBox`, and others), `ButtonAdv`, `TabControlExt`, `DockingManager`/`Ribbon` for the app shell, and `SfChart` for reporting. `TinapayanRMS.UI` takes its own `PackageReference`s (pinned to the shared Syncfusion version above) for exactly these.
  - **`DateTimeEdit`'s bindable date property is `DateTime`, not `Value`** (`Syncfusion.Windows.Shared.DateTimeEdit`, in `Syncfusion.Shared.WPF`) — it doesn't follow the `Value`-property convention `DoubleTextBox`/`IntegerTextBox` use. Confirmed by reflecting over the built assembly (`[System.AppDomain]::CurrentDomain.GetAssemblies() | Where Name -eq Syncfusion.Shared.WPF`, then `.GetType(...).GetProperties()`) rather than guessing again after the compiler rejected `Value`.
  - **Every editable `DoubleTextBox` binds `Value` with `UpdateSourceTrigger=PropertyChanged`** (2026-09-13). Without it the typed value reaches the ViewModel only when the box loses focus: the Syncfusion DoubleTextBox skill says to bind this way "for live updates", and Syncfusion's unit-converter showcase does.
    - It showed up as a dead click: Add Line (the BOM and Sub-Assemblies line editors) stays disabled until the quantity is positive, and a disabled button doesn't take focus, so the first click after typing a quantity neither committed it nor added the line.
    - It also kept the Purchase Cost Setup's cost per base unit and Products' Price Summary from updating as a number is typed.
    - Verified in the app on a new made ingredient (quantity starting at 0): with the cursor still in Quantity, Add Line was already enabled, and one click added the line. Typing 1234.5 arrived intact, with no reformatting mid-typing.
    - `Value` is registered on `DoubleTextBox` itself, so unlike the `SfTextBoxExt` default in `App.xaml.cs`, `OverrideMetadata` can't change the default; set it on each new binding.
  - **`ButtonAdv` icons use `IconTemplate` (a `DataTemplate`), not `SmallIcon`/`LargeIcon`** — those two want an `ImageSource` (a bitmap/vector image file), not a font glyph. With no `IconTemplate` set, the Windows11Light theme falls back to a generic placeholder glyph on every button (reported as "always shows the letter A"). Icons are **Segoe Fluent Icons, falling back to Segoe MDL2 Assets** (`App.xaml`'s `IconFontFamily` resource) — Windows' own icon font, so no extra icon package, and it matches the theme natively. Four reusable `DataTemplate` resources in `App.xaml` (`AddIconTemplate` U+E710, `SaveIconTemplate` U+E74E, `DeleteIconTemplate` U+E74D, `RemoveIconTemplate` U+E738) cover every button so far — reuse them (`IconTemplate="{StaticResource AddIconTemplate}"`, etc.) on new screens' buttons rather than repeating the glyph markup, and add a new named template here if a future screen needs a verb these four don't cover.
  - **`ButtonAdv.Label` defaults to the literal string `"Button"` when left unset** (confirmed by reflecting on `ButtonAdv.LabelProperty`'s metadata the same way `DateTimeEdit`'s shape was confirmed) — an icon-only button (`MainWindow`'s hamburger nav toggle, Phase 15) needs `Label=""` set explicitly, or that placeholder text renders squeezed into the button's icon-sized box ("the button has text that doesn't fit"). Every other `ButtonAdv` in the app already sets a real `Label` ("New", "Save", …) so this never came up before.
  - **`DropDownButtonAdv` is Add Sub-Assembly's button** (Products' Recipe Structure, 2026-09-20): one item per way a sub-assembly comes in, so the way is picked in the click that opens the form. It lives in `Syncfusion.Shared.WPF`, in `Syncfusion.Windows.Tools.Controls` beside `ButtonAdv`, so it needs no new `PackageReference` and takes the same `Label`, `IconTemplate`, `SizeMode` and `ToolTip`; the Windows11Light package styles it. Three things about it, from decompiling it and from Syncfusion's own `editor\Buttons\ButtonView.xaml` demo (no `syncfusion-wpf-*` skill covers it):
    - **Its `Content` *is* the drop-down** — it's a `ContentControl`, and whatever is put inside the element is what the pop-up shows. There is no `Items` or `DropDownContent`.
    - **It doesn't close when something inside the pop-up is clicked.** It closes the drop-down from its own `OnMouseLeftButtonUp`, which only runs for a click on the button itself: the handler it puts on the pop-up (`_dropdown_MouseLeftButtonUp`) forwards to the base `ContentControl` implementation and closes nothing, and a `ButtonAdv` inside has handled the click before it anyway. Each item's `Click` sets `IsDropDownOpen = false` in `RecipeStructureCard.xaml.cs`; the command still runs from the item's own `Command` binding.
    - **The pop-up holds `ButtonAdv`s, not the `ListBox` Syncfusion's demos put there.** A plain `ListBox` keeps the OS default look in this app, since `ApplyThemeAsDefaultStyle` is deliberately off (above), and its blue selection would fight the amber accent; and a `SelectionChanged` command would want `Microsoft.Xaml.Behaviors`, which isn't referenced. Stacked `ButtonAdv`s theme with everything else, reuse the `Add`/`Link`/`Copy` icon templates, and honour `HorizontalContentAlignment="Left"` (checked in the app), so they read as menu items.
      - **Each item is flattened with `Background="Transparent"` and `BorderThickness="0"`** (2026-09-21, at the user's request: the items read as raised buttons, not menu rows). The Windows11Light `ButtonAdv` template `TemplateBinding`s both onto its `ItemBorder`, so setting the two properties is enough — no `Style` and no template override, keeping App.xaml's rule that Syncfusion controls are only ever recolored through `Windows11LightThemeSettings`. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor: the drop-down shows three glyph-and-label rows with no chrome, and the header's own Add/Delete Sub-Assembly buttons are untouched.
      - **The theme's hover survives the transparent background but is nearly invisible on a flat item.** Its `IsMouseOver` trigger sets `ItemBorder.Background` by `TargetName` to an opaque `#FFF9F9F9`, which beats the `TemplateBinding`, so hovering still repaints the row — but against the pop-up's `#FCFCFC` that is a 3-level grey step, measured pixel-by-pixel in a screen capture (252,252,252 → 249,249,249). It also rules out the obvious fix of wrapping each item in a highlighting `Border`: the opaque `#F9F9F9` paints straight over it. A stronger hover would need a template override, which the convention above forbids, so the faint one stands for now.
- **Why the theme is no longer trimmed (Phase 16).** Through Phase 15 the theme was an in-repo project, `TinapayanRMS.Theme`, trimmed from Theme Studio's ~50-control output to the controls the app used, with the rest listed in `_ExcludedThemeFolders`. Trimming silently broke `SfDataGrid` from Phase 4 onward: the theme's `SfDataGrid/SfDataGrid.xaml` merges `ChromelessWindow/ChromelessWindow.xaml` (for the grid's own popup windows), which the trim had excluded. That dictionary therefore failed to load (`IOException: Cannot locate resource 'chromelesswindow/chromelesswindow.xaml'`), **and `SfSkinManager` swallows dictionary-load failures** — so every grid rendered with Syncfusion's unthemed generic style (14pt headers larger than the 12pt data, gridlines on every cell, no Touch-mode row heights) while every other control themed normally. It surfaced as "the grid header font is too big"; three fixes edited the theme's header style and did nothing, because that style was never applied.
  - **What found it**: a throwaway runtime diagnostic reading `DependencyPropertyHelper.GetValueSource` on a header cell's `FontSize` — it reported `DefaultStyle` (Syncfusion's generic style) rather than a theme `Style` — followed by loading `SfDataGrid.xaml` directly into a `ResourceDictionary` inside a try/catch, which surfaced the swallowed exception. **When a theme edit appears to do nothing, check at runtime whether the theme style is applied at all before editing it further.** Reading theme XAML, eyeballing screenshots, and UI Automation bounding boxes (which track row height, not font size) all misled here.
  - A scan of the trimmed theme for merged dictionaries pointing at missing folders found `SfDataGrid` was the only affected control the app used; `Ribbon` and `DockingManager` were also broken but unused. The complete theme has none of these gaps, which is why the app now uses Syncfusion's full official theme package instead.
  - Unrelated fact from the trimming era, still true: the export's "SfTabControl" folder is **not** a general tab control — it's `SfChromelessWindow`'s own tab strip (`Syncfusion.SfChromelessWindow.WPF`). The general-purpose tab control is `TabControlExt` (`Syncfusion.Tools.WPF`).
- **Syncfusion's own agent skills are installed in this repo** at `.claude/skills/syncfusion-wpf-*`, taken from [github.com/syncfusion/wpf-ui-components-skills](https://github.com/syncfusion/wpf-ui-components-skills). That repo carries 96 control skills; the ones matching this app's control set were copied in (navigation-drawer, theming, tabcontrol, datagrid, button, combobox, charts, the `SfInput` editors, plus getting-started and licensing) rather than all of them, keeping it to what's actually used. Syncfusion ships them for the cross-agent `skills` CLI (`npx skills add syncfusion/wpf-ui-components-skills`), which installs to `.agents/skills` — **a path Claude Code does not scan** — so they were copied to `.claude/skills/` instead. Copy in any of the remaining skills the same way if a new control is adopted. These are the first place to look for "how is this control meant to be used", ahead of the Sample Browser source noted in Open Items.
  - **`syncfusion-wpf-ui-builder`** (from [github.com/syncfusion/wpf-ui-builder](https://github.com/syncfusion/wpf-ui-builder), which packages it under `.apm/skills/`) is installed alongside them but is a different kind of thing: an 8-stage generator for building a screen from scratch, not per-control reference. It was installed **deliberately modified** — its `description` narrowed so it only triggers on an explicit "scaffold a new screen" request rather than on any WPF work, and a "TinapayanRMS override" note added at the top of its `SKILL.md`. The override matters because the skill prescribes `INotifyPropertyChanged` + `RelayCommand` MVVM, which contradicts this project's ReactiveUI convention, and its own screen/data-access conventions likewise lose to the ones in this document. **If that skill is ever refreshed from upstream, re-apply both edits** — an unmodified copy will fight the conventions here.
  - **`ui-ux-pro-max`** (third-party, MIT, from [github.com/nextlevelbuilder/ui-ux-pro-max-skill](https://github.com/nextlevelbuilder/ui-ux-pro-max-skill)) is general design intelligence — palettes, typography, 119 UX guidelines, and per-stack guidance including a `wpf` stack — backed by a local Python search script over bundled CSV data. Upstream ships it as a Claude Code **plugin**; it was copied in as a project skill instead to match the Syncfusion ones, which required **three adaptations**, all recorded in a "TinapayanRMS override" note at the top of its `SKILL.md`:
    1. **Script paths rewritten.** Upstream calls `search.py` via `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, which only exists in plugin mode (upstream's own path test says as much) — as a project skill it's empty, so every path resolved to the filesystem root and every search failed. They now use `$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)`, verified working from a subdirectory.
    2. **Stack pinned to `wpf`.** Its stack detection only reads `package.json`/`pubspec.yaml`/`*.xcodeproj`/`composer.json`, so in a .NET repo it detects nothing and would ask every time.
    3. **MVVM guidance overridden.** 9 of its 56 WPF guidelines assume CommunityToolkit.Mvvm and none mention ReactiveUI; its validation guideline even labels this project's deliberate `ViewModelBase.ErrorMessage` + FluentValidation pattern as the anti-pattern, and it recommends unit-testing ViewModels. The override tells it to use only the 47 toolkit-agnostic guidelines, and not to generate a design system that competes with the Syncfusion Windows11Light theme.

    Its upstream `scripts/tests/` CI suite was dropped (the skill never uses it at runtime, and it would now fail against the rewritten paths). Its sibling `ui-styling` skill was **not** installed: it's shadcn/ui + Radix + Tailwind, pure web. **Re-apply all three adaptations if `ui-ux-pro-max` is ever refreshed from upstream.**
- **Size mode: `Default`** (`skinManager:SfSkinManager.SizeMode="Default"` on `MainWindow` and `SettingsWindow`; since 2026-09-15, at the user's request). It was `Touch` before, where the theme gives inputs and buttons a 32px `MinHeight` (`TouchMode.MinHeight` in the theme's `Common.xaml`) and `SfDataGrid` 32px rows under a 36px header. Measured in the running app after the switch, at 100% scaling: `ComboBoxAdv` 25px, `ButtonAdv` 24px, `DoubleTextBox` 22px, grid rows 24px, and `SfTextBoxExt` only 18–20px at its natural height, since no theme minimum applies to it in `Default`. An `SfTextBoxExt` was taller only where its row stretched it (Raw Materials' search box 21px and Name 24px, the product header's name 37px), so an empty one on its own row (Settings' Name and Abbreviation) looked like a thin bar. The alignment fixes above still hold: `AlignTextBoxText`'s padding is a local value, which outranks the theme's size-mode triggers. Checked in the app: Raw Materials, a product's Recipe and Costing tabs, the sub-assembly form (its ingredient pop-up still opens clear of the cell) and Settings.
  - **Every text box and button is 24px tall** (the same day, at the user's request). `App`'s static constructor overrides `FrameworkElement.HeightProperty`'s default to 24 for `SfTextBoxExt`, `DoubleTextBox` and `ButtonAdv` (`ControlHeight`). The theme sets no height on them in `Default`, and a type default is the lowest precedence, so a `Height` set in XAML still wins; a box in a taller row now sits centred at 24px instead of stretching. The error toast's close button (a plain `Button` with its own template) went from 28px to 24px square. `ComboBoxAdv` pickers were left at their own 25px. A `DoubleTextBox` editing a grid number cell is sized by the grid to the 24px row either way (a handler clearing the height there changed nothing, and was taken out). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor (by then 1920×1080 at 125%, so UI Automation read 30 physical px = 24px): Raw Materials, a product's header, section buttons and sub-assembly form, a grams cell being edited, Settings and the error toast.
- **Applying the theme**: `App.xaml.cs`'s static constructor registers the license (`SyncfusionLicenseProvider.RegisterLicense`) before anything else touches a Syncfusion type; `OnStartup` then calls `SfSkinManager.SetTheme(mainWindow, new Theme("Windows11Light"))` before showing the window. Verified by actually launching the built app and screenshotting the themed `MainWindow` (custom title bar chrome, styled `ButtonAdv`) — not just a successful build.
- **Per-monitor DPI awareness (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/app.manifest`, wired via `<ApplicationManifest>`)**. On its own WPF is only *system*-DPI-aware: it renders at the scale the primary monitor had at sign-in, and on a monitor with a different scale Windows bitmap-stretches the whole window. That was reported as "the app is a little blurry on the secondary monitor": the dev machine's primary is 1920×1080 at 125% and its secondary 1366×768 at 100%, so the window rendered at 120 DPI and was shrunk by 0.8. The manifest declares `dpiAwareness` `PerMonitorV2, PerMonitor`, plus the legacy `dpiAware` `true` that only pre-1607 Windows reads. WPF then re-renders natively at each monitor's DPI and handles `WM_DPICHANGED` itself. That needs no code — none of the native-helper/`DisableDpiAwareness` scale-transform approach from Microsoft's Windows 8.1-era article. Layout is unchanged, since everything is in DIPs (the window is 1400×800 physical px on the 100% monitor either way); only sharpness changes. Things that would need extra per-DPI work if ever added: bitmap image assets, `HwndHost`/`WindowsFormsHost`, `RenderTargetBitmap`; the app has none today.
  - **Verified** by querying the running window before and after the manifest: `GetWindowDpiAwarenessContext` went from System aware to Per-monitor V2, and `GetDpiForWindow` on the secondary from 120 to 96. A zoomed before/after crop of the same grid text showed the blur gone. **`PrintWindow` can't show this kind of blur**: it captures the window's own surface at its render DPI, before Windows stretches it. A DPI-scaling check has to capture the screen itself (`CopyFromScreen`, with the window kept topmost via `SWP_NOACTIVATE` so it doesn't take focus).
- **Accent customization (Phase 16, UI/UX refactor)**: `App.xaml.cs` calls `SfSkinManager.RegisterThemeSettings("Windows11Light", new Windows11LightThemeSettings { PrimaryBackground = ..., PrimaryForeground = ... })` before the host is built, re-coloring the theme's accent to a warm amber (`#B45309`) app-wide — `PrimaryBackground`'s setter alone derives the Dark1-3/Light1-3/Opacity1-3 shades, so one property recolors `SfDataGrid` selection, the `TabControlExt`/`SfNavigationDrawer` selection indicators, `SfTextBoxExt` focus underlines, and `ButtonAdv` hover/press. **`SfSkinManager.ApplyThemeAsDefaultStyle` is deliberately never set to `true`** — turning it on makes `SfSkinManager` re-style every open `Window` as a `ChromelessWindow` (`SfSkinManager.ApplyWindowStyle`), replacing the native title bar with Syncfusion's custom chrome. (With the old trimmed theme it also crashed the app on startup, since `ChromelessWindow.xaml` was excluded; the full theme has it, so it would no longer crash — but the chrome change was never wanted.) The trade-off: Syncfusion controls theme normally via `SetTheme`, while plain framework controls (`CheckBox`, `Expander`, `ProgressBar`) keep the OS default look.
- **App-level design tokens (Phase 16)**, in `App.xaml`, alongside the icon templates: a small neutral scale plus the accent (`PageBackgroundBrush` `#F5F5F4`, `CardBackgroundBrush` `#FFFFFF`, `CardBorderBrush` `#E7E5E4`, `HeadingForegroundBrush` `#1C1917`, `MutedForegroundBrush` `#57534E`, `AccentBrush` `#B45309`, plus error/warning background+foreground pairs and two chart brushes) and keyed styles built from them (`CardStyle`, `SectionHeaderStyle`, `FieldLabelStyle`, `CaptionStyle`, `EmptyStateStyle`, `ErrorBannerStyle`/`WarningBannerStyle`, `AppTitleStyle`). **`TextBlock` has no `CharacterSpacing` property in WPF** — that's a WinUI/UWP-only property; a `Style TargetType="TextBlock"` `Setter` for it fails the build with `MC4005`, found while first writing `AppTitleStyle`. **These styles are keyed and target only native elements (`Border`, `TextBlock`) — never a `Style` with no `x:Key`, and never one whose `TargetType` is a Syncfusion control.** An implicit (unkeyed) style on a native type would leak into every Syncfusion control template that hosts one internally; a `Style` set directly on a Syncfusion control would replace its themed template outright. Syncfusion controls only ever get recolored through `Windows11LightThemeSettings`, per the bullet above. `EmptyStateVisibilityConverter` (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/Converters/EmptyStateVisibilityConverter.cs`) is registered alongside them — `Visible` when the bound value is `null` or `false`, `Collapsed` otherwise, so the same converter drives an empty-state hint off either a nullable selection (`SelectedIngredient == null`) or a boolean flag (Products' `HasMaterialCost == false`).
- **`ErrorToast`, the error toast** (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/Controls/ErrorToast.xaml`; 2026-09-14, at the user's request, replacing the Phase 16 `StatusBanner`). A screen's `ErrorMessage` shows as a toast in its page's bottom-right corner, drawn over the cards: a red error icon, "Error", the message and a close button. Like every control here, it never sets its own `DataContext`; it binds to the `ErrorMessage` every `ViewModelBase` has, so a screen only drops it into its root Grid, spanning every row and column (the toast aligns itself to the corner, 16 px in).
  - It stays until its close button is clicked or the error clears, which the next action does (`RunSafelyAsync` clears `ErrorMessage` first); a new error replaces it, and the same error twice still shows twice. Closing it only hides it: the error stays on the ViewModel.
  - The banner it replaced took a row at the top of every page, leaving an empty 12 px strip, and carried a busy bar. Both went: saves take well under a second and buttons disable while they run, so `ViewModelBase.IsBusy` and `HasError` were removed too.
  - **Why not Syncfusion's `SfToastNotification`, which the user asked for first:** tried and dropped (34.2.7). Its toasts are always-on-top popups, so an error left open floated over other programs (it covered Excel). And `Window` mode always anchors to the window's outer corner, 12 px in, reading `Window.Left/Top`, which a maximized WPF window reports as its restore position, so a maximized window's toast landed too low. It has no offset setting (the `Margin` in the 34.2.2 docs is gone). Its defaults also add a "Here's an update for you" header and Continue/Cancel buttons. The user chose a toast drawn inside the page instead.
  - `ReportsView` has no toast: nothing there can fail (its Phase 12 rationale).
- **`SuccessToast`, the success toast** (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/Controls/SuccessToast.xaml`; 2026-09-15, at the user's request: a toast after saving a raw material). A screen's `SuccessMessage` (on `ViewModelBase`, cleared with `ErrorMessage` at the start of `RunSafelyAsync`) shows in the error toast's corner, laid out like it in green: a check icon, "Success", the message and a close button. A screen drops it into its root Grid beside the `ErrorToast`; since every action clears both messages first, only one shows at a time.
  - It fades out 4 seconds after it opens, unless the mouse is over it (checked again every 4 seconds), and its close button hides it at once. An error replaces it. Verified in the app: shown after Save, gone 5 seconds later, still up after 6 seconds under the mouse.
  - Only the Raw Materials screen sets it so far: its Save says "Cake Flour was saved.", naming the material as saved.
  - The close button's style (`ToastCloseButtonStyle`) moved from `ErrorToast` into `App.xaml` for both toasts, next to the new `SuccessBorderBrush`.
- **`SfChart` shape** (`Syncfusion.UI.Xaml.Charts` namespace, first used Phase 12 — confirmed by reflecting over the built `Syncfusion.SfChart.WPF.dll` the same way `DateTimeEdit` was, rather than guessing): the root `SfChart` element takes exactly one `PrimaryAxis` and one `SecondaryAxis` (property elements, not collections) plus a `Series` collection (`SfChart.Series`) of `ChartSeriesBase`-derived series (`LineSeries`, `ColumnSeries`, …) and an optional `Legend` (`SfChart.Legend`, a `ChartLegend`). Each series binds independently — `ItemsSource`, `XBindingPath`, `YBindingPath`, `Label` (the legend text) all live on `ChartSeriesBase`/`XyDataSeries`, so two series (e.g. `ColumnSeries` for "Base Cost" and another for "Selling Price") can share one `ItemsSource` and just point `YBindingPath` at different properties of the same row type. `DateTimeAxis` binds cleanly to a plain `DateTime` `XBindingPath` — project a `DateOnly` column to `DateTime` (`.ToDateTime(TimeOnly.MinValue)`) in the chart-row DTO rather than binding `DateOnly` directly, since that conversion's support wasn't worth gambling on for a type newer than the library's own value-binding internals. **A fixed, distinct color per series is just `Interior="{StaticResource ...Brush}"` on that series** (`ChartSeriesBase`) — `Palette="Custom"` plus a `ColorModel`/`ChartColorModel.CustomBrushes` list (Phase 16's Reports rework first reached for this, going by the pattern in `.claude/skills/syncfusion-wpf-charts`) is for coloring the individual *segments within one series* differently (e.g. each category's bar its own color), not for giving two separate named series — "Base Cost" and "Selling Price" here — two different fixed colors; using it for that would need the two series merged into one with a value-based color selector, which is unnecessary when `Interior` alone does the job per series. Direct value labels on a `ColumnSeries` (so a comparison doesn't rely on color alone) are `<syncfusion:ColumnSeries.AdornmentsInfo><syncfusion:ChartAdornmentInfo ShowLabel="True" /></syncfusion:ColumnSeries.AdornmentsInfo>` — the property is `AdornmentsInfo`, the element type is `ChartAdornmentInfo`. **One series whose segments each need their own colour is where `Palette="Custom"` + `ChartColorModel` does apply** (Products' Recipe Cost Overview, 2026-09-14 until it was removed on 2026-09-19, following the Sample Browser's `Circular Charts\Doughnut\DoughnutChart.xaml` and `StackedDoughnut\CustomizedStackedDoughnut.xaml`): a `DoughnutSeries` over `CostShares`, with a `ChartColorModel` in the card's own resources (`CustomBrushes`: the amber accent, then teal, amber-light, slate, rose, sky, lime, violet, so neighbouring slices differ). The legend's `ItemTemplate` binds `{Binding Interior}` for the swatch and `Item.*` for the row's own fields (`Item.Name`, `Item.Amount`, `Item.Percent`). `CenterView` needs a `ContentControl`, as in the sample (a panel is rejected at runtime), with `Content="{Binding Costs}"` and a template.
- **A reporting screen with no new domain logic doesn't need new tests of its own** (`ReportsViewModel`, Phase 12): its price-trend and margin calculations call the *exact same* `ProductRules`/`CostingRules` functions and repository methods `ProductsViewModel` already uses and that are already covered by `ProductRulesTests`/`CostingRulesTests`/`ProductPersistenceTests` — there's no new validation, no new domain rule, nothing this screen computes that isn't a thin re-orchestration of already-tested pieces. Verified instead by launching the real app, seeding real price history and a real product, and checking the rendered numbers against a hand computation (Phase 12's margin bars matched the manual `BaseCost`/`SellingPrice` calculation exactly) — consistent with the project's standing rule that no `ViewModel` gets a direct unit/integration test; only `ApplicationCore` rules and `Infrastructure` repositories do. **`ReportsView`'s two chart panels moved from a horizontal `ScrollViewer` of fixed 620px-wide `StackPanel`s to a `*`/`*` two-column `Grid` of `CardStyle` cards in Phase 16** — neither panel needs more than the available half-width at the app's normal window size, so the scroll-to-see-the-second-chart behavior was never load-bearing, just a side effect of the original fixed-width layout.

## Application Architecture

- **Repository interfaces live in `ApplicationCore`, implementations in `Infrastructure`** (`IRawMaterialRepository`/`RawMaterialRepository`, and so on), consumed by UI ViewModels through DI. `ILookupRepository<T>` in `ApplicationCore`, with one generic `LookupRepository<T>` implementation in `Infrastructure`, covers every simple **read-only** lookup table (`UnitOfMeasure`, `IngredientCategory`, `PackagingCategory`) instead of writing a near-identical class per table. `SubAssemblyRole` turned out **not** to fit this: unlike those three, it's user-editable (Phase 7 gave it its own small embedded management panel), so it needed a dedicated `ISubAssemblyRoleRepository`/`SubAssemblyRoleRepository` with full CRUD instead. `ILookupRepository<T>` is for genuinely read-only lookups only — don't reach for it just because a table is small.
- **`TinapayanRMSDbContext` is registered with `AddDbContextFactory`, not `AddDbContext`.** ViewModels are DI singletons (`MainWindowViewModel`, `RawMaterialsViewModel`, …) so they outlive any single screen, and WPF has no ASP.NET-style per-request scope to hand a plain `AddDbContext`-registered instance to — resolving one into two singleton ViewModels at startup handed them the *same* `DbContext` instance, and two screens' tabs loading concurrently on app startup then threw `InvalidOperationException: A second operation was started on this context instance before a previous operation completed` the first time the app was actually run (not caught by the build or by any test — only by launching it). Every repository instead takes `IDbContextFactory<TinapayanRMSDbContext>` and creates-and-disposes a short-lived context per call (`await using var dbContext = await dbContextFactory.CreateDbContextAsync(cancellationToken);`), which is also naturally safe for concurrent calls from different screens. Follow this pattern for every future repository — never inject `TinapayanRMSDbContext` directly into a repository or ViewModel.
- **CRUD screen pattern** (established by `IngredientsView`/`IngredientsViewModel` and `PackagingMaterialsView`/`PackagingMaterialsViewModel`, Phase 4, merged into `RawMaterialsView`/`RawMaterialsViewModel` on 2026-09-13; card layout and right-rail arrangement reworked Phase 16): a read-only `SfDataGrid` list on the left, bound to a `DynamicData SourceCache<T,int>` via `.Connect().SortAndBind(out collection, comparer).Subscribe()` (not the obsolete `.Sort().Bind()` pair); a master-detail edit form on the right, populated from the grid's `SelectedItem` via a `WhenAnyValue` subscription; `New`/`Save`/`Delete` as `ButtonAdv`s bound to `ReactiveCommand`s. Follow this pattern for future CRUD screens rather than in-grid cell editing, which needs SfDataGrid event wiring that's much harder to get right without a live designer.
  - **Since Phase 16, the shape is a list card plus a right rail, not fixed Grid columns of bare panels.** The root `Grid` carries `PageBackgroundBrush`, `Margin="16"`, and an `ErrorToast` laid over the whole Grid (it takes no row of its own). The Grid is two columns: a `*`-width list card (`CardStyle` `Border` + `SectionHeaderStyle` header + the `SfDataGrid`, `ColumnSizer="AutoWithLastColumnFill"` so every column — including money columns — fits without a horizontal scrollbar; `RawMaterialsView` gives every column a fixed width instead and sizes its list card to fit them (see the Raw Materials note below). A column's `Width` accepts pixels only, as in `Width="120"`. A value of `"*"` or `"2*"` builds cleanly but crashes the app at startup with a `XamlParseException` ("* is not a valid value for Double"), so star-size through `ColumnSizer="Star"` on the grid or on a column), and a fixed ~360px right rail in a `ScrollViewer` holding, top to bottom: a Details card (the edit form), the screen's existing conditional detail card(s) (Price History, BOM, Lines) shown under their existing `Show*` flags with an `EmptyStateStyle` hint (bound through `EmptyStateVisibilityConverter`) filling the same slot when nothing is selected, and a collapsed `Expander` for any embedded lookup panel (see the note below). `UnitsOfMeasureView` and `CategoriesView`, the Settings dialog's tabs, are the simplest case — list card plus a single form card, no expander, no conditional detail card. Every number column in a grid, money included, uses `controls:NumericColumn` (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/Controls/NumericColumn.cs`). Never use `syncfusion:GridNumericColumn` or `syncfusion:GridCurrencyColumn` directly. Money shows as a bare number (`930.00`, not `₱930.00`), so `GridCurrencyColumn` would add nothing useful: its currency editor never opens in these read-only grids, its parentheses for negatives never apply because costs are never negative, and it doesn't group thousands by default. Money columns use 2 decimals (`NumberDecimalDigits`) for totals and prices, and 4 for per-unit costs (`CostPerBaseUnit`, `CostPerPiece`). `NumericColumn` is a `GridNumericColumn` whose constructor sets the app's defaults: `NumberGroupSeparator=","`, `NumberGroupSizes="3"` and `HorizontalHeaderContentAlignment="Right"`. Out of the box, `GridNumericColumn` doesn't group thousands (`NumberGroupSizes` is `[0]`) and centres its header. A grid column is a `Freezable`, not a `FrameworkElement`, so an implicit `Style` can't carry these defaults. This was confirmed by reflecting over `Syncfusion.SfGrid.WPF` 34.2.7. Set only what differs per column (`NumberDecimalDigits`); a value set in XAML still overrides a default.
  - **`ProductsView` (Phase 8/9) no longer uses a horizontal `ScrollViewer` of fixed-width `StackPanel`s — Phase 16 replaced that with sub-tabs plus a pinned summary**, once the six-panel version pushed the Selling Price summary off-screen. Now three columns: a 300px-wide left column (Products list card, then a Product form card below it), a centre `TabControlExt` (`CloseButtonType="Hide"`, `ShowTabItemContextMenu="False"`, `ShowTabListContextMenu="False"`, every `TabItemExt` `CanClose="False"` — these are fixed sub-tabs, not user-closable top-level tabs) holding **Usages**/**Packaging**/**Costing**/**Links & Clone**, each carrying its existing panel's content verbatim, and a pinned 260px right column with the Price Summary card (Material Cost, Base Cost, Pre-VAT Price, then Selling Price large and `AccentBrush`-colored) that stays visible across every sub-tab. Both the tab strip and the summary card are visible only when `ShowProductDetail` is true; an `EmptyStateStyle` hint fills the centre otherwise. The linked-tab notices (`IsSelectedRoleLinked`/`IsPackagingLinked`) moved from inline amber `TextBlock`s into `WarningBannerStyle` banners.
    - **Superseded (2026-09-14): the sub-tabs, pinned summary and per-part Saves are gone.** The screen was rebuilt from the user's Cream Loaf / Chocolate Cake mockups, in the Raw Materials shape: see "One Products screen" below.
- **Grids show lines both ways, and a grid with height to spare is padded with empty rows down to its bottom edge** (2026-09-15, at the user's request, following the technique in their `SfDataGrid_Demo_fixed` sample).
  - **`GridLinesVisibility="Both"` is set on every `SfDataGrid` in `Views/`.** The property's own default is Both, but the Windows11Light style sets Horizontal, so it's set locally on each grid (the app doesn't style Syncfusion controls itself: a `Style` would replace the theme's). The two recipe section tables draw their Total row under the grid, so each total cell but the last has a right divider (`SectionTotalDividerStyle`, `CardBorderBrush`) carrying the grid's vertical lines through it. The ingredient pickers' pop-up grids keep their look.
  - **The tables lines are typed into show a row header (`ShowRowHeader="True"`)** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "add row selector to datagrids", the typed-into grids when asked which): Packaging, the Products sub-assembly form's lines and add-ins, Sub-Assemblies' lines, the Bill of Materials and Alternative Units. It's the grid's own column: an arrow on the current row, a pencil while a row is edited and + on the add row; filler rows' header cells stay blank. The read-only lists (Raw Materials, Products, Sub-Assemblies, Price History, Settings) don't have it. The app's grid behaviours need no change for it: they go by row index, and `GridFillerRows.IsRecordCell` uses `GetFirstColumnIndex`, which counts the header. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor on all five screens' grids.
  - **Rows are dragged into order** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "Sub-Assembly Lines, Product Recipe Lines, Packaging Lines, and BOM should allow reordering of rows through dragging"). `controls:GridRowReordering.IsEnabled="True"` (`Controls/GridRowReordering.cs`) is on the seven grids lines are typed into and saved from: Sub-Assemblies' lines and a packaging set's lines, the Products sub-assembly form's lines, add-ins and packaging, the Packaging tab, and the Bill of Materials. Not on Alternative Units or Purchase Cost Setup, whose order means nothing. The order is the ViewModel collection's, which Save writes (see Domain Model: Line order), and it needs nothing else to count as a change: every form snapshot compares its lines with `SequenceEqual`, so a move is an undo step, marks the form dirty, and an undo puts the order back.
    - **It turns on the grid's own row drag** (`AllowDraggingRows`, `AllowDrop`, `RowDropIndicatorMode="Line"`), which starts from the row header or any cell not being typed into, once the mouse has moved the system drag distance: `GridRowHeaderCell` is a `GridCell`, and `GridCell.OnPreviewMouseDown` arms a drag only when the cell isn't editing, so selecting text in an open cell never drags the row. A plain click still opens the cell (`EditTrigger="OnTap"`).
    - **Each press decides whether the grid can drag** (`ArmOnLinePress`, on the grid's `PreviewMouseDown`, which tunnels before the cell's): only a press on a line of a grid whose lines can be changed sets `AllowDraggingRows`. Once armed, `GridCell` calls `ProcessOnDragStarting` on every mouse move until the button comes up, and that logs Syncfusion telemetry each time (`TelemetryService.Log` → `Telemetry.TrackFeature`, a new client and an HTTP post per call, no de-duplication; decompiled from 34.2.7). A read-only grid's drag is refused and the add row has no line to drag, so either would have retried, and posted, on every move of the gesture. Presses on filler rows never reach the cell (`GridFillerRows` handles them).
    - **Every drop is handled by the behaviour, because the grid's own doesn't reorder anything that's saved.** Read from the decompiled `GridRowDragDropController` (Syncfusion 34.2.7): within one grid, `DroppingItemsInDataGrid` removes and re-inserts the `RecordEntry` in `View.Records` only, so the bound `ObservableCollection` never moves. And across grids it moves a row into any other grid whose items are the same type, which the recipe lines and add-ins are (`IngredientLineRow`). So `Drop` is always `Handled`, and the line is moved in the collection with `ObservableCollection<T>.Move`, bracketed by the grid's `GridUndoSteps` recorder. It's a single Move notification, not a remove and an insert, so the row stays selected and a formula-base line never briefly leaves the batch, which would set off the baker's-percentage recomputation twice. `Move` is found by reflection, since the behaviour only knows the collection as an `IList`.
    - **The drop's `TargetRecord` is a record index in the view, not the item** (`ResolveToRecordIndex` of the row dropped on), and the line grids allow sorting, so it's resolved through `View.Records[i].Data` and then found in the collection. A sorted view is unsorted on the drop (`SortColumnDescriptions.Clear()`), so the grid shows the order that was just changed rather than a sort that hides it.
    - **Refused:** a drag from a read-only grid (`AllowEditing` false: a linked section's lines, a linked packaging), a drag in from another grid (`IsFromOutSideSource`, `DragOver` handled so no drop line shows), and a drop on the add row or a filler row, both of which `ResolveToRecordIndex` gives -1, so the grid's `GetDropPosition` returns `None` for them itself. Selection is `Single`, so one line moves at a time.
    - **Verified in the app** on the secondary monitor against the dev database, with real mouse drags and nothing saved: Sub-Assemblies' lines (up from the row header, down from a cell, and in a view sorted by Grams, which the drop unsorted with the line placed by the lines' own order), the Products Packaging tab, and a made ingredient's Bill of Materials each moved the line, kept it selected, and offered Save and Undo; Undo put the order back and Save went off again. A linked section's lines didn't move, and an add-in dragged onto the recipe lines above it showed no drop line and stayed where it was. Not driven by hand: a packaging set's lines and the Products form's own lines and packaging (the same behaviour on the same kind of grid). That a dragged order survives a save is `LineOrderPersistenceTests`' to show.
  - **`CurrentCellBorderBrush="{StaticResource AccentBrush}"` is set on every `SfDataGrid` in `Views/`, the same way and for the same reason** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: the cell selector outline was black). `SfGridBase` registers the property with a hardcoded `SolidColorBrush(Colors.Black)` default (confirmed by decompiling `Syncfusion.SfGrid.WPF` 34.2.7 with `ilspycmd`) that the Windows11Light accent re-color (`PrimaryBackground` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnStartup`) doesn't reach, so it stays black regardless of theme unless set explicitly. `CurrentCellBorderThickness` (default `2`) is set to `1` too, at the user's request the same day. `1.5` looked identical to the default when checked pixel-by-pixel in a screenshot (both rendered a 2px border at the secondary monitor's 100% scale — `UseLayoutRounding` snaps a fractional thickness up to the next whole device pixel), so only a whole-number step down actually reads as thinner.
  - **`controls:GridFillerRows.IsEnabled="True"`** (`Controls/GridFillerRows.cs`) is on the grids with a height to fill: the Raw Materials, Products and Sub-Assemblies lists, Price History, Settings' Units, Categories and Roles, and the tables lines are typed into (Sub-Assemblies' lines, the Products sub-assembly form and Packaging tab, the Bill of Materials). Not on the recipe section tables, which are sized to their rows, or Alternative Units, whose `MaxHeight` would make it always 240 px tall.
  - Like the sample, the rows are `GridUnBoundRow`s, recalculated when the grid loads, resizes, its view's records change, or a group expands or collapses. **What differs was read from the decompiled Syncfusion 34.2.7 (ilspycmd):**
    - **`ShowBelowSummary = true`.** `GridIndexResolver.ResolveUnboundRowToRowIndex` and `GridAddNewRowController.GetAddNewRowIndex` put bottom unbound rows *without* it before the add-new row, which would have sunk the add row below the filler rows. With it they're the grid's last rows.
    - **Those rows are frozen footers** (`UpdateFreezePaneRows` counts them in `FooterRows`). With a short body they follow it (`ScrollAxisBase.GetVisibleLines` starts footers at `Max(HeaderExtent, ViewSize − FooterExtent)`, and `PixelScrollAxis.ViewSize` is `Min(RenderSize, TotalDistance)`), but rows that no longer fit would be pinned over the grid's last rows. So rows that don't fit are removed at once, synchronously, when records are added, a group expands or the grid shrinks, **and after any layout pass that finds them overflowing** (see the crash notes below); adding rows waits for layout (one `Background`-priority recalculation however many triggers ask) and, when the grid grows, for 150 ms of calm.
    - **The space is measured on the row scroll axis:** `GetVisualContainer().ScrollRows` (`PixelScrollAxis`) `RenderSize` against `TotalExtent`, which counts the header, group captions and add-new row as drawn, in O(1). `GridFillerRows.FillerRowCount` (unit-tested) floors to whole rows, so filler never adds a scrollbar.
    - **Only the difference is added or removed, never `Clear()`**: `UnBoundRows`' Reset handling (and `ReCalculateUnboundRowsCount`) counts rows with `Select(cond).Count()`, which counts every row.
    - **The rows are inert.** Unbound rows are selectable, and in a single-selection list a click on one deselects the record, which clears the form beside it; in a table with `EditTrigger="OnTap"` a click anywhere begins editing the current cell (`GridCurrentCellManager.ProcessOnTapped`). A press on a filler row is handled in `PreviewMouseDown`, and `SelectionChanging`/`CurrentCellActivating` are cancelled for one (keyboard). Filler rows are recognised by position (the last rows of `VisualContainer.RowCount`), not with `SfDataGrid.GetUnBoundRow`: an unbound row's `RowIndex` isn't kept up to date as records arrive, and a first version using it swallowed clicks on the Ingredients group caption.
      - **Only a press in the grid's own window counts** (2026-09-17, reported by the user: a packaging material picked with the mouse never showed in its cell). A drop-down cell's list is a `Popup`, a window of its own but inside the grid's tree, so its presses tunnel through the grid's `PreviewMouseDown` too, and the filler-row check was by position alone. Where the list opens below its cell it lies over the filler rows, so a click on an item was taken for a click on a filler row and stopped. The list's own `GridCell` then never recorded the press (`GridSelectionController.ProcessPointerPressed`), the release selected nothing, and `SfMultiColumnDropDownControl.OnInternalGridTapped` committed no item. Opened above (the sub-assembly form, lower on the screen) it worked, which is what gave it away. Now the press must come from the grid's own `PresentationSource`. It hit every filler-row grid with a drop-down; found with a temporary trace of the press's `Handled` flag down the route. Verified in the app: a material picked with the mouse from a list opened below the add row, and a click on a filler row still selecting nothing.
    - **Changing unbound rows reaches the selection** (`GridSelectionController.ProcessUnBoundRowChanged`): it ends an edit of an existing row without committing it, and adding one while the current cell is on the row above the add-new row moves the current cell onto that row. Nothing changes while `View.IsEditingItem` (tried again 150 ms later), and a current cell moved by an addition is put back (`MoveCurrentCell`) — **only from the add-new row, and only onto a record's cell that still exists** (see the crash notes below).
  - **Two crashes it caused, fixed 2026-09-16** (reported as "sometimes the program crashes"; neither could be reproduced by clicking around). The evidence was the Windows Application event log (`.NET Runtime`, event 1026, which keeps the full stack of an unhandled exception) and the app's own Serilog file (`logs/tinapayanrms-<date>.log` beside the exe), whose warnings showed what the user did just before. Both were then reproduced by **fuzzing a real `SfDataGrid`** off-screen: random sequences of what the screens do to a grid, with the app's own behaviours attached, until one threw. The fuzz harnesses were throwaway; the sequences they found are the tests.
    - **"InValid RowColumnIndex" from `FillerState.Apply`** (twice on the Products screen, each shortly after an add-in was refused). The grid keeps the current cell of a row that's gone: select a line (the grid's `SelectedItem`, which `SelectedFormLine` binds), then replace the lines (another sub-assembly's form), and the current cell still points at the old row index. Filler rows added to the emptier table make the grid drop it (`ProcessUnBoundRowChanged`), and `Apply` moved it back — to a row past `GetLastNavigatingRowIndex`, which `CanMoveCurrentCell` rejects by throwing, from a `Background` dispatcher callback, so nothing caught it. **The move back now only undoes the one move it exists for**: the current cell pushed from a record onto the add-new row, and only onto a record's cell that still exists (`ResolveToRecordIndex`, the visible column range). `GridFillerRowsCurrentCellTests` fails on the old code with the same exception.
    - **`ArgumentOutOfRangeException` from `GroupDisplayElements.GetItemAt` during layout** (the grouped Raw Materials list). Fuzzing the list as `MaterialListCard` sets it up — grouped, name-sorted, `AllowDataShaping`, fed by a DynamicData `SortAndBind` with the ViewModel's merge-then-reselect refresh — crashed within 15 steps with filler rows on, and never in the identical sequence with them off. The row counts always added up (header + group display elements + filler rows); what preceded every crash was **filler rows left overflowing**: `ExpandAllGroup` (and `CollapseAllGroup`) raise no `GroupExpanded`/`GroupCollapsed` and change no records, so nothing told `GridFillerRows` the rows had grown, and frozen footer rows were left over the body until a later refresh broke row generation. Deferring the handlers (so they ran after the notification) changed nothing. **A `LayoutUpdated` check now takes away rows that no longer fit after any layout** — two numbers compared, so it's cheap on every grid. With it, 30 seeds × 70 steps of the list and 12 × 60 of an editable lines table ran with no crash and no filler row left overflowing after layout. `GridFillerRowsGroupedTests` pins the overflow (it fails on the old code); the crash itself only ever followed it.
    - **Grid tests run in a real window**: `Fixtures/OffscreenGrid` shows an `SfDataGrid` off-screen on an STA thread of its own and pumps the dispatcher, so an exception the grid throws from a dispatcher callback surfaces in the test as it would have closed the app. It needs `SYNCFUSION_LICENSE_KEY` (an unlicensed control shows a dialog) and skips without it.
  - **Found on the way, not caused by it:** Raw Materials' groups start collapsed although the grid has `AutoExpandGroups`. Syncfusion.Data expands a new top-level group only while grouping is being applied (`CollectionView.IsInGroup`), and the list loads empty, its records arriving later through `LoadCommand`.
  - **Verified in the app** on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved:
    - lists filled to the bottom with vertical lines and no scrollbar; a search down to one match filled more rows; expanding Ingredients removed them all (the list scrolled) and collapsing brought them back, with the form (New, or Cake Flour) untouched;
    - clicking a filler row changed nothing;
    - in Sub-Assemblies' lines table, a committed line kept the add row right under it, Down from the add row stayed put, a click below started no edit, and deleting the line brought a filler row back;
    - the Products list and form, Price History, and the Settings dialog's lists (opened twice, padded once) were padded; the recipe Total row's dividers lined up;
    - shrinking the window showed no filler rows over the data even 60 ms later, and maximizing refilled.
- **A tab keeps its view state while the app runs, and still picks up changes made on other tabs.** Nothing is saved to disk; the state lasts for the session only.
  - **Tab content stays loaded.** `MainTabControl` has `IsDisableUnloadTabItemExtContent="True"`. By default `TabControlExt` unloads a tab's content on every switch; measured off-screen, content was unloaded twice and re-loaded three times over four switches, and never unloaded with the property set. So scroll position, sorting, sub-tabs, expanders, focus and typed text survive a switch without any code.
  - **One load trigger: tab activation.** Views don't load data on `Loaded`. `MainWindow.xaml.cs` maps each `TabItemExt` to its screen's `LoadCommand` and runs it for the tab shown at startup and on each `MainTabControl.SelectionChanged`. It ignores bubbled events whose `OriginalSource` isn't `MainTabControl`, and skips a tab whose refresh is still running.
  - **Every `LoadCommand` is a fetch-then-merge, never a clear-and-reload.** Clearing a list is what used to lose the selected row, picker choices and Products' unsaved rows on every return.
    1. All `await`s finish first.
    2. The ids of the selected row, every picker and the lookup-panel selection are recorded. A ComboBoxAdv can clear its own `SelectedItem` when its item is replaced mid-merge, so the ids must be read before merging.
    3. Inside `ApplyRefresh` (`ViewModelBase`, which sets `IsRefreshing`), the lists are merged with `CollectionMerge.MergeFrom`, and each selection and picker is re-pointed by id.
  - **`CollectionMerge`** (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/ViewModels/CollectionMerge.cs`) keeps the existing object for every row whose values didn't change, which is what a grid's selection and scroll and a picker's `SelectedItem` are tied to. A `SourceCache` merge uses DynamicData's `EditDiff`; an `ObservableCollection` is synced in place. `HasSameValues` compares scalar properties plus those of referenced entities one level deep (so a renamed category counts as a change to an ingredient showing it). A collection the row owns (a raw material's `Units`) counts as part of the row: its elements are compared in order, the same way as the row, so an added unit — or a unit of measure renamed in Settings — replaces the row. A screen whose selected row is replaced that way rebuilds the unit rows and pickers derived from it, keeping picks by id.
  - **Selection-driven subscriptions skip while `IsRefreshing`**: form loads, "reset the detail fields" blocks, detail reloads, lookup-panel form loads, and Products' role and packaging-material pickers. Re-pointing a selection at its refreshed copy must not reload the form or throw away unsaved edits. `ReportsViewModel` isn't a `ViewModelBase` and has its own `_isRefreshing`.
  - **Products** also rebuilds its unsaved usage and packaging rows with the refreshed sub-assemblies, materials and units (`RefreshUnsavedRows`), keeping each row's numbers, so costs changed on other tabs reach the recipe sections and the cost summary. Since 2026-09-14 it also refetches the lines of every sub-assembly the recipe uses (`GetForSubAssembliesAsync`, one query), and rebuilds only a row whose sub-assembly or lines changed (`CollectionMerge.HasSameValues`). It also refreshes the selected product's tab links.
    - **Since sub-assemblies became owned (2026-09-14)** it's `RefreshRecipeRows`: a section of the product's own keeps its unsaved lines and is only repriced with the refreshed ingredients; a linked section is refetched (`ISubAssemblyRepository.GetByIdsAsync` and its lines, one query each), since its owner may have changed its lines, name or role there; each keeps its own piece size, and the sections are re-sorted if a role or name moved one. Who links the product's own sub-assemblies ("Also used by …") and its packaging link are refreshed too.
  - **Save, Delete and the lookup panels call the same `LoadCommand`**, so an existing record stays selected after saving, and renaming a category or role updates the grid.
  - Covered by `tests/TinapayanRMS.Tests/ViewModels/`: `CollectionMergeTests`, plus Ingredients and Products refresh tests that build the ViewModel on real repositories.
  - **Back and Forward between screens** (2026-09-23, at the user's request).
    - **What they do.** ← → buttons in the header, after the menu toggle, and Alt+Left / Alt+Right.
    - **What a step is.** A change of screen, however it happens: the menu, the tab strip, a tab closed, or a link from one screen to another (a Used By tile, a linked badge). Picking another record in a list isn't a step. The user chose this over "every record opened" and "screens only".
    - **Where it lives.** `NavigationHistory` (`ViewModels/NavigationHistory.cs`, on `MainWindowViewModel.Navigation`) holds the back and forward stacks of `ScreenVisit(AppScreen, RecordId)`, capped at 50. It knows nothing of tabs.
    - **Recording.** `MainWindow.MainTabControl_SelectionChanged` records each step, with the record the screen had open *as it was left*. Each tab maps to a `HistoryScreen`: the selected product, sub-assembly or raw material id; the screen's `Open*` method (`OpenProduct`, `OpenSubAssembly`, `OpenRawMaterial`); and its form's `History.IsDirty`. Reports has no record.
    - **Restoring.** Back reopens that record through the screen's `Open*` method before showing the tab, as the links do, **unless the screen's form holds unsaved changes**. Then the screen shows as it is, so Back never throws away typed work. `_isRestoringHistory` keeps the move from being recorded as a new step.
    - **The keys.** They're on the window's `PreviewKeyDown`, so no grid or box takes them first. With Alt held, WPF reports the key as `Key.System`, so the handler checks `SystemKey`.
    - **The lists scroll to a record opened this way.** `ProductListCard`, `SubAssembliesView` and `MaterialListCard` each watch their grid's `SelectedItem`.
  - **Fixed along the way: Products listed a role's usages twice.** `LoadProductDetailAsync` set `EditingUsageRole`, whose subscription loads the usages, then also awaited its own load.
