Whenever the role actually changed (the first product picked each session, for example), both loads ran side by side and each added every row, which double-counted those costs in the summary. It now loads directly only when the role didn't change, and `LoadUsagesForRoleAsync` recomputes the summary itself. (The per-role loading itself went in the 2026-09-14 rebuild: every role's usages now load together, with the product.)
- **Embedded management panel for an editable child lookup** (`SubAssembliesView`, Phase 7): `SubAssemblyRole` is editable but has no screen of its own — it gets a small self-contained grid + form + New/Save/Delete panel living directly inside `SubAssembliesView`, with its own independent `SourceCache`, selection, and commands in `SubAssembliesViewModel` (not a separate tab, and not folded into the sub-assembly form itself). Use this shape for a future lookup that's editable but too small/tightly-coupled to its owning screen to deserve a top-level tab. **Since Phase 16 it sits inside a collapsed native `Expander`** (`IsExpanded="False"`) at the bottom of the screen's right rail, rather than an always-visible side panel — it's used rarely enough (editing categories/roles is occasional, not part of the main workflow) that keeping it collapsed by default gives the main list more room without hiding the feature. The native `Expander` is used rather than a Syncfusion accordion (`SfAccordion` would need its own package just for this), and since `ApplyThemeAsDefaultStyle` is off (see the accent-customization note above), the native `Expander` renders with the plain OS chrome rather than the theme's — functional, just not accent-styled, the same trade-off as the other native fallbacks (`CheckBox`, `ProgressBar`) noted elsewhere in this document.
  - **Superseded (2026-09-14): the Sub-Assemblies screen is gone**, since sub-assemblies are made inside the product that owns them (see the Domain Model). Roles moved to a **Roles** tab in the Settings dialog (`SubAssemblyRolesView`/`SubAssemblyRolesViewModel`: Name and Order, the same shape as the Categories tab — the form resets after Save and Delete, and a fresh one offers the next place in the order).
    - **The Roles tab's Product-owned checkbox** (2026-09-23): `SubAssemblyRolesViewModel.EditingIsProductOwned`, disabled for the Packaging role (`CanEditProductOwned`); a read-only `GridCheckBoxColumn` shows it in the list. It's WPF's own `CheckBox`, the app's first outside a grid: Syncfusion has none of its own outside its grids and ribbon, and its samples use WPF's, which the theme styles. Changing it on a saved role asks first (`RunWithConfirmationAsync`, `OwnershipChangeMessage`), saying how many sub-assemblies move; a rename alone doesn't ask.
  - **Since 2026-09-23 it lists every sub-assembly but products' own** (product-owned roles), which are edited in their products; the ones products saved are named after them. The Name column is 260 wide and Role 110 (210 and 160 before), the same 370 in all, with a tooltip for a name cut short.
  - **Restored (2026-09-15) for standalone sub-assemblies only** (`SubAssembliesView`/`SubAssembliesViewModel`, a tab and drawer item between Raw Materials and Products; the user chose standalone only over also listing products' own read-only). Roles stay in Settings. It follows today's conventions, not the old screen's:
    - A list card (fixed-width columns, so the Auto column fits them: Name and Role; until 2026-09-17 also Batch (g), Batch Cost and Used By, removed at the user's request, since the form shows all three for the selected one) beside one form: the title and New, Save and Delete; Name and Role beside the batch weight, batch cost and cost per g, worked out from the lines as they're typed; the lines table from the BOM card (`IngredientLineRow`, autocomplete, `GridEditCommit`, `AutoCompleteDropDown`); and Used By: one tile per linking product (2026-09-23, at the user's request; a comma-separated sentence before), in a `WrapPanel` with a fixed `ItemWidth` so the tiles line up as rows and columns (a long name wraps inside its tile, and the row's tiles share its height), over a caption saying they follow every change and block Delete. A tile looks like a file in File Explorer's icon view (an icon over the name, flat until hovered; the icon is a full-color cake, pastry or bread drawing when the product's category name says so, else the Products glyph — `ProductCategoryIconConverter`, 2026-09-23) and a click opens the product on the Products screen: `GetStandaloneLinkedByAsync` returns `LinkingProduct`s (id, name and category name), the tile runs `OpenProductCommand`, `MainWindow` subscribes to `ProductOpenRequests` and calls `ProductsViewModel.OpenProduct`, which clears a search hiding the product, or, when the list hasn't loaded it yet, leaves it for the load that showing the tab starts. `ProductListCard` scrolls the list to a product selected this way. The request is a `Subject`, not the command's own results, because those arrive on the main-thread scheduler, which never runs in the tests.
    - **One Save** (`SaveStandaloneAsync`): `SubAssemblyValidator`, then `IngredientLineRow.CompleteLines` and at least one line; then `LoadCommand`, the saved row re-selected inside `ApplyRefresh` and its form reloaded directly, and a success toast. Delete asks first and shows the refusal naming the linking products.
    - Rows are `StandaloneSubAssemblyRow`s: "Used by" is a text property (`UsedByText`), with the names behind a method, because `CollectionMerge.HasSameValues` compares a list of strings by each string's scalar properties, which is only `Length`.
    - `LoadCommand` merges roles, ingredients and rows, keeps unsaved lines (`IngredientLineRow.Resolve` reprices them), and re-points the selection by id. A form load counts itself (`_formLoadVersion`), so one overtaken by another pick or New drops its lines rather than adding them twice.
    - Covered by `StandaloneSubAssemblyPersistenceTests`, `SubAssembliesViewModelRefreshTests` and a `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests` test that links one.
    - **Packaging mode** (2026-09-18): picking the Packaging role (`IsPackaging`, following `EditingRole`) swaps the ingredient table, its percentage caption and the batch totals for a packaging table with the Products Packaging tab's columns — Material, Qty, Unit, Base Qty, Unit Cost and **Cost per Set** — and a **Set Cost** total. Its rows are the same `PackagingLineRow`s (`PackagingLines`, `NewPackagingLine`, `CompleteLines(rows, "set")`), and Save goes to `SaveStandalonePackagingAsync`. A form loads whichever kind of lines its role says; Remove Line acts on the table showing. Covered by `PackagingSetPersistenceTests` and a `SubAssembliesViewModelRefreshTests` round trip.
    - **Verified in the app** on the secondary monitor against a migrated copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo`, with real clicks and keystrokes. Butter Glaze (Finishing: Butter Icing 150 g, Cornstarch 50 g, 200 g, ₱117.16) was typed in and saved with no owner. Cream Loaf linked it at 5 g a piece: a link-glyph tab with a "From Sub-Assemblies" badge, ₱2.9290 a piece, material cost ₱23.2754 → ₱26.2044, then Save. Raising Butter Icing to 250 g on Sub-Assemblies showed "Used By: Cream Loaf" and reached Cream Loaf (300 g batch, ₱3.2277, ₱26.5031). Delete was refused naming Cream Loaf. Clone copied Cream Loaf's four own sub-assemblies and kept Butter Glaze linked at 5 g. Copy mode on Cream Loaf Mini filled in its name, role and lines.
  - **The same shape was reused for `IngredientCategory` (in `IngredientsView`) and `PackagingCategory` (in `PackagingMaterialsView`)** once the user asked for a way to add/edit/remove categories, which neither had before (both were originally served only by the read-only, generic `ILookupRepository<T>`). Each got its own dedicated `IIngredientCategoryRepository`/`IPackagingCategoryRepository` (full CRUD, mirroring `ISubAssemblyRoleRepository`'s shape exactly: `GetAllAsync` ordered by name, `AddAsync`, `UpdateAsync` throwing `InvalidOperationException` for a missing id, `DeleteAsync` via `ExecuteDeleteAsync` relying on the existing `Restrict` FK from `Ingredient`/`PackagingMaterial` to block deleting a category still in use) plus a small `IngredientCategoryValidator`/`PackagingCategoryValidator` (Name required, matching `SubAssemblyRoleValidator`).
    - **Superseded (2026-09-13): one category list, in the Settings dialog.** The raw materials merge made the two category tables one (`RawMaterialCategory`, with `IRawMaterialCategoryRepository`/`RawMaterialCategoryValidator`), and the embedded panels left both screens — see the Settings dialog note below.
  - **The existing Category picker (`ComboBoxAdv`, already used on both screens' own edit forms) became searchable in the same change**, via `IsTextSearchEnabled="True"` and `AutoCompleteMode="Suggest"` — confirmed by reflecting over the built `Syncfusion.Shared.WPF.dll` assembly (the same way `DateTimeEdit`'s shape was confirmed in Phase 3) rather than guessing, after the user's initial ask named `SfTextBoxExt` for this, which reflection showed has no search/suggestion capability at all (it's a plain enhanced textbox). The picker deliberately stays **search-only**, not editable: typing a name that isn't in the list never auto-creates a category from the picker itself (`IsEditable` was left at its default `false`) — an explicit user choice to keep category creation in one place (the management panel above), rather than risk a typo silently creating a near-duplicate category from an edit form.
    - **Superseded: every picker is now editable and filters as you type, by default**, at the user's request.
      - `App`'s static constructor registers a class handler on `ComboBoxAdv`'s `Loaded` event (`ApplyPickerDefaults`). It sets `IsEditable=True` and `AutoCompleteMode=Suggest` on every `ComboBoxAdv` whose XAML doesn't set them itself. To opt a picker out, set `IsEditable="False"` on it in XAML.
      - A hook is needed because `ComboBoxAdv` owns both properties, so, unlike the `SfTextBoxExt` default above, `OverrideMetadata` can't change their defaults. A `Style` would replace the theme's, and a subclass risks losing the theme style, which is looked up by type.
      - `SetCurrentValue` keeps them as defaults, not local values, so a repeat `Loaded` reapplies them harmlessly.
      - Verified off-screen: a hook-configured picker matched one configured up front, with the same property values and one visible, writable text box.
      - Pickers no longer set `IsTextSearchEnabled`. The ComboBox skill says to pick it or Suggest, not both, and the Syncfusion AutoComplete sample needs `IsEditable` for Suggest to filter.
      - Typed text still can't create a category, because every picker's form binds only `SelectedItem`.
      - **The hook misses a picker whose panel is collapsed when its screen loads** (2026-09-22, at the user's request: "the combobox for selecting Existing Sub-Assembly or Packaging is not searchable"). `UsageFormPanel` is a `UserControl` inside a collapsed `Border` in `RecipeStructureCard`, so it is never measured with the screen, its template never applies, its content never joins the visual tree, and its `ComboBoxAdv`s never raise `Loaded` — not even once the form opens. Proved by logging every `ApplyPickerDefaults` call: the form's two pickers never appear, while every other picker does, collapsed ones (Linked Cost, Purchase Cost Setup) included. **So a picker in a panel like that spells `IsEditable="True"` and `AutoCompleteMode="Suggest"` out in XAML**, where they hold from the first template application. The form's Existing Sub-Assembly or Packaging and Role pickers both do.
      - **Text typed into a picker that matches no item stays in the box**, and survives loading another record: typing "zz" into Category leaves "zz" showing over a product whose category is Loaves. Only the box lies — the list column and what Save writes come from `SelectedItem`, which the text never touched. It is the Syncfusion control's own behaviour, on every picker the app has, not something the two pickers above brought in.
      - This includes the enum pickers with no `DisplayMemberPath` (Source, material kind, authored field, tab kind). They become editable too, although the skill says Suggest filters on `DisplayMemberPath`.
  - **`UnitOfMeasure` got the same "add a way to manage it" treatment next, but as a new top-level tab (`UnitsOfMeasureView`/`UnitsOfMeasureViewModel`), not an embedded panel** — unlike `IngredientCategory`/`PackagingCategory`, which each belong to exactly one screen, `UnitOfMeasure` is shared across Ingredients, Packaging Materials, and Products (purchase units, standard units, entry units), so no single existing screen was the natural home for it; PLAN.md's Domain Model section had already anticipated exactly this ("left for the shop owner to add once a Units of Measure management screen exists"). `IUnitOfMeasureRepository`/`UnitOfMeasureRepository` follows the same CRUD shape as the category repositories. This was also the point where `ILookupRepository<T>`/`LookupRepository<T>` were deleted outright: `UnitOfMeasure` was their last remaining consumer (after the category work above moved off it), and a zero-consumer generic abstraction is dead code, not a "just in case" — every `ILookupRepository<UnitOfMeasure>` constructor parameter across `IngredientsViewModel`/`PackagingMaterialsViewModel`/`ProductsViewModel` was replaced with `IUnitOfMeasureRepository` at the same time. The four `PurchaseUnits`/`StandardUnits`/`EntryUnitOptions` pickers across those three screens all got the same `IsTextSearchEnabled`/`AutoCompleteMode="Suggest"` treatment as the Category pickers, for the same reason.
    - **Superseded (2026-09-13): Units of Measure moved from a top-level tab into the Settings dialog**, next to Categories — see the Settings dialog note below.
  - **A unit's `Name` is always lowercase** (`UnitOfMeasureRules.NormalizeName`, an explicit user requirement added right after the screen shipped). First built as a rejection (`EnsureNameIsLowercase` throwing `InvalidOperationException` for "Bag"), then changed to **auto-correct** per explicit follow-up instruction ("autocorrect newly added unit of measure") — `UnitOfMeasureRepository.AddAsync`/`UpdateAsync` now silently lowercase the name before saving (`unitOfMeasure.Name = UnitOfMeasureRules.NormalizeName(unitOfMeasure.Name)`) instead of rejecting it, and the `UnitOfMeasureValidator`'s matching "must be lowercase" rule was removed accordingly — there's nothing left to reject, so nothing to show an error for. Saving "Bag" now just produces "bag" in the grid with no error message, matching how the name looked like it was going to behave the first time this was tried before the correction.
  - **The initial design seeded six fixed-id rows (Millimeter/Centimeter/Inch/Meter/Gram/Piece) and made the four length units permanently un-editable/un-deletable (`UnitOfMeasureRules.IsProtected`), because `LengthConversion`'s mm-per-unit factors were hardcoded in C# keyed to those exact ids. The user explicitly rejected both halves of that design** ("the six seed rows must be removed... I don't like the protection for that") **and asked for the conversion factor to live on the unit itself instead** — so `IsProtected`/`WellKnownUnitOfMeasureIds`/the `HasData` seed rows were removed altogether (a new migration deletes the six seeded rows), and `UnitOfMeasure` gained two real columns: `IsLengthUnit` (bool) and `MillimetersPerUnit` (decimal?, enforced by `UnitOfMeasureRules.EnsureLengthUnitFieldsAreConsistent` to be positive exactly when `IsLengthUnit` is true, null otherwise). `LengthConversion.Convert`/`ProductRules.EnsureValidEntryUnit`/`CalculatePackagingLineCostPerPiece` all now take full `UnitOfMeasure` entities instead of bare ids, reading `.IsLengthUnit`/`.MillimetersPerUnit` directly — nothing is special-cased by id anywhere anymore, so a shop owner's own custom length unit (say, a "foot") works identically to a built-in one the moment they tick the checkbox and enter its factor. `PackagingMaterialRules.IsValidStandardUnit`/`EnsureValidStandardUnit` ("must be Piece or a length unit") were dropped for the same reason — "Piece" was never a real concept once nothing was seeded, so there was no principled way left to restrict what a standard unit could be; a packaging material's standard unit is now trusted the same way an ingredient's purchase unit always was. `PackagingLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` was updated to load full `UnitOfMeasure` rows (for both the material's standard unit and each line's entry unit) instead of bare ids, which also meant adding an entry-unit-existence check it had never had before (mirroring the delete-before-validate fix from the Phase 14 audit) and a `.ThenInclude(m => m!.StandardUnit)` on the two `PackagingLineRepository` read queries so `PackagingLineEdit.CostPerPiece`/`ReportsViewModel`'s margin calculation have the material's standard unit entity already loaded, not just its id.
    - **Superseded (2026-09-13): the length-unit concept was removed entirely**, at the user's request, ahead of multi-unit features for ingredients and packaging materials. Gone: `IsLengthUnit`, `MillimetersPerUnit`, `LengthConversion`, `UnitOfMeasureRules.EnsureLengthUnitFieldsAreConsistent`, `ProductRules.EnsureValidEntryUnit`, `PackagingLine.EntryUnitId` and the Products screen's Entry Unit picker. `CalculatePackagingLineCostPerPiece` takes no units, and `IPackagingLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` tuples are `(PackagingMaterialId, AuthoredField, AuthoredValue)`. The Units of Measure tab (in Settings since 2026-09-13) edits Name and Abbreviation only.
      - Migration `DropLengthUnitsAndPackagingLineEntryUnit` has hand-added SQL (the first hand edit to a generated migration; the multi-unit migrations followed — see the migrations note below): `Up` converts any line entered in another length unit to its material's standard unit before the factors are dropped, and `Down` sets each restored `EntryUnitId` to the material's standard unit so the FK can be re-created. Both were verified on a scratch database (5 in → 12.7 cm, 80 mm → 8 cm, reciprocals converted inversely, a piece line untouched).
  - **`UnitOfMeasure` losing its seed data meant `DatabaseFixture` no longer needed a special case for it.** It previously excluded `UnitsOfMeasure` from Respawn's per-test reset specifically because the migration-seeded rows 1–6 were baseline data every other persistence test's FK depended on (Respawn can only "delete everything" or "touch nothing," no partial reset) — with no seed data left, that whole justification disappeared, so the exclusion (and the extra manual cleanup query it needed once `UnitOfMeasureRepository.AddAsync`/`DeleteAsync` existed) was simply removed, and `UnitsOfMeasure` now resets exactly like every other table. Every persistence test that referenced a `WellKnownUnitOfMeasureIds` constant as an FK value now seeds its own unit first via one of two new `PersistenceTestBase` helpers: `SeedUnitOfMeasureAsync` (always inserts a fresh row — for a test that specifically needs a distinct new unit) or `GetOrCreateUnitOfMeasureAsync` (looks up an existing row by name first — for a test's shared per-file seed helper that gets called multiple times in one test method with "any valid unit," where a second unconditional insert would violate the unique `Name` index).
  - **A `TabControlExt`'s inactive tab pages stay fully materialized in the UI Automation tree, not just the visible one** — confirmed the hard way while verifying this feature: a UI Automation query for "the first/last button named 'New'" (or "the first two Edit controls") across the *whole window*, run without scoping to the currently-visible tab's subtree, can silently match a control that belongs to a completely different, invisible tab page. Zero break-on-first-match logic combined with an index-based `.Item(0)`/`.Item(1)` guess landed on the wrong screen's controls one turn later even though the intended screen's own action had already appeared to work correctly moments earlier in the same session — the fix is to always scope a `FindFirst`/`FindAll` call to the specific View's own root automation element (e.g. found via its own distinctive, screen-unique control first), never to `AutomationElement.FromHandle(hwnd)` directly, when more than one tab's content could contain a same-named control.
  - **Syncfusion's number boxes take Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y for themselves, on the way down** (found 2026-09-20, building undo). `Syncfusion.Windows.Shared.DoubleTextBox.OnPreviewKeyDown` — and `CurrencyTextBox`'s — handles both keys for its own per-character undo and sets `e.Handled = true` on **every** branch, including when `IsUndoEnabled` is false. Because that's the tunnelling pass, the bubbling `KeyDown` never runs, so a `Window.InputBindings` Ctrl+Z is dead in every numeric grid cell and numeric field — most of the app. **The fix is a class handler on `typeof(Window)` for `PreviewKeyDown`** (`Controls/UndoShortcuts`, registered from `App`'s static constructor beside the three existing class handlers): tunnelling runs root to source, so the window sees the key before the focused box can claim it. The screen is found by walking up from `Keyboard.FocusedElement` to the first `DataContext` that is an `IUndoScreen`, which needs no registry and leaves Settings and the prompt window alone. Verified in the app: Ctrl+Z pressed with the cursor inside a `DoubleTextBox` took the change back rather than a character.
  - **A driving script must make its own thread per-monitor DPI aware, or its clicks miss** (found 2026-09-20 while verifying Products' Revert). UI Automation's `BoundingRectangle` is always in physical pixels, but `GetWindowRect`, `SetCursorPos` and `Graphics.CopyFromScreen` in a non-DPI-aware PowerShell host speak the *virtualized* coordinates Windows makes up for it — on this machine the primary is 125%, so the two spaces differ by 0.8 and a click computed from a screenshot landed hundreds of pixels away, silently selecting nothing. `SetProcessDpiAwarenessContext` is no use here: it only works before the process has done any DPI-dependent work, which a started PowerShell already has. **`SetThreadDpiAwarenessContext(-4)` (`PER_MONITOR_AWARE_V2`) works at any time** and brings all four into the same physical-pixel space; check it took by confirming `GetWindowRect` and the root element's `BoundingRectangle` agree before clicking anything.
- **A "replace-all" child collection editor** (`SubAssemblyLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`, following `IngredientBomLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` from Phase 6, and now also `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.SetUsagesForRoleAsync`/`PackagingLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` from Phase 8): the UI builds up an in-memory list of lines (here, `SubAssemblyLineEdit` — an ingredient plus a quantity, not yet saved) via Add/Remove commands, and a single Save command sends the *whole* list to the repository, which deletes the existing rows for that parent and inserts the new set, then (where relevant) recomputes and stores the roll-up totals, and saves once. Simpler and less error-prone than diffing an edited collection against the database, and matches how a recipe is actually edited by a person (the whole line list at once, not one row at a time). **Delete the old rows with `ExecuteDeleteAsync`, not `ToListAsync` + `RemoveRange`** — every row is being unconditionally discarded, never mutated first, so loading them into memory first (found during an EF Core query review across all four of these repositories) buys nothing but an extra round trip and change-tracking overhead. `ExecuteDeleteAsync` executes immediately against the database, so call it before the `AddRange`/`SaveChangesAsync` that follows, same ordering as the old load-and-remove version.
  - **"Replace the whole set" had to become "replace one role's slice of the set" once tabs needed independent per-role linking** (Phase 10): `SetUsagesAsync(productId, usages)` replacing *every* usage on a product in one call was fine through Phase 9, but Phase 10's per-tab Live Template links mean one role's usages can be edited while another role is linked (and must be left alone) or vice versa. Renamed to `SetUsagesForRoleAsync(productId, subAssemblyRoleId, usages)`, scoped to just that role's rows (`Where(u => u.ProductId == productId && subAssemblyIdsInThisRole.Contains(u.SubAssemblyId))` before the delete, not a blind `Where(u => u.ProductId == productId)`). This also meant `ProductRules.EnsureValidUsages`'s "a product needs at least one usage" check had to be **dropped** — enforced per-role-scoped call, it would wrongly reject a perfectly valid product that just has zero usages in one particular role. The lesson generalizes: a "replace everything for this parent" method's scope has to match whatever the UI's unit of independent editing actually is, and that unit can get finer as later phases add more granular concepts (roles/tabs here) on top of an earlier, coarser one.
    - **And coarser again (2026-09-14):** with role-tab links gone, the unit of editing is the whole recipe. `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.SetRecipeAsync(dbContext, productId, RecipeDraft)` (called by `ProductRepository.SaveAsync` and `ProductCloner`, inside their transactions) replaces all of a product's usages, and adds, updates, relines or deletes its own sub-assemblies to match. A `RecipeDraft` is the product's own sub-assemblies (`OwnedSubAssemblyDraft`: id or 0, name, role, gram lines, piece size — an owned one always carries its owner's usage, so there's no separate id to match a new one by) and the ones it links (`LinkedUsageDraft`). Every check runs before anything is deleted: names, lines, piece sizes, roles, that listed ids are the product's own and linked ones another's, and that no sub-assembly being dropped is linked elsewhere. Then: the usages go (`ExecuteDeleteAsync`), then the dropped sub-assemblies (their lines cascade), then kept ones are updated before new ones are added (so a new one can take a name a kept one gave up), each relined through `SubAssemblyLineRepository.SetLinesAsync(dbContext, subAssembly, lines)`, which recomputes its totals. The public single-purpose `SetLinesAsync`/`SetUsagesForRoleAsync` and the sub-assembly add/update/delete went with their screen.
- **A repository that only ever needs the single "latest" row from a growing history table should query for just that row, not load the whole table** (`IngredientPriceHistoryRepository`/`PackagingPriceHistoryRepository`'s `AddAsync`/`UpdateAsync`/`DeleteAsync`, fixed during the same query review): `PriceHistoryRules.FindLatest` picks one row by `(Date desc, Id desc)`, but the original code loaded every price-history entry for the ingredient/material to run that pick in C# — a table that only grows over the life of the app, reloaded in full on every single price edit. Fixed by querying `OrderByDescending(Date).ThenByDescending(Id).FirstOrDefaultAsync()` for the current latest row (already well-served by the existing `HasIndex(IngredientId, Date)` composite index) and, where a new/updated in-memory candidate also needs to be weighed against it, running `FindLatest` over just that 1-2-item set — mathematically identical to running it over the full list, since the max of a set unioned with one more candidate equals the max of (the set's own max, that candidate). (Superseded 2026-09-18: price history no longer sets the cost, so none of these queries remain; the lesson stands.)
- **Derived values are computed on read, never stored, until a phase actually needs to persist them** (Phase 8): `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` stores only `WeightPerPiece` (since 2026-09-14, only the typed figure — weight per piece or yield — and the other is computed too); its `Yield` and cost-per-piece, and `PackagingLine`'s cost-per-piece, and `Product`'s total material cost, are all plain `ProductRules`/`CostingRules` functions called fresh every time a screen needs them (`ProductsViewModel.RecomputeSummary`), not columns kept in sync by a propagator. This is deliberately simpler than Phase 6/7's stored-and-propagated `CostPerGram`/`TotalCost`: nothing yet depends on *Product's* cost the way `SubAssembly` depends on `Ingredient.CostPerGram`, and wiring a real propagator here would be building Phase 11 early. Revisit when Phase 11 wires the full cascade — at that point a `RawMaterialPriceHistory` change needs to reach a product's displayed price without the user reopening the screen, which read-on-demand doesn't do on its own.
- **Cost-plus pricing, not gross-margin-on-price** (`CostingRules.CalculateSellingPrice`, Phase 9, an explicit user decision over the gross-margin alternative): `BaseCost = SubAssemblyCost + PackagingCost + Labor + Overhead + Administrative` (the administrative cost joined on 2026-09-14), where each of those three is its percentage of `SubAssemblyCost` — **the sub-assemblies alone, not the packaging, since 2026-09-20** (they were percentages of the whole material cost from 2026-09-15 to then). That's why `CalculateSellingPrice` takes the sub-assembly cost and the packaging cost as **two separate parameters** rather than one material cost: only the first is the base for the three percentages, and both go into the base cost. It takes the percentages and works the pesos out itself, so every caller prices the same way. `PreVatPrice = BaseCost × (1 + Margin%/100)`, `SellingPrice = PreVatPrice × (1 + VAT%/100)` — three separate steps, never folded into one formula, so a shop owner can audit each input by hand. Two informational helpers sit beside it: `PercentOf(part, whole)` (0 when the whole is 0), for every "% of the price" on the Products screen, and `CalculateProfitMarginPercent(baseCost, preVatPrice)`, the markup's share of the pre-VAT price, since VAT goes to the government (a 30% markup is a 23.08% margin). Neither changes the price. If a future phase ever needs gross-margin pricing (margin as a % of the *final* price rather than a markup on cost), that's a different formula (`price = cost / (1 − margin%)`) and a different, explicitly-chosen mode — don't quietly swap the math in `CalculateSellingPrice`.
- **A full-precision in-memory reciprocal (e.g. `1m / 6m`) gets rounded to 4 places on its round trip through a `decimal(18,4)` column** (found by a failing Phase 8 test asserting the untruncated value): `PackagingLine.QtyPerPiece`/`PiecesPerPackagingUnit` are exact reciprocals in `ProductRules.ReconcileReciprocal`'s C# math, but SQL Server's `decimal(18,4)` (the app-wide convention from Phase 1) stores `0.1666666...` as `0.1667`. Expected and harmless for money-scale quantities, but any test asserting an exact reciprocal against data that's been through the database needs to assert the *rounded* value, not the raw division result.
- **A guarded field on an existing repository can be tightened once a later phase adds the FK that makes the guard meaningful, without that counting as "jumping ahead.**" `SubAssemblyRepository.UpdateAsync` left `RoleId` fully editable in Phase 7 because nothing referenced a sub-assembly yet; Phase 8 added `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` with a Restrict FK to `SubAssembly`, so `UpdateAsync` now blocks a role change (`InvalidOperationException`) whenever any `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` row references that sub-assembly, matching the Domain Model's "a sub-assembly's role can only be changed while no product uses it." Prefer this — tightening the one repository method whose precondition changed — over a broad revalidation pass across every earlier phase's code every time a new phase adds a relationship.
  - **The role lock itself went on 2026-09-14**: it existed only because a role change broke role-tab links, and those are gone. An owner can change its sub-assembly's role whenever it likes.
- **A "linked tab reads through to another product" feature resolves recursively at read time, with a visited-set guard, rather than eagerly propagating data on write** (`ProductTabLink`, Phase 10): `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.GetEffectiveForRoleAsync`/`PackagingLineRepository.GetEffectiveForProductAsync` follow a tab's `ProductTabLink` chain (if any) to whichever product's tab is actually unlinked, and return *that* tab's own rows — recursing through however many hops a chain of derived products has, not just one. `SetLinkAsync` separately rejects a cycle at write time (`ProductTabLinkRules.EnsureNoCycle`, loading only the other links for that exact tab identity into a dictionary first, mirroring `IngredientBomLineRepository`'s cycle-check shape from Phase 6), but the read path still carries its own `HashSet<int>` of visited product ids as defense in depth — cheap insurance against a cycle slipping in through a future write path that forgets the check, or a manual data edit. **Un-linking a tab materializes its current effective read into owned rows before removing the link row** (`ProductTabLinkRepository.RemoveLinkAsync`) — read the effective data while the link still resolves, delete the link and save, *then* write the materialized rows (which is only accepted once the tab no longer shows as linked) — so a product never momentarily shows less than it did the moment before.
  - **SQL Server needs `SET QUOTED_IDENTIFIER ON` for any INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE against a table with a filtered index** (`ProductTabLinks`, which has two — see its `HasFilter` unique indexes below) — this is a session-level setting, and `sqlcmd`'s default session has it off, so a raw `sqlcmd -Q "INSERT INTO ProductTabLinks ..."` fails with `Msg 1934`. EF Core's own connections already set this correctly, so the app itself is never affected — only ad-hoc `sqlcmd` seeding/cleanup against this table needs `SET QUOTED_IDENTIFIER ON;` prepended.
  - **A nullable column that should behave as "exactly one row per non-null group, plus at most one row where it's null" needs two filtered unique indexes, not one plain one** (`ProductTabLinkConfiguration`): SQL Server's default unique index treats every `NULL` as distinct from every other `NULL`, so a single `HasIndex(ProductId, SubAssemblyRoleId).IsUnique()` would let a product accumulate unlimited Packaging-tab links (all `SubAssemblyRoleId = NULL`, never colliding). Split into `HasIndex(ProductId, SubAssemblyRoleId).IsUnique().HasFilter("[SubAssemblyRoleId] IS NOT NULL")` for the many role-tab rows and `HasIndex(ProductId).IsUnique().HasFilter("[SubAssemblyRoleId] IS NULL")` for the at-most-one Packaging-tab row.
  - **Superseded (2026-09-14): only packaging links are left**, as `PackagingLink` (table `PackagingLinks`: `ProductId`, plainly unique, and `LinkedProductId`), with `IPackagingLinkRepository`/`PackagingLinkRepository` and `PackagingLinkRules` (renamed from `ProductTabLinkRules`). The read-through (`PackagingLineRepository.ResolveEffectiveAsync`), the visited-set guard, the write-time cycle check and the materialize-then-unlink order are the same; the role-tab resolution (`GetEffectiveForRoleAsync`/`GetEffectiveForProductAsync` on usages), `ProductTabKind` and the filtered indexes went.
  - **`ObservableAsPropertyHelper` (`ToProperty`) and manually calling `this.RaisePropertyChanged(nameof(...))` on that same property don't mix** — a real bug caught before it shipped in this phase: `IsSelectedRoleLinked`/`IsPackagingLinked` were first wired as `ToProperty` outputs of a `WhenAnyValue` pipeline, but their actual source of truth (whether `TabLinks`, a plain `ObservableCollection`, contains a matching link) only changes via imperative `Clear()`/`AddRange()` calls in `LoadProductDetailAsync`/`LoadUsagesForRoleAsync` — nothing `WhenAnyValue` can observe. Calling `RaisePropertyChanged` on an OAPH-backed property doesn't recompute it; the `Value` a bound control reads back is still whatever the last `WhenAnyValue` push produced (in this case, computed exactly once at construction and never again). Fixed by making both plain `RaiseAndSetIfChanged` properties recomputed by an explicit `RefreshLinkedFlags()` call at the same two sites. General rule: reach for `ToProperty` only when every input is itself a bindable/observable property; when the real driver is an imperatively-mutated collection, use a plain property and an explicit recompute call instead.
- **Every screen was verified by actually running the app**, not just building it or passing tests: launch `dotnet run --project src/TinapayanRMS.UI`, screenshot the running window, and where practical seed real rows via `sqlcmd` first so the screenshot proves the data-bound path (grid rows, selected-row-into-form binding, dropdown selection) and not just empty-state layout. Clean up any rows seeded this way afterward — the dev `TinapayanRMS` database should hold only what the user actually entered.
  - **Bringing the window to front needs `AttachThreadInput` around `SetForegroundWindow`, not just `SetWindowPos`-with-`HWND_TOPMOST`.** The plain topmost trick (recorded here through Phase 6) stopped reliably working in Phase 7: `SetWindowPos(hwnd, HWND_TOPMOST, ...)` would report success, but mouse clicks at the expected screen coordinates still landed on the foreground terminal window underneath, confirmed with `WindowFromPoint`. The fix that actually works: attach the calling thread's input queue to the current foreground window's thread (`AttachThreadInput(myThreadId, fgThreadId, true)`), then `ShowWindow`/`BringWindowToTop`/`SetForegroundWindow` on the target, all inside the *same* tool invocation as the click that follows (a separate tool call in between gives the terminal a chance to reclaim focus) — then detach. Verify with `WindowFromPoint` before clicking, not after. `PrintWindow` (not `CopyFromScreen`) remains the right way to capture the screenshot itself, since it captures a window's content directly regardless of what's on top of it on screen — it's specifically mouse input, not rendering, that needs true foreground focus.
  - **Even `AttachThreadInput` can't win against a real foreground app that keeps re-asserting focus** (seen in Phase 8: a game the user had open, not the terminal this time, stayed foreground through repeated retries, confirmed each time with `WindowFromPoint`). When that happens, stop retrying rather than keep fighting for focus — it means the user is actively doing something else on the machine. Fall back to whatever partial screenshot evidence was already captured (e.g. a new tab visible in the tab strip) plus the integration test suite and a manual line-by-line binding review (every `x:Path` in the view's XAML checked against the ViewModel's actual property names/types) as the verification of record for that screen.
  - **Synthetic click/type automation (`SetCursorPos` + `mouse_event` + `SendKeys`) against Syncfusion's custom input controls is unreliable** — coordinates that look right against a screenshot don't reliably focus/type into a `DoubleTextBox` (tried once, Phase 5, no app error, the value just never landed). Don't burn time chasing exact coordinates. The read/render/selection path (data flowing into the grid and the selected-row-into-form binding) is what screenshots verify well; for write paths (a button's command), rely on the repository/rules integration tests exercising the exact same call chain the command invokes, plus a code review of the binding — the same depth `New`/`Save`/`Delete` got in Phase 4.
    - **A working exception, found later (verifying the length-unit conversion-factor field): UI Automation's `ValuePattern.SetValue(...)` sets a `DoubleTextBox`'s displayed text but does not commit it to the bound property** — a save attempted right after `SetValue` failed FluentValidation as if the field were still empty, even though the screenshot showed the typed value. The fix: physically move the cursor into the field and click it first (real focus, not just an Automation reference to the control), send the digits through `keybd_event` as real keystrokes, then send a real Tab keystroke to force the focus-loss that commits the WPF binding — only then does `Value` update. (Since 2026-09-13 every editable `DoubleTextBox` binds with `UpdateSourceTrigger=PropertyChanged`, so real keystrokes commit as they're typed and the Tab is no longer needed; `SetValue` still isn't enough.) `ValuePattern.SetValue` alone is fine for a plain `SfTextBoxExt`/`Text` binding (used successfully throughout Phases 13-14's verification) but isn't sufficient for a `DoubleTextBox`'s `Value`; when a save silently fails validation right after an automation-driven numeric-field edit, suspect this before suspecting the validation rule itself.
    - **Picking a `ComboBoxAdv` item needs real clicks too** (verifying the one Save, 2026-09-13). Its items expose no SelectionItem pattern, and until the dropdown opens they carry no text (their name is the item's type name).
      - Open the dropdown with a real click near the picker's right edge.
      - Then find the `ComboBoxItemAdv` whose Text child matches, searching under the desktop root filtered by the app's process id (the dropdown is a separate window), and click it.
      - `ButtonAdv` has no Invoke pattern, so buttons need real clicks as well.
      - Look labels up inside the panel being filled, not the whole view: a grid column header ("Category", "Component") can share a form label's text.
      - Type into a `DoubleTextBox` without Tab when the next control is an editable picker. Tab opens the picker's dropdown, which swallows the next click.
      - When the item's position is known, the keyboard is enough (verifying the Products screen, 2026-09-14): a click on the picker didn't open its dropdown but gave it focus, and each Down press picked the next item.
  - **Make the script DPI-aware before placing, clicking or capturing** (2026-09-14). The primary monitor is at 125% and the secondary (1,366 × 768) at 100%. A DPI-unaware PowerShell gets scaled window rects and captures, and WPF resizes a window moved onto the other monitor: a 1,400 × 800 `SetWindowPos` landed at 1,120 × 640. Call `SetThreadDpiAwarenessContext(-4)` (per-monitor v2) first, and set the size again once the window is on the secondary monitor. 1,400 × 800 doesn't fit there, so check the default layout maximized (1,366 × 720).
  - **`SfDataGrid` sometimes renders only its first few columns when screenshotted via `PrintWindow` right after a tab switch or fresh data load** (seen in Phase 7's Sub-Assemblies grid, and on closer inspection, in the Phase 4 Ingredients grid too, so it predates Phase 7 and isn't a regression from it) — the grid's rendered width shrinks to fit only the columns it managed to realize rather than the full `*`-allocated Grid column width, with no visual trace (no header, no border) of the missing columns. This looks like a virtualization/layout-timing artifact of capturing a hardware-accelerated custom control via GDI shortly after it's populated, not a real data or binding problem — the same ViewModel data (e.g. a seeded sub-assembly's `Role`/`TotalWeight`/`TotalCost`) round-trips correctly through the integration tests. Don't chase this pixel-by-pixel; treat a screenshot showing at least the grid's leading column(s) with real seeded data, plus a passing integration-test suite covering every column's underlying property, as sufficient verification.
- **A value that can never be typed is shown in a `TextBlock`, not a read-only text box** (2026-09-19, found by a CPU trace of the Products screen). WPF connects every `TextBox` (and so every `SfTextBoxExt`, `DoubleTextBox`, …) to Windows' Text Services Framework after its first layout, whatever its `Focusable` or `IsReadOnly` (`TextEditor.InitTextStore`, confirmed by decompiling PresentationFramework 10.0.8), and there's no per-control opt-out. Each change of its text then calls `TextStore.RequestLock`, which waits on text services through COM: the Products form's Total Weight (`UsageFormTotalWeightText`, a read-only unfocusable `SfTextBoxExt`) cost about 115 ms of UI-thread waiting per product picked on the dev machine, 31% of the redraw, and the wait depends on the text services active (input methods, touch keyboard, dictation, assistive tools), which likely explains 1.76 s freezes seen on the user's own run. It's now a `Border` with `ValueBoxStyle` (`UsageFormPanel.xaml`: the theme's text box drawn by hand — 24 px, corners of 4, `#F1F1F1` with a `#8E8E8E` bottom pixel via the theme's flipped 2 px gradient, text `#1B1B1B` inset 6 px) holding a `TextBlock`. This is for a value that is *never* typed; a box that is only sometimes read-only stays a text box, greyed as below. Linked Cost's always-read-only inherited Cost Buffer % (`LinkedCostCard`) is the one other such box, on Raw Materials, not changed.
- **Result of both 2026-09-19 fixes** (this and the recipe tabs' one shared form): the UI thread's busy time over the same ten product clicks went from 3,767 ms to 970 ms (about 377 → 97 ms a click), measured with `dotnet-trace` on the dev database.
- **Picking another product swaps its details in one pass, with no empty product between** (2026-09-19, at the user's request: "why does the UI flicker between clicks in the product list?"). `ResetDetails` used to clear the recipe, packaging and costing the moment a product was picked, and the screen drew that empty product (no recipe tabs, ₱0.0000, the overviews' empty states) until the new details arrived, with the recipe's frame collapsing and growing back. Now, for another product, it only sets `AreDetailsReady` to false and leaves the previous product's details showing; `LoadProductDetailAsync` clears them (`ClearDetailsForAnother`) and applies the new ones in the same dispatcher turn, so nothing is drawn in between, then sets it back. Picking none (New) still clears at once, and picking the same product again still keeps its details until the reload. If the reads fail, the previous product's details are cleared rather than left under the new name. While `AreDetailsReady` is false (a fraction of a second) the details aren't the form's any more: the details tabs ignore the mouse (`IsHitTestVisible`, no visual change), and Save returns without saving, since it would write the previous product's recipe under the picked one's id and name. Save has no `canExecute` for it on purpose: the button would grey for that moment on every pick, a flicker of its own. Measured by sampling the recipe tab strip's pixel every ~16 ms for 800 ms after each of six clicks: the previous commit lost it for 300–450 ms a click, this build never. Test: `Picking_another_product_keeps_the_previous_details_until_its_own_arrive_and_Save_waits_for_them`.
  - **Another product's sections take over the recipe's tabs in place** (2026-09-19, at the user's request after a UI performance review: a CPU trace of product picks put a fifth of the UI thread's time in the recipe's tab strip). `ClearDetailsForAnother` used to empty `UsageEdits`, which took every tab away, and `ReplaceUsages` then added a new `SubAssemblyRecipeTab` per section, so `TabControlExt` built a new `TabItemExt`, template and all, for every tab on every pick (`TabLayoutPanel.MeasureOverride` → `ApplyTemplate`), and dropped and re-added Packaging's as well. Now the previous recipe's tabs stay (`ClearDetailsForAnother(keepRecipeTabs: true)`), and `ReplaceUsages` replaces the sections index for index (`MirrorUsageTabs`' Replace sets the tab's `Usage`, and its header and glyph follow), adding or removing only the difference; Packaging's tab stays when both products have packaging. The form is closed first, so no tab carries the previous product's form over: the recipe still opens on its first tab, on the picked product's section. A read that fails still clears everything (`keepRecipeTabs: false`). Measured off-screen against the dev database, six interleaved runs of 24 Recipe-tab clicks each: UI-thread time per click 86.6 → 57.2 ms (median of the runs' medians), tab-strip changes per click 3.3 → 1.3. Test: `Picking_another_product_keeps_the_recipe_tabs_and_opens_the_form_on_its_first_section` (fails on the old code). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved: Almond Cheese Bread (linked Dough, Topping, Filling, Packaging), then Cheese Roll (its own Dough and Packaging: the first tab's glyph turned from link to list, and two tabs went), Pan Bread, All Purpose (Dough alone: Packaging went too), Almond Cheese Bread again (all four back), and Cheese Roll picked while Almond Cheese Bread's Filling showed (it opened on its Dough, with its own form).
- **Picking another raw material swaps its details in one pass too, and a quick second pick no longer mixes two materials' details** (2026-09-19, at the user's request, found by an off-screen probe during the same review). A material's price history, brand prices and BOM were three loads, each clearing its grid at once and adding what it read when the read came back, with no cancellation (`Observable.FromAsync(() => …)`), so a second pick before the first one's reads returned got both. On the dev database Butter Icing then Custard Filling showed 6 BOM lines instead of 2, and All Purpose Flour then Buttermilk Powder 5 brand prices instead of 2, even 2 ms apart. Save sends every brand row, so saving then would have written one material's base-unit prices onto the other; a Save in that moment sent none and deleted the material's own. Now it has the Products shape: one `LoadDetailsAsync` reads all three on the thread pool (`ReadDetailAsync`, in one `Task.Run`), a newer pick cancels it (`FromAsync(cancellationToken => …)` with `Switch`), and `ShowDetails` replaces the grids in one pass without clearing them first, so they don't draw empty in between. `AreDetailsReady` is false from picking another material until its details arrive: the details tabs ignore the mouse (`IsHitTestVisible`) and Save returns without saving. A new material's first Save isn't another material: `ReloadSavedRawMaterialAsync` sets `_detailMaterialId` before selecting it, as Products does. Delete Price still reloads the price history alone (`ReplacePriceHistory`). No cost to speak of: a click took 26.3 → 27.3 ms of UI-thread time (three interleaved runs); the mouse guard is about 4 ms of it, the same with a transparent overlay in its place, which was tried and dropped. Tests: `A_second_pick_before_the_first_ones_details_arrive_shows_only_its_own` (fails on the old code: Sugar showed Flour's brands too) and `Picking_another_material_keeps_the_previous_details_until_its_own_arrive_and_Save_waits_for_them`; the tests that pick a material and then edit or save it now wait for its details first (`SelectAsync`). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved: six Down presses in a row from All Purpose Flour left Buttermilk Powder's two brands alone, the Price History tab answered a click once they'd arrived, and Butter Icing then Custard Filling at once left Custard Filling's two lines.
- **A read-only number box greys its text** (2026-09-17, at the user's request): `controls:ReadOnlyText.IsGreyed`, bound to the same flag as the box's `IsReadOnly` (or `"True"` for one that always is), paints the text in `ReadOnlyForegroundBrush` (#A8A29E), so a value that can't be typed doesn't look typeable. Used by Linked Cost's inherited Cost Buffer %, and Purchase Cost Setup's Quantity per Purchase Unit (read-only for the base unit and a saved alternative unit, whose multiplier is edited in Alternative Units) and Cost Buffer ₱ (read-only until a price above 0 is entered). The user had it reverted once, then asked for it back once it was clear the grey says "edited elsewhere", not "broken".
  - A `DoubleTextBox` never draws its text with `Foreground`: a non-zero value uses `PositiveForeground`, and 0 uses `ZeroColor` only once `ApplyZeroColor` is on (the theme's template reads them; `EditorBase` decompiled, 34.2.7). So those three are set, and turning it off **clears** them rather than setting a colour back, which returns the theme's own text colour exactly — a converter binding would have had to know that colour. Verified in the app: Cost Buffer ₱ grey at 0.00 on a new material and black at ₱246.75 on a priced one, and a saved tray's 1,500.00 greyed.
- **One Raw Materials screen (`RawMaterialsView`/`RawMaterialsViewModel`), from the user's Ingredients mockup (2026-09-13): the list beside the selected material's cards.** Ingredients and Packaging Materials were two copies of this layout until the raw materials merge the same day; one screen now covers both, and a material's type decides which cards and fields apply.
  - **List card:** a search box (a DynamicData `Filter` on the cache, bound to `VisibleRawMaterials`; the unfiltered `RawMaterials` feeds the pickers, and `Ingredients` — the same cache filtered to the Ingredient type — feeds the BOM component picker). Columns: Name, Category, Cost and Buffered (fixed-width `NumericColumn`s on `CostPerBaseUnit` and `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit`, 4 decimals). **Fixed column widths** (2026-09-15, at the user's request; the same day a star-sized list with draggable, frozen columns was tried and reverted): Name is 215 px and Category 165 px (2026-09-19, at the user's request; they were 220 px, wide enough for 30 characters, and 185 px, for 25). The old widths were measured in the grid's font (Segoe UI, 12 px) against capitals, the widest case: WHOLE WHEAT FLOUR MEDIUM GRAIN is 211 px of text, and mixed-case names of 30 characters take about 180. A cell adds about 6 px of padding, and text past the width is cut off, not ellipsized. Cost and Buffered are 96 px each. The list's column in `RawMaterialsView` is `Auto`, so the card is exactly as wide as its columns (about 655 px) and the details take the rest: about 620 px at the default 1,400 px window, where the header's figure tiles trim, and about 770 px maximized on the 125% secondary. Cost is shown as the bare value everywhere on this screen — list, header tile, Purchase Cost Setup, Usage Example — never "₱0.0372 / g": the user found the unit suffix redundant, since the labels already say per base unit (`CostPerBaseUnitConverter` went with it). The Alt Units column is gone, at the user's request, since the Alternative Units card shows them (`UnitsSummaryConverter` went with the old packaging list).
    - **Superseded (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "Show Name and Category column to Raw Materials list", as the Products list does): the list's columns are just Name and Category.** Cost and Buffered went; a material's costs are in the header's Current Cost per Base Unit and Buffered Cost tiles. Name and Category keep their fixed widths, so the `Auto` card is 192 px narrower than before (about 465 px), and the details take the rest: checked in the app maximized on the 1,366 px secondary, where the header's tiles no longer trim.
  - **Rows are grouped by type** into collapsible "Ingredients (n)" and "Packaging (n)" sections (`RawMaterialGroupConverter`; `ShowColumnWhenGrouped="False"` hides the Type column the grouping keys on). **The grid also sorts by Name itself** (`SortColumnDescriptions`). Without that, a grouped `SfDataGrid` placed rows by its own order rather than the bound collection's, and the order within a group changed from run to run. That was confirmed by comparing two back-to-back loads (`CollectionMerge.HasSameValues` held for every row, so no refresh was replacing anything) and by three restarts after the fix.
    - **That sort only covered the first load: a row added or replaced while the app ran went to the end of its group** (found while verifying the one Save, below). A row inserted straight into the database and merged in by a tab refresh landed after "Zz Test Syrup" instead of in name order. **`LiveDataUpdateMode="AllowDataShaping"`** fixes it, as in Syncfusion's `AddNewRowDemo.xaml`: verified by inserting a row mid-session (it landed in order) and by renaming a material with Save (its row moved and stayed selected).
    - **Superseded (2026-09-17, at the user's request): grouping is gone.** The list is flat and just Name-sorted again; Ingredients and Packaging no longer collapse into their own sections. The Type column goes with it — it only ever existed for the grouping to key on (`ShowColumnWhenGrouped` hid it) — so the grid's columns were Name, Category, Cost and Buffered (since just Name and Category, see above), and `RawMaterialGroupConverter` is no longer referenced from the view (it stays in the codebase; `GridFillerRowsGroupedTests` still uses it to pin the crash fix below). `LiveDataUpdateMode="AllowDataShaping"` and the Name `SortColumnDescriptions` stay, for the same live-reorder reason as above.
  - **Summary header:** an initial-letter tile, the name as a big title, then tiles for Base Unit ("gram (g)" for an ingredient; a picker for packaging, disabled once anything is measured in it), Current Cost per Base Unit, Buffered Cost (both the saved values), and Last Updated (the latest price's date). New/Save/Delete sit top-right; Type, Category, Source (ingredients only — packaging is always purchased) and Weight Loss % (made only) below. Type and Source can only be picked for a new material. Switching a new one to Packaging resets Source to Purchased and clears the weight loss; switching it back clears the base unit. Switching an ingredient to Made starts its weight loss at 3% (0% until 2026-09-21), and an emptied box saves as 0% too, since the `DoubleTextBox` shows 0.00 when empty (at the user's request: the box showed 0.00, yet Save said the weight loss was required). Saving a new material selects it. **New starts with an empty form, as a purchased ingredient** (2026-09-15, at the user's request: it pre-filled the first category, the gram as purchase unit and the last material picked in the usage example). Name, Category, Purchase Unit and its Quantity per Purchase Unit, and the usage example's Material and unit start empty. Type and Source start as Ingredient and Purchased: they were emptied too at first, and the user asked for those two defaults back. The numbers keep a value: Purchase Price, Cost Buffer % and Cost Buffer show 0.00, and Use Quantity stays as it was. A price typed with no purchase unit is refused ("Pick the purchase unit the price is for.") rather than dropped. Quantity per Purchase Unit shows empty rather than 0.00 while it has no value (`UseNullOption`, as in Syncfusion's `DoubleTextBoxDemo.xaml`). Delete leaves the same form (`ClearForm`); it used to keep the deleted material's details and id in the form. A saved material still loads everything, its latest price's unit included.
    - **The header is sized like the Products one** (2026-09-21, at the user's request: "i want the Raw Materials Header is as compact as Products header"). Every measurement now matches `ProductHeaderCard`: the card's own `Padding="12"`, a 40px avatar with an 18px initial (was 52 and 24), a 20px title (was 24), tile values at 14px SemiBold (was 16, with the two cost tiles at 18), and `TileLabelStyle` back to `FieldLabelStyle`'s margin (was 6px). **The bordered box around the tiles is gone** — they're a plain `Grid` with `Margin="0,12,0,0"` and the separators between them, as the Products figures row is — which was the biggest part of the difference (12px padding top and bottom, a 1px border, and 4px more top margin). The header went from about 275px to about 215px, the same as Products'. Verified in the app for a made ingredient (six tiles) and a purchased one (four).
    - **A made ingredient's header carries two more tiles, Total Weight and Total Cost** (2026-09-21, at the user's request: "the card should also display the Total Weight g and Total Cost"), between Buffered Cost and Last Updated, so the figures the buffered cost is worked out from are on the screen: `BomTotalWeight` is the batch's yield (Σ BOM grams less the weight loss, from `IngredientBomRules.CalculateCost`, the same formula the cascade saves with) and `BomTotalCost` is `Σ IngredientLineRow.Cost` — the BOM grid's Cost column, at the components' buffered costs. **Both read the form**, so they follow the grid and the Weight Loss % box as they're typed into (`RecomputeBomTotals`, driven by a `BomLineEdits` change set on `Cost`, the `EditingWeightLossPercent` subscription, `ShowDetails`, the `LoadCommand` merge after `IngredientLineRow.Resolve`, and once at the end of `RestoreSnapshot` — the last two because a refresh and a restore suppress the change set). They aren't in the snapshot: they're derived, and adding them would make a recompute look like a user step. **So the Buffered Cost per g tile follows the form too for a made ingredient** (the user's call over keeping it strictly "as saved"): `UpdateHeaderCostTexts` divides the two tiles, which is exactly what Save then stores, so all three always agree while typing. Current Cost per g stays the saved true cost. **Every tile takes an equal share of the row** (2026-09-21, at the user's request: "Raw Materials tiles width are not distributed equally" — the two made-only ones were `Auto`, so they sized to their own text while the other four split the rest). Their columns bind `IsEditingMade` through `BooleanToStarWidthConverter`, one star while shown and zero while hidden: a plain star column would hold its share open around a collapsed tile, and `Auto` sizes to content. The separators stay `Auto`, so a hidden one measures to nothing and any other material's header is unchanged; the Weight Loss % column became `Auto` for the same reason, with a `MinWidth` on the box so it keeps its size. The tiles show `0 g` / `₱0.00` on an empty form. **Verified in the app** on the secondary monitor against the dev database, with real clicks and keystrokes — including the six tiles sharing the row evenly and a purchased material's four doing the same with no gap: Butter Icing read 2,968.2 g / ₱704.07 / ₱0.2372 (and ₱704.07 is the BOM grid's Cost column, 58.74 + 79.38 + 565.95, so the card can be checked by eye); typing 20 into Weight Loss % moved the yield to 2,448 g and the buffered tile to ₱0.2876 while Total Cost stayed put, and Ctrl+Z put all three back; Custard Filling reads 970 g / ₱123.97 / ₱0.1278; a purchased material still shows four tiles with no stray separator and no gap.
    - **The Last Updated tile carries two dates** (2026-09-21, at the user's request: "the last updated card is not showing dates, why?" — it was the newest price history entry's date, which a made or linked ingredient never has, so it read "—" for them however recently their cost had moved). `RawMaterial` gained **`UpdatedAt`** (when the form last saved it, stamped by `RawMaterialRepository`'s Add and Update as `Product.UpdatedAt` is) and **`CostUpdatedAt`** (when `CostPerBaseUnit` or `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit` last moved), both nullable local `DateTime`s, migration `RawMaterialUpdatedDates`. **The cost date is stamped in `TinapayanRMSDbContext.SaveChangesAsync`** from the change tracker, comparing each entry's original values: a cost is written from too many places to stamp one by one (a brand price, a buffer or buffered-cost edit, a BOM or weight-loss edit, a unit multiplier correction, the cascade reaching a made or linked ingredient), a new one would be easy to forget, and comparing originals means a save that rewrites the same figures leaves the date alone. **The tile is one date, `CostUpdatedAt`, to the day** ("Sep 21, 2026", at the user's request the same day: "Last Updated title should display a single date only for price change", replacing a two-line tile that had the save date over the cost date with a time on each). So the tile answers "when did this cost last move?", whatever moved it. `UpdatedAt` is still stamped and stored; nothing shows it. Existing rows were backfilled by hand (not in the migration, which is generated): the 48 purchased materials took both dates from their newest price history entry, since a price is written by a Save; the 3 made and 2 linked took today's date for the cost (the 2026-09-21 recompute moved it) and keep a null save date, which nothing shows.
    - **The tabs sit in a card of their own** (2026-09-19, at the user's request): a `CardStyle` border with 4px padding around the `TabControlExt`, as Products does, since the Windows11Light template draws the tab control's frame square and `TabControlExt` has no `CornerRadius`. **The tab cards have no frame of their own** (same day, at the user's request: "why not place the purchase cost setup directly inside the TabControlExt"): Purchase Cost Setup, Bill of Materials, Linked Cost, Alternative Units, Unit Conversion Overview, Usage Example and Price History each start with a plain `Border Padding="12"` in place of `CardStyle`, so they sit straight in their tab and only the tab card is framed. The UserControls keep their `*Card` names. **The tab control draws no frame either** (same day, at the user's request): `BorderThickness="0"` on the `TabControlExt`, which the Windows11Light template honours, so the card around it is the only border; the tab strip keeps its own background along the bottom. **Products has the same layout** (same day, at the user's request: "do the same layout with the other pages having a TabControlExt"): its one details card (header, fields, a divider, then the tabs) became the header card (header and fields, 12px padding) and, 4px under it, a `CardStyle` card with 4px padding around the `TabControlExt` (`BorderThickness="0"`); the divider went. The tab contents had no frames already, so each tab's `ScrollViewer` padding grew from `0,0,14,12` to `12,12,14,12` to keep them off the card's edge (Products' Recipe tab swapped its `ScrollViewer` for a plain `Border Padding="12"` on 2026-09-20, when the scrolling moved inside `RecipeStructureCard` — see "The tab strip stays put while the form scrolls"). The Recipe Structure's own tab control (a role per tab) sits inside the Recipe tab and keeps its frame, and the main window's tab strip and the Settings window aren't pages, so they're unchanged.
    - **The Sub-Assemblies and Products lists are as wide as Raw Materials'** (2026-09-19, at the user's request): Name 220px and Category / Role 185px, the default 16px card padding, and an `Auto` page column, so each card is as wide as its columns. Sub-Assemblies' columns were 200 and 110; Products' list sat in a fixed 340px column with 12px padding and a Name column that stretched to fill (`ColumnSizer="Star"`, now gone).
    - **Every navigation page follows it** (same day, at the user's request): Sub-Assemblies, Products and Reports have the same 4px page margin and 4px gap between cards (they had 16px, 12px and 16px). Spacing inside a card is unchanged, including Products' one details card, which holds its header, fields and tabs.
    - **Cards are 4px apart on Raw Materials** (2026-09-19, at the user's request; 16px before): the list/details gap column, the header's bottom margin and the Alternative Units gap column in `RawMaterialsView.xaml`, matching the page's own 4px margin. Only the gaps changed, so the header card keeps its height.
    - **Name moved out of the tiles** (2026-09-19, at the user's request): it's the first field of the row under them, before Type, Category and Source (a `1.5*` column, the others `*`), still `NameTextBox`, so New still puts the cursor in it and leaving it still checks the name is free.
    - **The Name field flags a taken name the moment it's left** (2026-09-17, at the user's request), not only when Save hits the same message from the database's unique index (`ViewModelBase.RunSafelyAsync`'s `DbUpdateException` catch, which stays as the fallback for a race or a bypass of this check). `NameTextBox`'s `LostFocus` (`SummaryHeaderCard.xaml.cs`) calls `RawMaterialsViewModel.CheckNameAvailability()`: a case-insensitive, trimmed comparison against the already-loaded `RawMaterials`, so no round trip, excluding the material being edited by id so leaving its own unchanged name alone never flags it. Fixing the name and leaving the field again clears the message this raised, without touching an unrelated one. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor: typing an existing material's name and tabbing to another field showed the error toast at once; fixing the name and tabbing out again cleared it.
    - **The Category picker lists only the categories of the material's type** (2026-09-18): `CategoryOptions` filters `_categoryCache` on `EditingType`, a new material switched to the other type lets its category go, and one added through "< Add New .. >" takes the material's type.
    - **The Category picker adds a category without leaving the screen for Settings** (2026-09-16, at the user's request): its list starts with a "< Add New .. >" row, set apart by a line under it, that opens a one-box prompt (`Views/TextPromptWindow` — WPF has no input box, and a `MessageBox` takes no text) and, on OK, saves the category and files the material under it. The row is a `RawMaterialCategory` with Id -1 (`RawMaterialsViewModel.AddNewCategory`) kept in a cache of its own, combined with the category cache by DynamicData's `Or` into `CategoryOptions`: inside `_categoryCache` it would be dropped by `LoadCommand`'s `MergeFrom` (an `EditDiff` against the categories loaded), and `Categories` stays a list of real ones for the lookups. `EditingCategory`'s setter refuses the row and runs `AddCategoryCommand` instead, which raises `PropertyChanged` for `EditingCategory` once it's done to put a category back in the picker: the new one, or the one that was there if the prompt was cancelled or the name refused (a duplicate is the usual `RunSafelyAsync` error toast). A value raised during the pick's own binding update is ignored, which is why it waits.
      - **Two things the picker forces.** The prompt opens after a `Dispatcher.Yield` in the View's interaction handler: one opened from inside the pick takes no typing, since the list it was picked from still has the mouse captured, and the list is left open behind it. And the row is drawn by a `ContentTemplate` set on that row alone through `ItemContainerStyle`, not by the picker's `ItemTemplate`: `ComboBoxAdv`'s closed box shows its `Text`, which is the picked item's `ToString()` unless `DisplayMemberPath` names the property to read (decompiled 34.2.7), and `ItemsControl` refuses `DisplayMemberPath` and `ItemTemplate` together. Its `SelectionBoxItemTemplate` can't stand in — the setter is internal.
      - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against the dev database: the row above the categories with its line, a category named and saved from it (success toast, the picker showing it). ViewModel tests cover the list's order and that a refresh keeps the row, the save-and-pick, and the cancel.
      - **Superseded: Cancel didn't actually leave the category that was picked — it did nothing at all** (found 2026-09-17, fixed the same day). `TextPromptWindow`'s Cancel `ButtonAdv` had `IsCancel="True"` and no `Click` handler, on the assumption it would work like a native `Button.IsCancel` (set `DialogResult = false` and close). It doesn't: decompiled and confirmed against the `syncfusion-wpf-button` skill's own docs, `ButtonAdv.IsCancel` only wires the **Escape key** to raise `Click` — with nothing listening, Escape and a mouse click on Cancel both did nothing, and the dialog just sat there. Fixed by giving Cancel the same explicit `Click="CancelButton_Click"` → `DialogResult = false` handling OK already had.
      - **The row renames itself to keep matching whatever's typed** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: typing a category that doesn't exist yet showed Syncfusion's "No Records Found" instead of Add New). `ComboBoxAdv`'s own AutoComplete (decompiled `Syncfusion.Windows.Tools.Controls.ComboBoxAdv.FilterItem`, `Syncfusion.Shared.WPF` 34.2.7) re-filters `CategoryOptions` by the typed text against every item's `Name`, "< Add New .. >" included, with no per-item exemption — so it dropped out exactly when it was the one useful option. `CategorySearchText` (bound to the picker's `Text`) drives a subscription that renames the row to `< Add New "{typed text}" .. >` through `_addNewCategoryOption.AddOrUpdate`, the same `SourceCache` path a real category's rename already flows through, so the picker's `CollectionView` sees a proper replace and re-filters on its own — no need for `RawMaterialCategory` to implement `INotifyPropertyChanged`, which it doesn't. `EditingCategory`'s setter switched from `ReferenceEquals(value, AddNewCategory)` to `value?.Id == AddNewCategoryId` accordingly, since the row is a fresh instance every time it's renamed.
        - **Two approaches tried and dropped first.** Re-templating `ComboBoxAdv` just to swap its "No_Records" `TextBlock` (found via `ilspycmd --resource` decompiling the control's own `generic.baml`) was ruled out as too much surface for one row. Filtering `CategoryOptions` itself client-side and setting `AutoCompleteMode="None"` (so Syncfusion's own filtering never ran) worked, but cost the picker its auto-open-on-type — confirmed in the app: clicking the field alone no longer opened the dropdown, only the dropdown arrow did, since `ComboBoxAdv.OnKeyUp` only sets `IsDropDownOpen = true` when `AutoCompleteMode == Suggest`. The rename approach keeps `Suggest` mode untouched, so auto-open-on-type still works.
        - **The prompt starts with what was already typed** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: picking "< Add New "Herbs and Spices" .. >" opened an empty box, meaning it had to be typed twice). `PromptForNewCategory`'s Input changed from a bare label string to `(string Label, string InitialValue)`; `AddCategoryCommand` passes `CategorySearchText.Trim()` as the value, and `TextPromptWindow.Prompt` takes it as a fourth parameter, pre-filling and selecting `ValueTextBox` so typing replaces it outright. Confirmed in the app that this reads the plain typed text ("Herbs and Spices"), not the row's decorated `Name` — `CategorySearchText` is read before `ComboBoxAdv`'s own `UpdateSelectedText` would overwrite it to the picked item's full display text.
  - **Alternative Units card**, typed straight into (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "the alternative units be edited directly inside the datagrid instead of clicking the buttons add alternative unit and edit"). Until then it was a read-only grid with Add Alternative Unit and Edit opening a form beneath, whose Apply put the unit in the list. Now it's the same kind of grid as the Bill of Materials: padded with empty rows (`GridFillerRows`), columns Alternative Unit (an autocompleting `GridMultiColumnDropDownList` on `UnitOfMeasureId`), Abbreviation (read-only, follows the unit) and Multiplier. The Example ("1 bag = 25,000 g") and Description columns went, as noise; a saved unit keeps whatever description it had, and nothing shows or edits it now. Remove, below the grid, stays for now and acts on the row picked.
    - **`RawMaterialUnitRow` is an editable row** (a `ReactiveObject`) over the form's copy of a `RawMaterialUnit`: setting `UnitOfMeasureId` or `BaseUnitsPerUnit` writes straight through, so Save, the purchase unit choices and the overview, which all read the units, needed nothing new. It resolves its unit of measure itself (a lookup the screen hands it), so the add row names its unit as soon as one is picked; `NewUnitRow` is the add row's item (`controls:GridAddNewRow.ItemFactory`). The screen changes rows in place rather than replacing them under the grid: a base unit change sets each row's `BaseUnitAbbreviation`, a rename in Settings `RefreshUnitOfMeasure()`, and a quantity typed in Purchase Cost Setup the row's multiplier.
    - An edit in the grid (a row added, its unit or multiplier changed) reprices the brands bought in that unit straight away (`OnUnitRowsEdited`, from `UnitRows.ToObservableChangeSet().AutoRefresh(...)`; the screen's own changes go through `SyncUnitRows` so they don't count).
    - **The add row goes at two units, through `controls:GridAddNewRow.ShowWhen="{Binding CanAddUnit}"`, not a binding on `AddNewRowPosition`.** Committing the second unit adds it to `UnitRows`; `CanAddUnit` turns false inside that `CollectionChanged`, and hiding the add row then makes SfDataGrid commit it again, which threw "Cannot change ObservableCollection during a CollectionChanged event" and closed the app (found in the app, 2026-09-17; the ViewModel tests can't see it). `ShowWhen` sets the position after the grid is done (`Dispatcher.BeginInvoke`).
    - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor, against the dev database without saving: Egg Whole's tray row, then pail and 5,000 typed into the add row (its abbreviation filled in as it was picked), the add row gone and the overview showing the pail; Remove on the pail brought the add row back.
  - The **Unit Conversion Overview** sits beside it (`Controls/UnitConversionOverview`: a box per unit pointing down into the base unit, bound to any rows exposing `UnitName` and `BaseEquivalentText`).
  - **Purchase Cost Setup** (purchased materials). Since 2026-09-18, at the user's request, it's a **Cost Buffer %** box (one per material, a cleared box saves as 0%) above a grid typed into like Alternative Units: **Brand, Purchase Unit, Qty per Purchase Unit, Purchase Price, Cost Buffer (₱)** (the price with the buffer on top, read-only: ₱930.00 at 5% is ₱976.50), with Remove below. The card has no summary box under the grid (dropped 2026-09-18 as redundant, at the user's request): the material's cost per base unit is in the header tiles. The ViewModel still works it out as prices are typed (`PurchaseCostText` and its companions, which the tests check). **Save logs a change in the cost to Price History, dated today.** It replaced two rows of text boxes for a single price (Purchase Unit, Quantity per Purchase Unit, Purchase Price, Cost Buffer %, Cost Buffer), where typing the buffered price worked out the percent; with several brands the percent is the only input.
    - **Since 2026-09-19 (the fixed buffered cost, see RawMaterial) the top row is Cost Buffer % | Buffered Cost per {base unit} (₱) | Recalculate**, with a line under it. `EditingBufferedCost` is the fixed amount (loaded from `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit`, sent on Save for a purchased material; a cleared box sends 0, which Save works out from the new prices and the %). `RecalculateBufferCommand` sets it to the dearest brand as typed (`DearestPurchaseCost`) with the % on top, rounded to 4 decimals; it's enabled only while a brand is priced. The % alone no longer moves it. `PurchaseBufferedCostText` says what the amount comes to over the dearest brand ("₱0.0525/g is 5% over the dearest brand's ₱0.05/g."), or, with none yet, what Save will work out. `BufferedCostWarningText` shows while the dearest brand costs more than the amount. The grid's last column is **Buffered (₱)**: the fixed amount in that row's unit (per base unit × Qty per Purchase Unit), or Save's preview while there's none (`IBrandPriceRowHost.BufferedCostPerBaseUnit`). The Usage Example's buffer is what the saved buffered cost comes to over the saved cost, which drifts from the % as prices move. The bullets below describe the setup before this.
    - **The Cost Buffer % and the Buffered Cost follow each other as they're typed** (2026-09-19, at the user's request, replacing "the % alone no longer moves it" above): typing the % sets the buffered cost to the dearest brand as the grid now is with the % on top (`TypedCostBufferPercent`, which the % box binds; nothing to work out from while no brand is priced), and typing the buffered cost sets the % to (buffered − dearest) / dearest, rounded to the 4 decimals the column keeps (`EditingBufferedCostInUnit`'s setter).
