Only typing links them: loading a material sets `EditingCostBufferPercent` and `EditingBufferedCost` directly, so a saved pair that drifted apart after a price change stays as saved, and a new price still moves neither (the line under the boxes and the warning say how far apart they are). Recalculate stays, to put the % back on top of a new dearest price. A buffered cost typed under the dearest brand works out a negative %, which Save rejected (0–100%) until the 2026-09-21 clamp below, and which the same day's rule turned into a % the dearest price keeps up to date.
    - **The grid's last column is Buffer %** (same day, replacing Buffered (₱)): each brand's `BufferPercent`, the buffered cost in the row's unit less its price, over its price (`CostBufferPercentOf`), so the dearest brand shows the material's %, a cheaper one more, and one priced above the buffered cost a negative figure. Read-only, 2 decimals.
    - **The Buffered Cost box is in a unit you pick** (2026-09-19, at the user's request): a `ComboBoxAdv` beside it, labelled Per, lists the base unit and the form's alternative units with a multiplier (`BufferedCostUnitOptions`, `PurchaseUnitChoice`s; rebuilt only when they change, and a picked unit that goes falls back to the base unit). It starts on the dearest brand's unit when a material loads (`DearestBrandUnit`), and isn't saved. The box binds `EditingBufferedCostInUnit`: `EditingBufferedCost` times the unit's multiplier, **shown rounded to 2 decimals**, while what's typed is divided back to the base unit unrounded (the user's call: displayed to two decimals, no limit on the precision inside). So it updates when focus leaves (`UpdateSourceTrigger=LostFocus`), since the value it shows back is rounded, and takes up to 10 decimals as typed (`NumberDecimalDigits=-1`, `MinimumNumberDecimalDigits=2`, `MaximumNumberDecimalDigits=10`). The line under it is in the same unit, to 2 decimals. Recalculate no longer rounds. **The summary header's two cost tiles follow the Per unit too** (2026-09-19, at the user's request: the header "can also display a different unit like kilogram or pack depending on what I select on the purchase cost setup"): "Current Cost per bag" and "Buffered Cost per bag" (`HeaderCostUnitText` in the labels), the saved costs times the unit's multiplier, to 2 decimals, or to 4 on the base unit as before (`UpdateHeaderCostTexts`, run on a new selection and from `UpdatePurchaseCostTexts`). The Base Unit tile stays the base unit, since it's what costs are kept in. A made or linked ingredient has no Purchase Cost Setup, so its Per unit is always the base unit and its header stays per gram. **Replacing the choices is guarded (`_rebuildingBufferedCostUnits`)**: clearing them makes the `ComboBoxAdv` write back an empty pick through its TwoWay `SelectedItem`, and that setter updated the texts, which rebuilt the choices again halfway through, until the app died of a stack overflow (0xc00000fd) the moment a material loaded. Found in the app; the ViewModel tests have no picker to write back, so they can't see it.
    - **Since 2026-09-21 the buffer row sits under the brand grid, and Recalculate is gone** (at the user's request: "move the controls Cost Buffer %, Buffered Cost, Per, and the recalculate button at the bottom of the DataGrid", then "remove the recalculate button. it serves no purpose"). The card reads: header, the brand grid, Remove, then Cost Buffer % | Buffered Cost (₱) | Per with the two lines under them. `RecalculateBufferCommand` is deleted: typing the % again is what puts the buffer back on top of a new dearest price, so `BufferedCostWarningText` says so ("Type the Cost Buffer % again to put the buffer back on top."). **The % box is capped 0–100** (`MinValue="0"`, `MaxValue="100"`), the range the validator and the `CK_RawMaterials_CostBufferPercent` check constraint have always enforced, and the % is clamped to the same range wherever it's worked out, so it never leaves a value Save would reject; `PurchaseBufferedCostText` and `BufferedCostWarningText` say where the amount really stands when the % is pinned. The clamp also keeps the box's own `MinValue`/`MaxValue` coercion out of it: a pinned value it wrote back through the TwoWay binding would reach `TypedCostBufferPercent` and overwrite the amount.
    - **Since 2026-09-21 the % follows the dearest price and the amount stays fixed** (at the user's request: "why do the Cost Buffer % does not recalculate when the dearest price in the datagrid changes", choosing "% follows the Buffered Cost" over making the amount follow the %). `UpdatePurchaseCostTexts` sets `EditingCostBufferPercent` to `CostBufferPercentOf(dearest, EditingBufferedCost)`, rounded to 4 decimals and clamped to 0-100, whenever it runs with a priced brand and an amount above 0: a price typed into the grid, a unit's multiplier, a brand added or removed, a material loaded, an undo. So the box agrees with the dearest row's **Buffer %** column instead of showing the % that was applied when it was last typed, and `EditingBufferedCostInUnit`'s setter no longer works the % out itself (the amount it sets reaches the same place). **The amount is still what a price change never moves**, and typing the % still sets the amount (`TypedCostBufferPercent`), so the two directions are: type the % to put it on top of the dearest brand, type or leave the amount and the % says what it comes to. The costs: the saved `CostBufferPercent` is now the live ratio rather than the figure originally chosen, and a buffer that's gone negative or gone over 100% pins the box at 0 or 100 (the line and the warning under the boxes still give the true figure). This is what replaced Recalculate; it's also why the box is the one place the % is worked out.
      - The last two follow each other: typing the % works out Cost Buffer (`RawMaterialRules.BufferedCost`, rounded to 2 decimals), and typing Cost Buffer works out the % (`CostBufferPercentOf`, rounded to the 4 decimals the column keeps). A `_syncingCostBuffer` guard stops each from setting the other back.
      - Only the % is saved: Cost Buffer (`EditingBufferedPrice`) is worked out from the price, so a new price keeps the % and moves it. It's read-only until there's a price. Typed one keystroke at a time it passes through low values, so the % goes briefly negative; Save still rejects anything outside 0–100%. It first sat in the summary header, and moved here since it's part of costing a purchase. So the screen only edits a purchased material's buffer; a made ingredient keeps whatever buffer it has, though the model and cascade still support one. Under the live cost per base unit, the setup shows it with the buffer on top ("Buffered (5%): ₱0.0525"). The Usage Example is pure ViewModel math (`UnitConversionRules`), for **the material being edited only** (2026-09-18, at the user's request: its Material picker was dropped, and `ExampleRawMaterial` follows the selection). It starts on the material's largest alternative unit. Its big number is the buffered total, and the breakdown reads quantity in base unit, cost per base unit, calculated cost, buffer and buffered cost (the user's own table: ₱10.00, 5%, ₱10.50), from the material's saved costs.
  - **Price History** (Date, Brand, Unit, Price, cost per base unit; Delete Price, which only removes a log entry) for a saved purchased material, and the BOM card for made ingredients.
  - **The cards sit in tabs along the bottom, under the summary header** (2026-09-14, at the user's request; a `TabControlExt` with `TabStripPlacement="Bottom"`, fixed tabs like the Settings window's):
    - **Cost Setup:** Purchase Cost Setup, or for a made ingredient the Bill of Materials in its place (a material is one or the other; the user's choice), or for a linked ingredient the **Linked Cost** card (2026-09-16: Linked To — `LinkTargets`, every ingredient but the one being edited — Weight Share %, Cost Share %, the Cost Buffer % it inherits (read-only and greyed, `controls:ReadOnlyText`), a live `LinkedCostPreviewText` worked out with `RawMaterialRules.LinkedCost`, and `LinkedPartsText` naming the linked-to ingredient's other parts and the totals with this one). **The tab is one full-width card** (2026-09-18). The Usage Example was a card beside the cost card, then a section at the bottom of each of the three cost cards, and since 2026-09-18 (at the user's request) is **its own tab, Usage Example, after Alternative Units** (`UsageExampleCard`; Price History moved to index 3, `RawMaterialsViewModel.PriceHistoryTab`). It prices from the material's saved cost, so an unsaved edit on Cost Setup shows only once saved.
      - **The BOM's Component column suggests `BomComponentOptions`, not `Ingredients`** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: the material being edited showed up as a suggested component of itself). Built the same way as `LinkTargets` — same `notTheSelection` filter, now shared by both — it's every ingredient but the one being edited. `IngredientBomRules.EnsureNoCycle` already rejected a self-reference at save time ("An ingredient can't be a component of itself."), so this only keeps the invalid pick off the suggestion list; `Ingredients` itself stays unfiltered, since it also resolves a saved BOM line's `IngredientId` and a saved `LinkedToId` back to their material, which must find every ingredient, not just the pickable ones.
        - **Save also checks `BomLineEdits` for a self-reference directly, unconditionally** (2026-09-17, at the user's request): the picker keeps a *new* self-reference off the grid, but a self-referencing line already in the database (only reachable by editing it directly, never through this app) could still slip past. `ChangedBomLines` only re-validates a BOM that changed from what was loaded, so `EnsureNoCycle` never runs for a Save that leaves an untouched, already-bad BOM alone while saving something else — the SaveCommand's own check runs regardless, before `ChangedBomLines` decides whether to send anything, and refuses the whole Save (nothing partial) with the same message.
    - **Alternative Units:** the Alternative Units card beside the Unit Conversion Overview.
    - **Price History:** its grid fills the tab. The tab only shows for a saved purchased material (`ShowPriceHistory`).
    - The header stays put and each tab scrolls on its own, so the strip stays at the bottom.
    - The ViewModel owns the selected tab (`SelectedDetailsTab`). **Picking a made or new material while on Price History goes back to Cost Setup, and must happen before that tab hides:** a `TabControlExt` whose selected tab collapses picks a neighbour itself (it landed on Alternative Units). So the subscription that moves the selection is made before `ShowPriceHistory`'s, and runs first.
  - **Each card is its own `UserControl` in `Views/RawMaterials/`** (2026-09-14, at the user's request: the view was too nested, about 14 elements deep). `RawMaterialsView.xaml` is a skeleton naming the cards (`MaterialListCard`, `SummaryHeaderCard`, then per tab `PurchaseCostSetupCard`/`BillOfMaterialsCard`/`LinkedCostCard`, `AlternativeUnitsCard`/`UnitConversionCard`, `UsageExampleCard`, `PriceHistoryCard`).
    - Like `ErrorToast`, a card never sets a `DataContext`; it inherits `RawMaterialsViewModel`, so its bindings are the screen's. When a card shows (`ShowPurchaseSetup`, `IsEditingMade`) is set where the skeleton uses it, not inside the card.
    - Inside a card, a row of side-by-side fields is one two-row `Grid`: labels in row 0, fields in row 1, as its direct children, with no `StackPanel` per field. A label and field that hide together both carry the `Visibility` binding. A control without a label (the Usage Example's unit picker and Calculate) spans both rows, bottom-aligned.
    - No file nests deeper than 7 elements. The move changed no pixels: before and after captures of each tab matched.
    - A screen that outgrows one file follows the same shape: its cards in a folder named for it under `Views/`. Styles only one card uses live in that card.
    - `SummaryHeaderCard.FocusName()` is how the screen puts the cursor in Name on New, since `NameTextBox` now lives in the card.
  - Every packaging line editor — the Products Packaging tab, the Add Sub-Assembly form and a packaging set's Lines — has a Unit picker (a `GridMultiColumnDropDownList` over `PackagingUnitChoice`: base unit first, then the material's alternative units largest first, each beside what one of it costs; `BaseUnitText` names the base unit). Recipe line editors (the BOM card, and a sub-assembly's lines in the Products form) had one too until 2026-09-14; they now take grams only, showing Component (or Ingredient) and Grams. The BOM card also shows read-only Cost per g (the component's buffered cost per gram) and Cost (Grams × Cost per g), as the sub-assembly lines do. Recipe editors offer ingredients only, Products' packaging editor packaging only, and Reports' price trend any purchased material.
- **One Save writes the whole material** (2026-09-13, at the user's request: adding one took a Save for the details, then Save Unit per alternative unit, Save Price and Save BOM).
  - **The screen edits everything in memory.**
    - Alternative units are rows over the form's own copies of the units (Id 0 until saved). Save checks them first (`EnsureValidUnitRows`, what Apply used to): every row has a unit ("Pick a unit for every alternative unit, or remove the empty row.") and a positive multiplier (naming Quantity per Purchase Unit for a unit added there, else Alternative Units), then `RawMaterialUnitValidator`, not the base unit, not already listed, at most two. Remove only drops the row.
    - The purchase unit choices (`PurchaseUnitOptions`, `PurchaseUnitChoice` records keyed by unit of measure id, `0` for the base unit) are the base unit and every unit of measure, and `BaseUnitName`/`BaseUnitAbbreviation` follow the form's type and base unit, so a new packaging material shows its own unit before it's saved. The list is rebuilt only when it actually changes, so an open unit cell keeps its items.
    - **An alternative unit can also be added from the Purchase Cost Setup** (2026-09-13, at the user's request: a unit had to be added in Alternative Units before it could be priced in; carried into the grid 2026-09-18).
      - The unit cell lists every unit of measure, the ones the form doesn't have yet among them, by name. A packaging material's base unit and a unit named like an ingredient's gram are left out.
      - Picking one the form doesn't have adds it as a row straight away, while there's room for another alternative unit (otherwise it says so and adds nothing). The row's Qty per Purchase Unit is then its multiplier: typeable only for a unit not saved yet (`BrandPriceRow.CanTypeQuantity`, through `controls:GridCellEditing.AllowWhen`), read-only for the base unit and saved units, whose multiplier is corrected in the Alternative Units grid, since that reprices.
      - A unit added that way stays only while a brand row is in it: picking another unit, or removing the row, takes it back out (`DropUnusedAddedUnits`), so trying units out leaves nothing behind. Save rejects one whose quantity is still empty.
      - The grid's add row edits the screen's own row (`NewBrandPriceRow`, through `controls:GridAddNewRow.ItemFactory`), which talks to the screen through `IBrandPriceRowHost`: a unit picked there is added before the row is committed, and its buffered price follows the buffer. (The single-price picker it replaced had to keep a `ComboBoxAdv`'s items in place while it handled a pick, or it fell back to its first item. The grid's unit choices don't change when a unit is picked, since they already list every unit of measure.)
    - The cards follow the form, not the selection. Purchase Cost Setup follows the editing source, the BOM card `IsEditingMade` and the Linked Cost card `IsEditingLinked`, so a new material shows every card before its first Save. Price History (`ShowPriceHistory`) only shows once a purchased material is saved.
  - **What Save sends** to `IRawMaterialRepository.SaveAsync(material, units, brandPrices, bomLines)`:
    - An edit still open in the Alternative Units grid is committed when focus leaves it (`controls:GridEditCommit.OnFocusLeave`), so clicking Save includes it.
    - `units` is the complete list.
    - For a purchased material, every brand row (a row left entirely blank is skipped), each naming its unit by unit of measure, since a unit added in the same save has no id yet. A row missing its unit or price, in a unit the form doesn't have, or repeating a brand in the same unit is refused first. `null` for any other material, which leaves brand prices alone.
    - BOM lines only when they differ from the lines last loaded. An untouched BOM is neither replaced nor, when empty, rejected, so a made ingredient can still be saved before it has lines.
    - Afterwards `LoadCommand` runs, then a fresh copy of the material is selected, so the form reloads what was saved: units with ids, the brand prices, the price history and the BOM.
  - **How it saves: one context and one transaction.**
    - Every step goes through the `internal static` write methods the single-purpose repository methods now share: `RawMaterialRepository.AddAsync`/`UpdateAsync`, `RawMaterialUnitRepository.AddAsync`/`UpdateAsync`/`DeleteAsync`, `RawMaterialBrandPriceRepository.RemoveAllAsync`/`AddAllAsync`/`RepriceAsync` and `IngredientBomLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`. Each takes the caller's `TinapayanRMSDbContext`, saves to it, and says whether the cost cascade must run.
    - Order: the material; the old brand prices go (so a unit only they were in can be removed); its units, removed first, then updated, then added (so a replacement fits within the limit of two, and a removed unit of measure can come back as a new row); the new brand prices, then the reprice, compared with the cost from before the save so at most one price is logged; the BOM. Each step saves as it goes, so the next step's checks see what it wrote.
    - A rejection anywhere rolls all of it back, and the cascade runs once, after the commit.
    - The caller's entities are only read, so a rolled-back save never leaves an id on them.
    - `SetLinesAsync` gained the transaction its delete-then-insert lacked.
    - The public single-purpose methods keep their behaviour, and their tests keep covering the checks; `RawMaterialSavePersistenceTests` covers the whole save.
  - **Refreshes keep unsaved units.** `LoadCommand` points each row at its refreshed unit of measure (so a rename in Settings shows) instead of rebuilding the rows from the refreshed material.
    - `NewCommand` clears the details itself: after an unsaved new material nothing was selected, so the selection subscriptions don't fire.
    - Delete Price stays immediate and reloads only the price history, where selecting the refreshed material would drop unsaved edits. It changes no cost, so nothing else reloads.
- **One Products screen (`ProductsView`/`ProductsViewModel`), rebuilt from the user's Cream Loaf / Chocolate Cake mockups (2026-09-14): the Raw Materials shape, with one Save.** A product is the mockups' "recipe". The app shell stays as it is, so the mockups' sidebar, breadcrumb and Back button were left out, as were the product photo, sub-assembly descriptions (sections show the role), "by Admin" (the app has no users) and the usage example's product picker.
  - **Product categories** (2026-09-17, at the user's request; the Domain Model has the entity):
    - **The header's Category picker works as Raw Materials' does**: an "< Add New .. >" row above the categories (`ProductsViewModel.AddNewCategory`, Id -1, in a cache of its own), renamed to include what's typed so it survives `ComboBoxAdv`'s own AutoComplete filter, opening `TextPromptWindow` pre-filled with the typed text and picking the saved category. Unlike Raw Materials, adding one reloads only the categories, not the whole screen. Deleting the picker's text clears the category, and Save then saves none: verified in the app, since a category is optional here.
    - **The list has a Category column** (`Category.Name`, 110 px wide, empty for an uncategorized product), beside Name, which takes the rest; the list stays flat and name-sorted. To fit both, the list's column went from 280 to 340 px. Checked in the app at 1,366 px maximized: full product names and the details region laid out as before.
      - **Superseded: grouping by category** (built earlier the same day, removed at the user's request: "don't group products in the product list, instead add a column for Category"). It grouped into collapsible sections with Uncategorized last, through a group converter and an attached `GridNewGroupsExpanded` behaviour; those, and their `ProductListGroupingTests`, were deleted. Two Syncfusion findings from it still hold: **a `GroupColumnDescription` converter only runs for a column the grid has** (without one, every row fell in a single null-keyed group), and **rows that arrive in one Reset leave every group collapsed despite `AutoExpandGroups`** (Syncfusion.Data expands a group only while grouping is applied, `CollectionViewAdv.IsInGroup`), while rows added one by one come out expanded.
    - Tests: `ProductCategoryPersistenceTests`, and the picker's flows in `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests`. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against the dev database with a seeded test category and product (removed afterwards): the picker and its Add New prompt, a Save changing the product's category, clearing the category, and deleting a category in Settings leaving the product without one.
  - **Skeleton** (`ProductsView.xaml`; sections in `Views/Products/`, each inheriting the screen's DataContext, none nested deeper than 7 elements): a root Grid `340 | 12 | *` (margin 12; the list was 280 wide until its Category column, 2026-09-17) with the `ErrorToast` over it, and **two bordered regions** (compact since 2026-09-15, see "The compact layout" below). `ProductListCard` is the first: a search box feeding `VisibleProducts` (a DynamicData `Filter`), while the link and clone pickers keep the unfiltered `Products`. The second is a `CardStyle` Border (padding 12) holding `ProductHeaderCard`, then `ProductDetailsCard` (the fields typed in), a divider, then a `TabControlExt` with fixed tabs along the bottom, bound to `SelectedDetailsTab`, each tab its own `ScrollViewer` (padding `0,0,14,12`, which keeps unboxed text clear of the overlaid scrollbar):
    - **Recipe:** `RecipeStructureCard`, the tab's full width (2026-09-19). The Recipe Usage Example beside it (a quantity in pieces and Calculate, giving that many pieces' material cost by section) was removed entirely at the user's request, with `RecipeSidePanel`, `RecipeUsageExampleCard` and the ViewModel's `ExampleQuantity`, `ExampleResult`, `CalculateExampleCommand` and `RecipeUsageExampleResult`. The Raw Materials screen's own Usage Example tab is a different feature and stays.
    - **Overview: removed** (2026-09-19, at the user's request: "it serves no purpose for me now"). It held `CostOverviewCard` (the Recipe Cost Overview doughnut) beside `PieceConversionCard` (each sub-assembly's batch and pieces, a `UnitConversionOverview` in rows), in a `ResponsiveColumnsPanel`. Deleted with them: the ViewModel's `CostShares`, `CostShareRow`, `HasMaterialCost` and `HasUsages`, `ProductUsageEdit.UnitName` and `BaseEquivalentText`, `UnitConversionOverview.ShowAsRows`, and `ResponsiveColumnsPanel`, which nothing else used. The tabs are Recipe, Costing, Packs and Clone: `ProductsViewModel.LinksTab` (Clone) is 3.
    - **Costing:** one `CostingCard` (2026-09-19, at the user's request: the Costing Summary and Recipe Cost Breakdown merged, the parameters on the left and the breakdown on the right): the five percentage tiles stacked in the left column (2*), a divider, then the breakdown table (3*). It replaced `CostingSummaryCard` (the tiles across the top), `CostBreakdownCard` and `ProfitMarginCard`, which was removed entirely at the user's request with `ProductCostSummary.ProfitMarginPercent` (`CostingRules.CalculateProfitMarginPercent` stays, for `PackCostSummary`).
    - **Clone:** `CloneCard`, only for a saved product (`ShowLinks`): one New Product Name, with **Clone** (`CloneCommand`) and **Create Variant** (`CreateVariantCommand`, since 2026-09-19; see Derived products) side by side and a caption saying which copies and which links. It was **Links & Clone** until 2026-09-18, with `PackagingLinkCard` (`TabLinksCard` until 2026-09-14) beside it; linking the packaging moved into Add Sub-Assembly (see Derived products). The subscription that moves `SelectedDetailsTab` back to Recipe is made before `ShowLinks`, as Raw Materials does for Price History.
    - There's no empty state: with nothing selected the form is "New product", which can be filled in completely before its first Save.
  - **Header** (the user's own mockup, 2026-09-15): the initial tile, the title (the name, or "New product") and New, Save and Delete; under the title, the description as a link (pencil glyph, accent colour, underlined under the mouse, trimmed with an ellipsis), reading "Add description" while there's none (`DescriptionLinkText`). Below, one row of figures, labels over values, **nothing typed into** (2026-09-19, at the user's request: "decouple data entry from the Products header and place it below the header"): Material Cost, Base Cost (`ProductCostSummary.BaseCost`), Current Price (`EditingCurrentPrice` as typed, "—" until there is one), Markup % and Last Updated. Base Unit and Selling Price left the header then (Selling Price is still on the Costing tab). The fields typed in — Product Name, Category (since 2026-09-17; see below) and Current Price — are `ProductDetailsCard`, under the header, laid out like Raw Materials' Type/Category row. Current Price and Markup % came on 2026-09-19: a `CurrencyTextBox` bound to `EditingCurrentPrice`, empty rather than ₱0.00 until typed, and in the header `ProductCostSummary.CurrentMarkupPercent`, "—" with no price and red when the price doesn't cover the base cost, `IsCurrentPriceBelowCost`; it follows the price and the costing fields as they're typed) and Last Updated ("Sep 15, 2026, 11:44 AM" on one line, or "—"; the tooltip has it in full when a narrow window trims it). No full-width Description box: the link opens a `Popup` under itself with a multi-line box, Cancel and Done. The box edits `EditingDescription` as it's typed, so the link follows it; Done, Enter or a click anywhere else keeps what was typed, and Cancel or Esc puts back what was there when it opened (`EditDescriptionCommand` remembers it). A click on Save with the pop-up open therefore saves what was typed. The box gets the cursor, with the text selected, when the pop-up opens. `ProductDetailsCard.FocusName()` puts the cursor in Product Name on New.
  - **Recipe Structure:** a tab for every sub-assembly the product uses, its own and linked, called by its role, and its name too when the role repeats (`UsageEdits` of `ProductUsageEdit`, in role `SortOrder`, inserted in place; see "Tabs are called by their roles" below), then **New Sub-Assembly** while one is being added, then **Packaging** while the product has any (since 2026-09-18; until then it was always there, and a new product opened on it), with **Add Sub-Assembly** and **Delete Sub-Assembly** buttons in the card's header (see "The Recipe Structure is tabs" below; stacked sections until 2026-09-15, and a permanent Add Sub-Assembly tab until 2026-09-17). A section's batch totals come from its lines (`SubAssemblyRules.CalculateTotals` over the ingredients' buffered costs), so an unsaved one prices like a saved one.
    - **No title inside a tab** (2026-09-19, at the user's request: the subtitle was redundant, the tab header already names it): `UsageFormPanel` no longer shows `EditingUsage.SectionName`, nor `PackagingSectionView` "Packaging". Their badges (linked from, shared with) stay, now at the left edge, each with its own bottom margin so the row takes no room without one. **Cost per piece moved onto the Remove Line row** under each table (caption and value side by side, just left of the button), in the recipe lines, the packaging form and the Packaging tab. The row now always shows, and only the button hides when the lines aren't the product's own (linked), so a linked section still shows its cost. The "Linked Recipe" heading over a linked section's lines went too (same day, at the user's request), since the badge already says where they're from. The "This Product's Add-Ins" heading over the add-ins grid went later (2026-09-19, at the user's request), so neither table carries a heading.
    - **A sub-assembly's tab is its form and nothing else** (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "refactor the sub-assembly tab to show only the UsageFormPanel, if the subassembly is link then show the ingredients inside the UsageFormPanel in readonly mode"). `SubAssemblySectionView` and the read view it drew are gone, and with them `ProductUsageEdit.IngredientRows`, `PercentTotal` and `RecipeIngredientRow`, which nothing else used. Showing a sub-assembly's tab is what opens its form (`OpenUsageForm` from `RecipeTabShown`), so `EditUsageCommand` is now just "show that tab" and `SubAssemblyRecipeTab.IsEditing` is gone — a tab has no other state to be in. Per-piece figures stay on the Costing tab's Cost Breakdown and in the Recipe Cost Overview; the form's own table is the batch, in grams.
      - **The form's header** (shown for a section already in the recipe, in place of the "Add Sub-Assembly" title) has the role (the name until 2026-09-17), the "From Cream Loaf" badge for a **linked** one ("Variety Bread Dough, from Sub-Assemblies" for a standalone one, which keeps its name) and the "Also used by Cream Loaf Mini" badge for one of the product's own that others link, and its cost per piece live off the form (`UsageFormCostPerPieceText`, worked out by `RecomputeUsageForm` with the preview text). The "Role · 1,700 g batch · 28.33 pcs · 60 g per piece" caption went with the read view: Total Weight, Yield and Weight per Piece are all in the form below it, live.
      - **A linked one's lines show, read-only.** `FormLines` is filled for every mode now, not just the editable ones, and `IsFormShowingLines` (`IsFormEditingLines || FormLines.Count > 0`) shows the table while `IsFormEditingLines` alone drives `AllowEditing`, `AllowDeleting`, the Name and Role fields and the Remove Line row. The add row has to be hidden as well (`AddNewRowPositionConverter`: `Bottom` or `None`) — `AllowEditing="False"` leaves it there, inviting a line the grid then refuses. No caption says whose the lines are: the Linked badge already does, and the caption that did went on 2026-09-19 at the user's request. A linked section refreshed from its owner refills the table (`MirrorUsageTabs`), so the read-only lines are never stale.
      - **Make a Copy and Remove moved into the form's button row** (Remove moved on to the card's header as Delete Sub-Assembly on 2026-09-17, below), beside Cancel and Apply (both gone since 2026-09-20, below), taking `EditingUsage` as their parameter; Remove refuses one that others link, on the spot (`SubAssemblyRules.EnsureNotLinkedElsewhere`). Make a Copy binds `IsEditingLinkedUsage`, not `EditingUsage.IsLinked`: a binding through a null `EditingUsage` has no value at all, and the button fell back to showing on the add form. `ReplaceUsage` reopens the form on the copy, since its tab has nothing else to show.
      - **Leaving a tab applies the form it was showing** (`CommitUsageFormOnLeave`), the way `GridEditCommit.OnFocusLeave` commits a grid row, so nothing typed is lost now that a tab change swaps which section the form holds. A rule refusing it shows the message and puts the tab showing back (`_revertingRecipeTab` stops the tab it puts back applying its own form again). An edit that changes nothing is skipped (`ProductUsageEdit.ShowsSameAs`), so merely clicking through tabs doesn't churn the recipe or its costs. This made `ApplyUsageFormAsync` synchronous (`ApplyUsageForm`): a tab change can't await, so the Link branch builds from `FormLines`, which `LoadUsageSourceAsync` already keeps in step with the pick, in place of fetching the lines a second time. It refuses a pick whose lines haven't arrived rather than linking a batch of nothing.
    - **The Packaging tab is typed straight into** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "make it use [the padded datagrid] for inline editing, remove the Edit button and Add Packaging"), set up like the sub-assembly form's lines table: `GridFillerRows`, a fixed 200 px height, `EditTrigger="OnTap"`, the add row at the bottom (`ItemFactory` = `NewLineEdit`, `DropWhenUntouched`), `GridEditCommit.OnFocusLeave`, `AutoCompleteDropDown` on the material picker, and Remove Line. The Add/Edit form (`PackagingLineFormPanel`, its `EditingLine*` fields, `EntryOptions`/`PackagingEntryOption`, Apply and Cancel) and the Total row under the grid are gone; the tab's header still shows the cost per piece.
      - **Columns: Material, Qty, Unit, Unit Cost, Cost per Piece** (2026-09-17, at the user's request; Unit became a picker 2026-09-20). **Qty is typed in whichever of the material's units the Unit cell names** — its base unit, or one of its at most two alternative units — and `PackagingLineRow` stores that as `UnitId`, which Save now writes instead of the `null` it wrote from 2026-09-17 to 2026-09-20. **What the quantity comes to in base units is never shown** — a read-only **Base Qty** column ("1,000 cm") was there for part of 2026-09-20 and the user dropped it: the conversion belongs behind the scenes. `PackagingLineRow.BaseQty` still works it out, and it is what the line's total cost is worked out from. **Unit Cost is what one of the *picked* unit costs** (2026-09-20, at the user's request: "the unit cost column is cost per unit if the unit is converted to the alternative unit") — ₱0.05 for a cm of ribbon, ₱50.00 once its 1,000 cm roll is picked — not the flat cost per base unit it showed before. A line saved as pieces per unit still shows as the quantity it comes to (`ReconcileReciprocal`) and is rewritten that way once the packaging is changed and saved; `UnitsPerPackagingUnit` and `AuthoredField` stay in the schema with no entry of their own.
        - **The Unit cell is an editable `GridComboBoxColumn` bound to `MappingName="UnitKey"`**, an int on the row where 0 is the base unit (`PackagingUnitChoice`, the same scalar-key shape as Purchase Cost Setup's `PurchaseUnitChoice`, since a grid cell binds a key rather than an object). `IsEditable="True"` lets the unit be typed as well as picked, `DisplayMemberPath="Abbreviation"` shows it as "roll", and `SelectedValuePath="Key"` commits it. It was briefly a `GridMultiColumnDropDownList` on 2026-09-20 — first with a Cost per Unit column beside the unit, then with the row's own Qty echoed beside it and no header row — and the user reverted both in favour of a plain editable combo. `RawMaterialUnitOption` is not used here: it carries a nullable id, which a `SelectedValuePath` can't key on.
          - **Every row has its own material, so its options come from the row** through `PackagingUnitSelector`, an `IItemsSourceSelector` declared once in `App.xaml` and shared by all three packaging grids — a dropdown column's own `ItemsSource` is one collection for the whole column. `GridComboBoxColumn` and `GridMultiColumnDropDownList` both support it, each re-invoking `GetItemsSource(record, dataContext)` with the row as the record.
          - **`PackagingLineRow.UnitCost` is the picked unit's cost, and must not feed the line's total.** `ProductRules.CalculatePackagingLineCost` multiplies its first argument by the quantity *converted to base units*, so passing it a figure already scaled to the picked unit would double-convert (1 roll at ₱50 would cost ₱50,000). The row keeps a private `CostPerBaseUnit` for that call, and `UnitCost` — which only the grid reads — looks up the selected `PackagingUnitChoice.UnitCost`. `PackagingLineRowTests` pins the two apart at a quantity of two rolls: ₱50 a roll, ₱100 the line.
        - **A row is never in a unit its material doesn't have**: `PackagingLineRow.UnitKey`'s setter falls back to the base unit for a key its `UnitOptions` don't offer, so changing a row's material, restoring an undo step or reading a snapshot can't leave a unit behind that `SaveStandalonePackagingAsync`/`SetLinesAsync` would then refuse. Covered by `PackagingLineRowTests`.
      - The rows are `PackagingLineRow` (`ViewModels/PackagingLineRow.cs`, which replaced `PackagingLineEdit`), a `ReactiveObject` built like `IngredientLineRow`: the picker sets `PackagingMaterialId`, the screen points `PackagingMaterial` at its copy (`Resolve`, also on refresh, in place), which prices the row. `PackagingLineRow.CompleteLines` checks the rows on Save (blank rows are dropped; a material without a quantity, a quantity without a material, or a material on two rows is refused). A change to `LineEdits` or a row's material or cost recomputes the costs. Linked packaging shows read-only: `IsPackagingEditable` (not `IsPackagingLinked`) turns off editing, deleting and the add row, and hides Remove Line.
    - **The sub-assembly form (`UsageFormPanel`, rebuilt 2026-09-14)** takes the sub-assembly the way Add Sub-Assembly's drop-down picked (`UsageFormMode`, carried by `AddUsageCommand`; the form asked for it itself, in a **Sub-Assembly** picker, until 2026-09-20). Its title says which, and its **Existing Sub-Assembly or Packaging** picker shows only for a Link or a Copy (`IsFormPickingSource`), alone on a full-width row. New and Copy show Role (Name and Role until 2026-09-17) and a table the lines are typed straight into (Ingredient, Grams, Cost; see "Recipe lines are typed into the table" below); a copy starts from its source's lines and role. Link and Copy pick from other products' sub-assemblies that have lines ("Dough — Cream Loaf", or "Chocolate Glaze — Sub-Assemblies (Glaze)" for a standalone one; linking leaves out ones in a role the recipe already has, which takes in the ones it uses; a product never picks its own), loading the pick's lines in the background (a pick the form has moved on from is dropped). Every mode ends with **Shrinkage %**, **Weight per Piece (g)**, **Yield (pcs)**, then **Total Weight (g)** and **Final Weight (g)** (both read-only): typing either of the pair works out the other live, rounded to the 2 decimals the boxes show, and records which was typed (`_syncingPieceSize` stops each box setting the other back, as Raw Materials' Cost Buffer pair does); a caption says which one stays put, and a preview gives the cost per piece. **Shrinkage isn't one of the pair** — typing it never changes which figure is "the one typed" — but the pair divides the final weight, so a change to it works the other figure out again exactly as a line does: `ResyncPieceSize` is the one entry point all three boxes, the lines and the add-ins go through. Its box is `MinValue="0" MaxValue="99.99"`, as Raw Materials' Weight Loss % is, and `CurrentShrinkagePercent()` clamps to the same range so a figure that reached the form another way can't price a piece at a negative. With a shrinkage set, the caption names the batch the pair is read against ("out of the 1,530 g the batch makes"), since that's the figure that stops matching the lines above. Editing the product's own section opens it with its role, lines and piece size; editing a linked one, its piece size, with its lines read-only below. Apply checks that there's a role and a line — not a piece size, since 2026-09-20 — and that no other section has the role (a name, through `SubAssemblyValidator`, until 2026-09-17; the Products screen doesn't use that validator any more).
    - **The Recipe Structure is tabs** (2026-09-15, at the user's request: "Sub-Assembly 1 | Sub-Assembly 2 | + Add Sub-Assembly | Packaging", with the edit form inside the sub-assembly's own tab and Remove kept as a button inside it, no close buttons on the tabs; the tab became the form outright on 2026-09-16, above).
      - **The tab strip stays put while the form scrolls** (2026-09-20, at the user's request: "in the recipe structure, is it possible that the tabs are static during the scroll?"). The Recipe tab used to wrap the whole card in one `ScrollViewer`, so scrolling to the bottom of a long sub-assembly form took the card header, the Add/Delete Sub-Assembly buttons and the tab strip off the top with it — and the tabs are how you move between sections, so they were gone exactly when a long form made you want them. **The scrolling moved inside the card**: `RecipeStructureCard`'s root is a four-row `Grid` (header, empty-state caption, tab strip, then the content frame at `Height="*"`) and the `ScrollViewer` wraps only what's inside the frame, so the frame takes whatever height is left and its content scrolls within it. The Recipe tab in `ProductsView` therefore holds the card in a plain `Border Padding="12"` — **it is the one details tab with no `ScrollViewer` of its own**, and putting one back would undo this. `FocusScrolling.SkipOnClick` moved in with the scrolling, onto the `Grid` inside the `ScrollViewer` (it has to sit inside, so the bring-into-view request is marked handled before it reaches the viewer). One deliberate change in look: the frame now fills the card down to the tab strip at the bottom instead of stopping where the form ends, which is how a tab panel normally reads. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor on Mini Ube-Cheese Ensaymada x 8: the header and the Dough/Topping/Filling/Packaging strip held still while the ingredient table scrolled up under them and the add-ins table came in from the bottom; switching to Packaging and back still worked.
      - **Undo and redo, one finished change at a time** (2026-09-20, at the user's request: "can we implement undo which undo the last change like adding a row, typing a quantiy or adding a subassembly?"). The fine-grained counterpart to Revert, which throws away everything since the last Save. **Products first**; Raw Materials and Sub-Assemblies are meant to follow with their own snapshot type and nothing else to write. The Settings tabs are deliberately **out of scope**: they write a row to the database on every Save, so undo there would be a compensating write rather than a restore. **Snapshot and restore, not inverse operations** (`ProductFormSnapshot`, `UndoHistory<TSnapshot>` and `IUndoRecorder`/`IUndoScreen` in `ViewModels/`): the state is too mutually derived for ~15 inverses to be safe, and a product form is only tens of small objects. `UndoHistory` takes `capture`, `showsSame` and `canRecord` as delegates so it's a property initializer, **not a constructor parameter** — one would have meant editing `CompositionRoot`, `CompositionRootTests` and the five test files that each rebuild their ViewModel by hand. **A step is bracketed, not diffed after the fact**: `BeginUndoStep` captures, the gesture runs, `EndUndoStep` captures again and keeps the step only if the two differ, so an abandoned gesture (Esc on the add row, an apply a rule refused) settles into nothing and needs no cancel path. Opening a step settles one already open, which is how the add row is covered — `SfDataGrid` raises nothing when an add commits. The recording points are `Controls/GridUndoSteps` (`CurrentCellBeginEdit`/`CurrentCellEndEdit`, `CellCheckBoxClick` for the Base tick, whose renderer never opens an edit, `RecordDeleting`/`RecordDeleted`, `AddNewRowInitiating`) on six grids, `Controls/FieldUndoStep` (keyboard focus in and out, so "typed and left" is literally the rule and no timer is needed) on fourteen editors, `AsUndoStep` around eleven mutating commands, and `CommitUsageFormOnLeave`, which is the only path that applies a section. **Three things the restore has to get right, each of which fails silently:** `IngredientLineRow.Restore` assigns the backing fields, because the `Grams` and `IsFormulaBase` setters null a typed percentage and `Percent` refuses one while `BaseWeight` is 0 — a state a row is allowed to be in (`IngredientLineRow` line 195 says so), so restoring through the setters loses the figure and only ticking the base again shows it; a pack is rebuilt as a fresh `ProductPack` **carrying its Id**, since `ProductPackRow` writes through to the entity and `SetPacksAsync` reads an Id of 0 as "add", which would delete the stored pack and its lines; and the restore never touches `_savedRecipe`/`_savedLines`/`_savedPacks`/`_savedCosting`/`_savedPackagingLinkKey`, which is why it can't call `ReplaceUsages`/`ReplaceLines`/`ReplacePacks` (each resets its own baseline) and why `ReplaceUsageItems` was split out of `ReplaceUsages`. **A new `IsRestoring` guard on `ViewModelBase`**, beside `IsRefreshing` and with `IsBusyApplying` for both: `IsRefreshing` covers neither the four collection change-sets, nor the costing recompute, nor `RecipeTabShown`. The eight existing guards widened to `IsBusyApplying`; the six unguarded subscriptions gained `IsRestoring` alone, leaving a load's behaviour byte-identical. Tabs are put back under `_arrangingRecipeTabs` so `RecipeTabShown` short-circuits and the form being left isn't applied again, and `_shownRecipeTab`/`_lastRecipeContentTab` are then set by hand — left stale, the *next* real tab change applies the wrong form. One `FormLinesChanged()` + `RecomputeCosts()` pass runs at the end, outside the guard. **Sections compare price-insensitively** (`SectionsShowSame`): a `LoadCommand` landing mid-gesture swaps every section for a repriced copy, and comparing by reference — or through `ProductUsageEdit.ShowsSameAs`, which reads the ingredient's cost — recorded a step for a reprice the user never made, whose undo would have put the stale prices back. **The tab showing is restored but not compared**, so moving between tabs records nothing on its own; the consequence is that undoing a change applied by leaving a tab puts the recipe back and leaves you where you navigated to. **Two ways a change of tab still recorded a step, fixed 2026-09-23** (the user's report: switching recipe tabs turned Undo on with nothing changed). Leaving a tab applies its form, and the apply closes the form, so `CommitUsageFormOnLeave`'s step always differed from where it began; it now ends with `UndoHistory.End(rebase)`, measured from its start moved on by `WithFormOf` (what Save compares), so only a change to the recipe is a step, while the step kept is still the full snapshot. And WPF selects a tab as it takes the cursor (`TabItem.OnPreviewGotKeyboardFocus`), before the field losing it hears so, so a field's step, or an add row's (which nothing closes), was still open while the form changed over to the other section; from the Packaging tab, where no apply opens a step of its own, that recorded the form opening as an edit. `RecipeTabPicked` runs a pick made in the strip between steps: it ends the open one on the tab being left and opens another on the tab arrived at, for the field to close. A pick the screen makes (`SelectRecipeTab`, the tabs rearranged, a restore) stays inside whatever step it belongs to. `Moving_between_the_recipe_tabs_is_not_something_to_undo` and `A_step_left_open_across_a_change_of_tab_records_nothing` fail on the code before. Covered by `ProductsViewModelUndoTests` (16), `UndoHistoryTests` (10) and five cases added to `IngredientLineRowTests`. **Raw Materials and Sub-Assemblies took the same pattern the same day**, which is what the delegate-driven `UndoHistory` was shaped for: each declares its own snapshot record (`RawMaterialFormSnapshot`, `SubAssemblyFormSnapshot`) with its own `ShowsSameAs`, a `History` property initialised the same way, the two `IUndoRecorder` methods and a `TryUndo`/`TryRedo` pair, and Undo/Redo buttons beside Save. **The Controls/ behaviours, the Ctrl+Z handler and `UndoHistory` itself needed no change at all** — they only ever see `IUndoRecorder`/`IUndoScreen`. Two screen-specific points. Raw Materials' **price history stays outside undo**: its Delete writes to the database on the spot, so there is nothing unsaved to put back, and `DeletePriceCommand` is deliberately the one mutating command not wrapped. Its **alternative units need the pack treatment** — `RawMaterialUnitRow` writes through to the stored `RawMaterialUnit` and Save hands those very entities to the repository, so a restore rebuilds each as a fresh entity carrying its Id; `BrandPriceRow` is carried as `(Brand, UnitKey, Price)` only, since its Quantity has no field of its own and reads back through the unit row, which is already in the snapshot. **A trap worth remembering: the history has to be constructed before any subscription in the constructor.** `WhenAnyValue` fires straight away with the value it finds, and Sub-Assemblies' `SelectedRow` subscription reaches `ClearForm`, which clears the history — four tests failed with a null `History` until it was moved to the top of the constructor. Covered by `SubAssembliesViewModelUndoTests` (6) and `RawMaterialsViewModelUndoTests` (7). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor: Ctrl+Z inside a DoubleTextBox on Products, a name field on Raw Materials, and a grams cell on Sub-Assemblies, each taking back the whole change with its recomputed figures; the database was untouched throughout.
      - **Save is offered only when there is something to save** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "in Raw Materials, if there is no modification made, the Save button should be disabled. The same can be made to Products."). Built on the undo snapshots rather than a second set of dirty flags: `UndoHistory` keeps a **settled snapshot** beside its stacks, `MarkClean()` takes the form as it stands for it, `Clear()` drops it, and `IsDirty` says whether the form still matches. `SaveCommand`'s `canExecute` is `History.WhenAnyValue(h => h.IsDirty)` on both screens. Four things it has to get right. **The settled snapshot is taken where the details land** — `ShowDetails` on Raw Materials, the end of `LoadProductDetailAsync` and of `StartNewProduct` on Products — never where the header is filled in, since the grids arrive a moment later and a form measured before them reads as changed when they do. **It is re-read on every change, not only when an undo step settles**: a step closes on the way out of a field, and a disabled Save swallows the very click that would have enabled it, so the screens call `RefreshDirty()` from `this.Changed` and from each editable collection. Those collections are watched through their **`PropertyChanged`, not their `CollectionChanged`** — an `ObservableCollection` only enforces its re-entrancy guard once more than one handler is listening, and the recipe's tab mirror does change a collection from inside its own notification, so a second `CollectionChanged` listener turns a tolerated re-entrant change into a throw. **An edit the form has not seen yet keeps Save offered** (`IUndoRecorder.IsEditPending`, set by `GridUndoSteps` around an open cell and by `FieldUndoStep.LandsOnLeave` on the one box bound `UpdateSourceTrigger=LostFocus`): a cell's value reaches its row only when the edit ends, and the click that ends it is the click that saves. **Products needs a narrower comparison than undo's**: `ShowsSameAs` includes where the form is pointed, and selecting a section's tab opens its form over the previous one, so `SavesSameAs` drops the Create Variant name and `RebaseSettledForm` moves the settled snapshot's form and tab fields onto the form now showing (`WithFormOf`) whenever one opens, closes or is added — never the recipe, packaging, costing or header, so a rebase can never hide a change a Save would write. The trap from the bullet above bit again on the way in: Products built its `History` beside the Undo and Redo commands, after the `SelectedProduct` subscriptions, and those fire as they are wired — the blank-form `MarkClean()` hit a null `History`, and the `NullReferenceException` killed the detail-load subscription for the whole session, so every product opened with an empty recipe and nothing was logged. The history now goes above those subscriptions, as it already did on the other two screens. **Sub-Assemblies took the same pattern on 2026-09-23**, and needed the least of the three: its snapshot holds nothing but the form's own data, so `ShowsSameAs` is the dirty comparison (no `savesSame`, no rebase), the settled snapshot is taken at the end of `ClearForm` and of `LoadIntoFormAsync`, and its history was already built before the subscriptions. Settings stays out, as it does for undo: it writes a row per Save, so there is no unsaved form to measure. Covered by five cases each in `SubAssembliesViewModelUndoTests`, `RawMaterialsViewModelUndoTests` and `ProductsViewModelUndoTests` (the Products set includes moving between recipe tabs, which is the rebase), plus six in `UndoHistoryTests`; each asserts `SaveCommand.CanExecute`, which is what the button binds. **A refresh can legitimately fill in a form, and two places do**: Sub-Assemblies gives a brand-new form the first role once the roles have loaded, and Products puts the costing defaults on an untouched new product. The screen opens before either has arrived, so the blank form was settled without them and Save came up offered on a form nobody had touched (Products' was invisible in the dev database, whose defaults are all zero). Both now `Rebase` the one field the refresh supplied - the role, the costing - rather than re-marking the whole form, so a role or a costing the user picked still counts as a change. `Showing_the_tab_again_is_not_something_to_save` and `Save_is_not_offered_on_the_blank_form_the_screen_opens_with` guard both on all three screens; the Sub-Assemblies and Products ones fail on the code without the rebase.
      - **The sub-assembly form's Apply and Cancel went, and the header gained Revert** (2026-09-20, at the user's request: "there is a Cancel and Apply button in the sub-assemblies in the products. Let the Save command handle all the changes to the product"). Apply was already redundant: `CommitUsageFormOnLeave` applies the open form whenever its tab is left, and `SaveCommand` applies it too, and neither ever wrote to the database — `ApplyForm` only puts the section into `UsageEdits`, so Save was already the one thing that persists. Both stay; only the `ApplyUsageCommand` and `CancelUsageCommand` commands and their two buttons went. Cancel was the form's only discard, and `RemoveUsageCommand` couldn't stand in for it (it's disabled on the New Sub-Assembly tab and casts to `SubAssemblyRecipeTab`), so **`RevertCommand` took its place on the product header**, beside Save: it reads the picked product's detail again (`LoadIntoForm` then `LoadProductDetailAsync`), which replaces the recipe, packaging, packs and costing wholesale, so everything staged but unsaved goes — a section being added or edited included; an unsaved new product goes back to being a blank one (`StartNewProduct`). It confirms first through the same `ConfirmDelete` interaction the deletes use, whose MessageBox title is now "Confirm" rather than "Confirm Delete", since it no longer only asks about deletes. **Discarding is now all-or-nothing for the whole product rather than per section** — the user's choice, over widening Remove Sub-Assembly to cover an add. The form keeps Make a Copy, which was on that row too. The ViewModel tests that drove the flow through Apply now leave the tab or press Save; ones that leaned on Apply leaving the form open had to wait for Save's reload, since Save reloads the product and the tab and usage that come back are fresh objects. `Revert_reads_the_product_back_as_it_was_saved_and_drops_a_sub_assembly_being_added` covers the new command.
      - **Packaging comes in through Add Sub-Assembly** (2026-09-18). A new product's Recipe Structure is empty (`HasRecipeTabs` false: the card shows a caption saying how to start instead of the tab strip). The form offers the **Packaging role** in New and Copy, and packaging sources in the Link/Copy picker — "Gift Box Set — Sub-Assemblies (Packaging)" for a set, "Packaging — Cream Loaf" for another product's (`LinkableSubAssemblyOption` is now a sub-assembly or a product, keyed by `Key`) — both only while the product has no packaging (`HasPackaging`), since it has one. With either (`IsFormPackaging`), the form swaps its ingredients, add-ins and piece size for a packaging table (`FormPackagingLines`, the Packaging tab's columns; read-only for a link), and Apply (`ApplyPackagingForm`, via `ApplyForm`, which Save and leaving the tab use too) fills `LineEdits`, sets or clears the link (`_packagingLinkTarget`), and adds the Packaging tab (`SetHasPackaging`), always last. **Delete Sub-Assembly** works on the Packaging tab too, taking out the lines or the link (`RemovePackaging`); the packs keep theirs. With no tabs, nothing is selected (`PackagingTabIfShown` replaces every fallback that used to land on Packaging). Covered by `ProductsViewModelPackagingTests`.
      - `ProductsViewModel.RecipeTabs` is what the `TabControlExt` binds: a `SubAssemblyRecipeTab` for each of `UsageEdits` at the same index, then `AddSubAssemblyTab` and `PackagingTab` (`ViewModels/RecipeTab.cs`; each gives its header and Segoe Fluent glyph, a link for a linked sub-assembly). `MirrorUsageTabs` keeps them in step from `UsageEdits.CollectionChanged`. A replaced section keeps its tab (`Usage` is set on it), so Apply on an edit (`ReplaceInRecipeOrder`: replace in place, then move to where it sorts), Make a Copy and a refresh never move the tab showing. Since 2026-09-19 another product's sections take over the tabs the same way (`ReplaceUsages`; see "Picking another product swaps its details in one pass" above). The tabs have no content of their own (an empty `ContentTemplate`, and the tab control's `BorderThickness="0"`): since 2026-09-19 `RecipeStructureCard` holds **one** `UsageFormPanel` and **one** `PackagingSectionView` under the tab strip, built once, shown by `ProductsViewModel.IsUsageFormShowing` (a sub-assembly's tab, or New Sub-Assembly's while its form is open) and `IsPackagingShowing` (the Packaging tab), inside a stand-in for the tab control's content frame. Before, each tab's content was an implicit `DataTemplate` by type, so the form and its grids were built again whenever a tab showed — on every product picked, since each product brings new tab objects: about 80 ms of templates plus the grids' row generation and `ItemsSource` setup, measured with a CPU trace. The stand-in frame matches the old one pixel for pixel (checked against the previous build's screenshots: only four corner pixels a side differ): a childless `Border` with the tab control's own `BorderBrush`, `1,0,1,1`, `SnapsToDevicePixels`, *behind* the content rather than around it (a snapped Border shifts what it holds by the sub-pixel fraction, which redrew the form's box edges), and the content inset as the tab control inset it: `10,6,10,10` for the form (its 8, plus the control's 2 at the sides and bottom, less 2 at the top), `2,-2,2,2` for the packaging.
      - `SelectedRecipeTab` is the ViewModel's, and `_shownRecipeTab` is the tab whose form is open, so `RecipeTabShown` knows which one a change is leaving. Leaving a tab applies its form (above); showing a sub-assembly's opens the form on it, showing New Sub-Assembly opens a fresh add form (one already adding is left alone), and an add form nothing was entered in just closes (`IsUsageFormUntouched`). Save shows the sub-assembly's tab, where the form stays open on what was applied (the form's own Apply and Cancel went on 2026-09-20, below); deleting the sub-assembly showing shows the next one, else the one before, else Packaging. Reloading a product (after Save, a new product's first included) shows the same sub-assembly's tab (by id, or by name for one saved since), or Packaging if that was showing, else the first sub-assembly's; another product's recipe opens on its first tab (since 2026-09-17, below), else Packaging, which a new product starts on. `_arrangingRecipeTabs` ignores the tab strip's own pick while the sections are rebuilt.
      - **Save leaves an untouched add form alone**, which has nothing to save (Save applies any other open form, as before, and since 2026-09-17 shows its tab, so an add applied by Save stays the tab showing through the reload). An add form opened before the roles loaded takes the first role when they arrive.
      - **The tab control mustn't pick tabs itself**: `CloseMode="Delete"` and `SelectOnCreatingNewItem="False"`. With its default `CloseMode="Hide"`, removing a tab reselects the last tab shown before (read from the decompiled `TabControlExt.OnItemsChanged`, `SelectionStack`): removing Glaze just after cancelling an add jumped to Add Sub-Assembly instead of the next sub-assembly. Nothing can be closed from the strip either way (`CloseButtonType="Hide"`), nor dragged into another order (`AllowDragDrop="False"`, since 2026-09-17, below). `TabScrollButtonVisibility="Auto"` shows the scroll arrows only when the tabs don't fit.
      - **A bug fixed on the way: Apply after a refresh added the edited sub-assembly a second time.** A refresh (Products shown again, e.g. after repricing an ingredient) replaces a section whose costs changed with a new copy; the form still pointed at the old one, so Apply removed nothing and inserted the edit beside the refreshed copy. `MirrorUsageTabs` now moves `EditingUsage` onto the replacement. The ViewModel test for it fails without that line.
      - **A click inside the tab no longer scrolls the Recipe tab first** (`Controls/FocusScrolling.cs`, `controls:FocusScrolling.SkipOnClick="True"` on its `RecipeStructureCard`). A focused control asks the ScrollViewer to bring all of itself into view; the form's 200 px lines table, now lower in the page, often wasn't, so the page scrolled between mouse down and up and the click on the add row was lost. While the left button is down the request is dropped; Tab still scrolls.
      - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against a migrated copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo`: Cream Loaf's four sub-assemblies then Add Sub-Assembly and Packaging; Edit opened the form in Sweet Dough's tab and it stayed there while other tabs were shown; Cream Loaf Mini's linked tabs had the link glyph; a Glaze sub-assembly typed in the Add tab (Butter Icing 100 g, 5 g a piece) was applied into its own tab (material cost ₱10.3229 → ₱14.1479), saved (Glaze stayed selected through the reload) and stored; Cancel went back to Glaze; Remove on Glaze showed Sweet Dough, Make a Copy kept the tab (link glyph to list, badge gone), removing the last one showed the one before; a new product's Packaging tab showed its empty state and the packaging form. ViewModel tests cover the tab order, selection after load, Apply, Cancel and Remove, the untouched add form on Save, and the refresh-while-editing fix.
      - **Add and Delete Sub-Assembly became buttons, and a linked recipe's add-ins wait behind Show Add-Ins** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "add a command button for showing Add-ins to a link subassembly tab. additionaly the first tab should follow the role order defined in the settings … removing the + Add Sub-Assembly tab and have it as a button alongside the delete subassembly").
        - **Tabs out of role order came from dragging them, not from the sort.** ViewModel tests passed for every path that orders the tabs (a load, a role reordered in Settings then a refresh, adds made out of order, a role changed on Apply), and an off-screen `TabControlExt` drew Insert, Move and Replace in `ItemsSource` order. But `TabControlExt` coerces `AllowDragDrop` to true whenever `ItemsSource` is bound, unless it's set False (`OnCoerceAllowDragDrop`), and a tab dropped on another is removed from the bound list and inserted at the drop (`TabLayoutPanel.MoveItem`; both read from the decompiled 34.2.7). That took `RecipeTabs` out of role order and out of step with `UsageEdits`, which `MirrorUsageTabs` mirrors index for index, so later edits could land on the wrong tab. `AllowDragDrop="False"` stops a drag where both of its starts check it (`OnPreviewMouseLeftButtonDown`, `OnMouseMove`); the scroll arrows don't depend on it, only the drag's own auto-scroll buttons (`EnableAutoScroll`, off). Verified in the app: dragging Filling onto Topping left the tabs where they were.
        - **The dev database's Almond Cheese Bread was in order for the roles it has**: its "Topping" sub-assembly has the Dough role (the add form's Role starts on the first role), so it ties with the linked Variety Bread Dough and sorts first by name.
        - **Add Sub-Assembly** inserts `AddSubAssemblyTab`, now headed "New Sub-Assembly", before Packaging and shows it. The form showing is applied first, as leaving its tab would; one a rule refuses keeps its tab, and nothing opens. Since 2026-09-20 it is a drop-down of the three ways in and stays offered while New Sub-Assembly shows, since that is the only place the way can be changed: another item over an add form nothing was entered in (`IsUsageFormUntouched`) only changes its way, keeping the tab, while over one already typed into it applies what's there and starts another, as a second Add Sub-Assembly always did. That tab goes once another shows: after Save, or a click on another tab (what was typed is applied; nothing typed, the form just closes). A new product starts on Packaging, its only tab.
          - **Taking the tab away has to wait for the tab strip.** This screen's own `WhenAnyValue` hears of a `SelectedRecipeTab` change before the strip's binding does, so taking the tab out of `RecipeTabs` there, on a pick made by the screen, took away the tab the strip still showed, and the strip picked a neighbour of its own (Packaging, not the Filling picked; reproduced off-screen with a real `TabControlExt`). `SelectRecipeTab` sets `_selectingRecipeTab` while its pick is on its way, and its second `RecipeTabShown` takes the tab away; a click, which the strip has already made, takes it away at once (`RemoveAddSubAssemblyTab`, with the strip's pick ignored under `_arrangingRecipeTabs`).
          - **Another product's recipe opens on its first tab.** The permanent Add tab used to take the strip's pick as `ClearDetails` removed the old sub-assemblies, and "else the first sub-assembly's" followed; without it the strip lands on Packaging, and "Packaging if that was showing" would have opened every product there. `ClearDetails` now sets `_isRecipeClearedForAnother`, and `ReplaceUsages` reselects with no previous tab. A new product's first Save isn't another product's recipe: `ReloadSavedProductAsync` sets `_detailProductId` before selecting it, so it keeps its tab as any reload does.
        - **Delete Sub-Assembly** (`RemoveUsageCommand`, parameterless now) takes out the sub-assembly whose tab shows, and is only offered on one; Remove left the form's button row. Still nothing to confirm: the sub-assembly goes when the product is saved (Save deletes the product's own; a linked one stays with its owner), and one others link is refused on the spot.
        - **Show Add-Ins** sits under a linked recipe, beside the caption saying whose its lines are (`IsFormOfferingAddIns`), and opens the add-ins table (`IsFormAddInsExpanded`, `ToggleFormAddInsCommand`): "Hide Add-Ins" while it's open, "Show Add-Ins (2)" while two are out of sight, with a chevron that points as `ExpandChevronStyle`'s does. A link's tab opens with the table open only when it has add-ins, and a refused add-in opens it (`CompleteAddIns`), so the line the message is about is in sight. Add-ins out of sight still count in the batch. The Linked Recipe heading binds `IsFormOfferingAddIns`, so it doesn't come and go with the table.
        - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved: a new product showed only Packaging, with Delete Sub-Assembly greyed out; Almond Cheese Bread showed Topping, Variety Bread Dough, Filling and Packaging; Add Sub-Assembly put New Sub-Assembly before Packaging, both buttons greyed out, and a click on Variety Bread Dough took it away; Show Add-Ins opened the table and read Hide Add-Ins; the drag above moved nothing; Delete Sub-Assembly on Filling showed Variety Bread Dough and took the material cost from ₱67.1820 to ₱44.9985; Cheese Roll then opened on its Dough, and Almond Cheese Bread reopened on Topping with Filling back. ViewModel tests: New Sub-Assembly's tab through a click away, Apply and Cancel, Show Add-Ins including a refused add-in, and a product picked from a sub-assembly's tab (the strip's pick simulated), with the tab tests updated.
      - **Tabs are called by their roles** (2026-09-17, at the user's request; the domain side is under SubAssembly above), **and by their names too where a role repeats** (2026-09-18). Every sub-assembly tab's header, linked ones too (the user's choice), is `ProductUsageEdit.SectionName`: its `RoleName`, or "Filling: Ube" while `IsRoleRepeated` (its `Label` beside the role, when it has one). So are the form's heading and title, the Recipe Cost Overview's slices and the Unit Conversion Overview's batch ("Filling: Ube batch"). `ProductUsageEdit` is a `ReactiveObject` for that flag alone: `MarkRepeatedRoles` sets it on every section whenever `UsageEdits` changes (after `MirrorUsageTabs`), and refreshes the tabs' headers (`SubAssemblyRecipeTab.RefreshHeader`). `Name` is a product's sub-assembly's own name (this product's, or the owner's for a linked one); a linked standalone one keeps its name in `StandaloneName`, which its badge reads ("Variety Bread Dough, from Sub-Assemblies"), and `DisplayName` gives it where the recipe isn't plain (the add-ins caption and messages).
        - **The form has Role and an optional Name** (Name since 2026-09-18, gone from 2026-09-17), side by side under the add form's pickers (`EditingUsageRole`, `EditingUsageName`); packaging has no name. Role offers every recipe role (`FormRoleOptions`, `RefreshFormRoleOptions`, refreshed with `RefreshLinkableOptions`, which puts the pick back since the picker can drop it as its list changes), and the add form starts on the first role the recipe doesn't have yet, else the first, so sections can be added in role order without picking; a copy takes its source's role and name. Apply refuses sections it can't tell apart (`EnsureSectionsDistinct` in the view model, before `SetRecipeAsync`'s own check) — but only for a section that changed, and never for a linked one being edited, whose role and name are its owner's. A name typed alone counts as an entry, so leaving the add form with one applies it.
        - **Order and reselection**: `RecipeOrder` breaks a tie in role by saved id, a new one last; `IsSameSubAssembly` finds an unsaved own section after a reload by its role and name. Make a Copy keeps what the section went by (its `Label` while its role repeats), and the link picker leaves out only the sub-assemblies the recipe already links.
        - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against the migrated dev database, nothing saved: Almond Cheese Bread showed Dough (linked, "Variety Bread Dough, from Sub-Assemblies"), Dough, Filling and Packaging — its old Topping is saved in the Dough role — with Dough, Dough and Filling slices and two "Dough batch" boxes; the own Dough's form had only Role, whose list was Dough, Topping, Inclusion and Finishing; Add Sub-Assembly's form had no Name and started on Topping. ViewModel tests: the tab headers, the role offered to an add and an edit, a role the recipe has refused on Apply and on leaving a tab, and a link in a taken role not offered. Persistence tests: one per role across own and linked, an old name cleared on save, a role change refused where a linking product has it (owned and standalone), and a clone of a source with two in one role refused.
    - **Recipe lines are typed into the table** (2026-09-14, at the user's request), in the sub-assembly form and in Raw Materials' Bill of Materials card alike. Until then each had an Ingredient (or Component) picker, a Grams box and Add Line under a read-only grid.
      - The grid is an editable `SfDataGrid`: `AddNewRowPosition="Bottom"`, `EditTrigger="OnTap"`, `AllowDeleting` (the Delete key removes the selected line; Remove Line stays under it), a `GridMultiColumnDropDownList` on `IngredientId` and a `NumericColumn` on `Grams`.
      - **The add row is a plain empty row, with no "Click here to add an ingredient"** (2026-09-16, at the user's request, on every grid). The Windows11Light `AddNewRowControl` template covers the row, until it's edited, with a light-gray band (`PART_AddNewRowTextBorder`) holding `AddNewRowText`, and an empty `AddNewRowText` only brings back the localized "Click here to add new row" (`SfDataGrid.OnAddNewRowTextChanged`). So `App`'s static constructor registers a class handler on `AddNewRowControl.Loaded` (`HideAddNewRowText`) that collapses the band; the grids set no `AddNewRowText`. The band's `Edit` visual state collapses it too, so the local value holds either way. The row then looks like the filler rows under it, with column lines, and clicking it still starts a line (the filler rows stay inert). The user chose this over keeping an empty gray band. Since the band no longer covers the row, the grid's current-cell outline shows on it when the current cell is there (after Enter commits a line). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor: Sub-Assemblies' lines table and Butter Icing, Special Ensaymada's Bill of Materials showed the add row as an empty row with column lines; clicking it began a line ("salt", Tab, 5, Enter committed Salt, Fine at 0.08, not saved), and the next add row was plain too.
      - **The Base column is the app's first `GridCheckBoxColumn`** (2026-09-16, baker's percentage), the last column (moved there from between Ingredient and Grams on 2026-09-17, at the user's request), with a read-only `NumericColumn` "Baker's %" (1 decimal) after Grams. It's on Sub-Assemblies' lines table and the Products form's lines table; the add-ins table has the percentage but no tick. On Products its `AllowEditing` binds `IsFormEditingLines`, the same as the grid's, so a linked recipe shows its owner's tick greyed out. **One click toggles it** (with `EditTrigger="OnTap"`; verified in the app on the secondary monitor: ticking Water beside the flour moved the base to 1,450 g and every percentage with it, flour and water reading 69.0 and 31.0), and `IngredientLineRow.ApplyFormulaBase` pushes the new base to every row as the tick, the grams or the lines change. **The add row shows no tick** (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "hide the blank tick box"), so it stays the plain empty row the other grids show: `controls:GridAddNewRow.HideCheckBoxes` swaps the grid's `"CheckBox"` renderer for `AddRowHiddenCheckBoxRenderer`, which hides the checkbox where `DataGrid.IsAddNewIndex` and shows it everywhere else — the way the renderer's own doc comment shows it being replaced. `OnUpdateEditBinding` just calls `OnInitializeEditElement`, so the one override covers rows as they recycle. A new line gets its tick once it's committed. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor, with the typing both ways above: the add row showed no box; typing 65 into Water's Baker's % made it 650 g and bold; doubling the flour to 2,000 g took Water to 1,300 g at 65% while every line typed in grams kept them (Washed Sugar stayed 250 g, 25.0 to 12.5); typing into the flour's own percentage did nothing. None of it was saved.
      - **The ingredient cell autocompletes** (at the user's request, replacing a `GridComboBoxColumn` the same day; Syncfusion's "Auto-complete support" for this column, as in its `GridDropDownAndReadOnlyColumnsDemo.xaml`). `DisplayMember="Name"`, `ValueMember="Id"`, `ItemsSource="{Binding Ingredients}"` (a column takes it from the grid's DataContext). `AllowAutoComplete` picks an ingredient as it's typed, and `AllowIncrementalFiltering` narrows the dropdown to the matches. Since 2026-09-15 the dropdown opens as you type and lists the names that contain the text, not only those that start with it (see "Names that contain the typed text" below). Text that matches nothing clears the cell when you leave it.
      - Its dropdown is a small grid of Name, Category and Cost per g (the buffered cost, 4 decimals), styled by the Windows11Light theme (`SfMultiColumnDropDownControl`). `PopUpMinWidth` and `PopUpWidth` alone were ignored, and the popup took the cell's width, clipping the cost. It needs `IsAutoPopupSize="False"` with `PopUpWidth="380"`/`PopUpHeight="260"`, plus `GridColumnSizer="Star"` so Name takes what the fixed-width columns leave.
      - Keyboard entry: click the add row once, type part of a name (the dropdown opens), Tab to Grams, Enter commits the line and moves to a new add row. With the dropdown open, Enter picks the highlighted row and closes it, and the next Enter commits the line.
      - **Three bugs in Syncfusion's drop-down cell, fixed by `controls:AutoCompleteDropDown.IsEnabled="True"` on the grid plus `AllowNullInput="True"` on the column** (`Controls/AutoCompleteDropDown.cs`, reported by the user on first use, the causes read from the decompiled `SfMultiColumnDropDownControl` and `GridCellMultiColumnDropDownRenderer` 34.2.7). The attached property swaps the grid's `"MultiColumnDropDown"` cell renderer for `AutoCompleteDropDownRenderer`.
        - **A new row started as the previous row's ingredient.** One editor serves every row of the column. With `AllowNullInput` off (the default), an empty text box, and a commit whose text doesn't match, both fall back to the control's `PreviousSelectedItem`: the last row's pick. Its text box also kept what was last typed, because the text is only cleared when the stale `SearchText` is empty too. `AllowNullInput="True"` (`IngredientId` is nullable) stops the fallback, and the renderer's `OnInitializeEditElement` sets the editor's text to the row's own ingredient, or nothing, as each edit begins.
        - **A name typed and tabbed out of at once wasn't picked.** Autocomplete runs `FilterDelay` (500 ms by default) after a keystroke, and a quick Tab committed before it ran, leaving the line with no ingredient. The renderer's editor has `FilterDelay = 0`.
        - **The pop-up covered the cell being typed into.** The control places it in `ProcessOnPopupPositionCalculation`, measuring the room below against `SystemParameters.VirtualScreenHeight` (the whole desktop's height, not the cell's monitor). On the 768 px secondary monitor beside a 1080 px primary it opened below regardless, and Windows pushed it back up over the cell. `ScreenAwareDropDownControl` overrides that method: it takes the monitor's work area (`MonitorFromPoint`/`GetMonitorInfoW`) and opens below when the pop-up fits, above otherwise, using the pop-up's drawn height (its border and resize grip make it about 40 px taller than `PopUpHeight`, which the control's own placement also missed). The renderer creates it in `OnCreateEditUIElement`, repeating the base renderers' setup (no error template, `WantsMouseInput`, a 1 px border).
        - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor with real keystrokes: "fir" + Tab straight away picked First Class Flour; clicking the next row opened it empty, and "u" + Tab picked Unsalted Butter; clicking an existing row showed its own ingredient; F4 on a row near the bottom opened the list entirely above the cell; the BOM card did the same, and saved Cake Flour (typed "cak" + Tab) and Unsalted Butter.
