      - **Names that contain the typed text, in a dropdown that opens as you type** (2026-09-15, at the user's request: like `SfTextBoxExt` with `SuggestionMode="Contains"`; the first key typed didn't open the list, and only names starting with the text were found). All three columns set `SearchCondition="Contains"`, and the renderer's editor sets `AllowImmediatePopup`, which opens the dropdown on the key press (the column has no property for it, so the renderer sets it when it creates the editor, like `FilterDelay`).
        - **`SearchCondition` only ever filtered the dropdown's rows.** The pick as you type is a separate path (the control's `ProcessTextChanged`, read from the decompiled `SfMultiColumnDropDownControl` 34.2.7): it takes the first item for which `ProcessAppendText` is true, a hard-coded `StartsWith`, then writes that item's name into the box through `ProcessAppendStringList` and selects the part not yet typed. `ScreenAwareDropDownControl` overrides both, only when the condition is `Contains`.
        - **Which name is picked** (the user's choice, over always the list's top row): the first name starting with the text, or, when none does, the first containing it. "ss" lists First Class Flour, SSL and Third Class Flour and picks SSL; "flour" picks All Purpose Flour. `ProcessAppendText` is asked of each name in list order, so it asks once per text change whether any name starts with the text (reading `DisplayMember` itself, since the control's own lookup is private).
        - **The box keeps what was typed.** A name the text sits inside can't be completed in place, so `ProcessAppendStringList` hands back the typed text (`SearchText`) instead of the name.
        - **Leaving the cell keeps the pick.** `AllowAutoComplete` stays on, so `SelectedItem` follows the typing and Tab commits it. But leaving the cell runs `CommitValue(false)`, which keeps a pick only when the box holds its whole name, and would have cleared a pick made from "ss". The override commits it as Tab does.
        - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor with real keystrokes, on the Sub-Assemblies form against the dev database (nothing saved): "s" opened the dropdown at once, listing the names containing it with Salt, Fine highlighted; "ss" narrowed it to First Class Flour, SSL and Third Class Flour, with SSL highlighted and "ss" still in the box; focus leaving the app then committed the line as SSL. On a new form, "flour" listed the four flours with All Purpose Flour highlighted, Tab took it to Grams on the line, and 10 + Enter committed it at 0.52. Not yet checked in the app: Down + Enter, clicking another cell, "zzz", F4, and the Bill of Materials card and product form (same renderer and column settings).
      - **The key that starts an edit is typed into the box** (2026-09-19, reported by the user: on the Ingredient cell, reached by keyboard, a letter had to be typed twice before it showed). A key typed on a cell that isn't being edited starts the edit, and the grid keeps its character in `PreviewInputText` (`GridCellRendererBase.OnPreviewTextInput`), since the editor doesn't exist yet to take it. `GridCellTextBoxRenderer.OnEditElementLoaded` writes it into its box; `GridCellMultiColumnDropDownRenderer`'s (decompiled 34.2.7) only focuses the box and sets it to null, so the first key was lost. `AutoCompleteDropDownRenderer.OnEditElementLoaded` now takes it before the base drops it and, once the editor has finished loading (`Dispatcher.BeginInvoke` at `Input`; typed during `Loaded`, it showed but neither filtered nor picked), selects the box's text (so it replaces the row's name, as a text cell's does), opens the list (`IsDropDownOpen`, as the control's own key-down does for `AllowImmediatePopup`) and types it in as a `TextComposition`, so it filters and picks like any key. The box is reached through the template's `PART_TextBox` (`ScreenAwareDropDownControl.TypingBox`), since the control's `Editor` is internal. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor on a new sub-assembly (nothing saved): Grams on the add row, Esc, Left onto Ingredient, one "a": the box read "a", the list opened on the names containing it and All Purpose Flour was picked (₱0.0519 a gram). Before the fix the same "a" only opened the editor.
      - Both screens' rows are `IngredientLineRow` (`ViewModels/IngredientLineRow.cs`), a `ReactiveObject` with a parameterless constructor for the add row. It replaced `SubAssemblyLineEdit` in `FormLines` (applying copies the rows into `SubAssemblyLineEdit`s, so editing the form never touches a section's lines) and `IngredientBomLineEdit` in `BomLineEdits`. The picker sets `IngredientId`, and a DynamicData `AutoRefresh` subscription on the collection points `Ingredient` at the screen's copy (`IngredientLineRow.Resolve`), which prices the row. It maps by id rather than by object because BOM lines load their component from a separate query, and a refresh swaps in new ingredient copies. On the Products form, the same subscription recomputes the batch weight, the other piece-size figure and the preview.
      - **A line being typed into the add row prices itself** (2026-09-15, at the user's request: its Cost read 0.00 until the next row). The add row's item isn't in the screen's collection until it's committed, so the `AutoRefresh` subscription never reached it. `controls:GridAddNewRow.ItemFactory` (`Controls/GridAddNewRow.cs`) hands the grid's `AddNewRowInitiating` the screen's own item (`e.NewObject`, settable in 34.2.7, confirmed by reflection): `SubAssembliesViewModel.NewLine` and `ProductsViewModel.NewFormLine` make an `IngredientLineRow` with a lookup, which finds its ingredient when `IngredientId` is set. The Cost cell updates as soon as the grams are entered. Verified in the app on both tables ("Corn" + Tab, 50 + Tab: 2.41 while still in the add row). The batch totals still count committed lines only. The BOM card has no Cost column, so it's unchanged.
      - `IngredientLineRow.CompleteLines` checks the rows on Apply (and on Save for the BOM). It leaves out a blank row, and refuses grams with no ingredient, an ingredient with no grams, or an ingredient on two rows, each with a message naming it ("Enter how many grams of Cake Flour go into Half Filled, or delete its line.").
      - **`controls:GridEditCommit.OnFocusLeave`** (`Controls/GridEditCommit.cs`): a cell or add row being typed into only reaches its row when the edit ends, and the grid doesn't end it when the user clicks away. So a line typed just before Apply or Save would have been missed. When keyboard focus leaves the grid it ends the edit, then commits the add row through the grid's `GetAddNewRowController()`, or cancels it when every column still has a new row's value (otherwise an add row the user only clicked became an empty line). Three things came out of checking it in the app. Focus in a cell's dropdown counts as inside the grid (the dropdown is a `Popup`, followed back through its `PlacementTarget`; without that, opening the dropdown ended the edit and closed it). Ending the add row takes focus with it, so focus is handed back to the element that was clicked (the first click on Weight per Piece was lost otherwise). `ButtonAdv` takes focus, so Apply and Save both commit first.
      - **Leaving the add row for another line drops it when nothing was typed in it** (2026-09-16, reported by the user: clicking the empty row under the lines and back onto a line added an empty line). `GridEditCommit` only covers focus leaving the grid. Inside it, the grid commits the add row whenever the current cell moves off it, whatever it holds: a click on another line (`GridCurrentCellManager.HandlePointerOperation`), an arrow key or Enter (`GridSelectionController`'s key handling), all through the selection controller's `CommitAddNew` (read from the decompiled 34.2.7). **`controls:GridAddNewRow.DropWhenUntouched="True"`** (`Controls/GridAddNewRow.cs`) replaces the grid's `SelectionController` with `UntouchedAddRowSelectionController`, a `GridSelectionController` whose `CommitAddNew` cancels an untouched row (`GetAddNewRowController().CancelAddNew()`, as the grid's own Esc does) and commits any other. "Untouched" is the same test as `GridEditCommit`'s, now shared as `GridAddNewRow.IsUntouched`. The grid makes its controller only in its constructor (or when `SelectionUnit` changes, which these grids never set), so the replacement stays. A row with anything in it is still added when left, as on Apply and Save: the user asked for the addition to be cancelled when the row is left, reported for an empty row. On all three tables. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor on a new, unsaved Sub-Assemblies form: after a Salt, Fine line, clicking the empty row and back onto the line left one line; Water picked in the add row and left with a click on Salt was added; Enter on an untouched add row added nothing.
      - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor, against a migrated copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo`, with real clicks and keystrokes. A new product's sub-assembly was entered by keyboard (First Class Flour 1,000 g at ₱40.90, Unsalted Butter 150 g left uncommitted), a weight per piece of 50 typed with one click, then Apply and Save: 1,150 g, ₱155.65, ₱6.7674 a piece. Editing it loaded both lines by name. Delete removed the butter, 1,200 g typed over the flour was picked up by a direct click on Apply, and Save stored one 1,200 g line. The half-filled line above was refused. A new made ingredient's BOM line (Cake Flour, 500 g, typed and saved straight from the cell) saved and reloaded at ₱0.0460 a gram. After the switch to autocomplete, again: "fir" completed to First Class Flour and "u" with the dropdown open filtered it to Unsalted Butter (both lines committed, ₱40.90 and ₱114.75), "zzz" cleared on Tab, and a BOM line typed as "Unsa" saved as Unsalted Butter, 250 g, at ₱0.7500 a gram.
      - **Syncfusion's classic `GridControl` (`Syncfusion.Grid.WPF`) was tried first, at the user's request, and dropped.** It's a cell-indexed, spreadsheet-style grid: rows come from `Model[row, col]` or `QueryCellInfo`/`CommitCellInfo` handlers, not an `ItemsSource`, so every line read and write would be code-behind. More decisively, the Windows11Light theme package has no style for it (it covers `SfDataGrid`, `SfTreeGrid` and the rest; the Sample Browser only ever shows GridControl's demos in its default theme). A scratch render beside an `SfDataGrid` showed the Excel look: gray headers and row-header column, dense rows, hard borders. The user chose `SfDataGrid` after seeing it.
  - **The compact layout** (2026-09-15, at the user's request: "limit the use of card border, and use StackPanel and Grid in positioning"). Only the two regions are bordered; the sections inside them (Recipe Structure, the side column's three, Costing's three, Packaging Link, Clone) lost their `CardStyle` boxes and are set apart by `SectionDividerStyle` lines (1 px, `CardBorderBrush`, 12 px either side) and, side by side, a 1 px column divider in the gap. Their titles use `PanelHeaderStyle` (15 px, 8 px under), in `App.xaml` beside `CardStyle`, which Raw Materials still uses unchanged. The Products views position with `Grid` and `StackPanel` only: the `DockPanel`s (list, header, section headers, form button rows, banners, breakdown rows) and the Costing `UniformGrid` were converted. Inside a tab, a section's header lost its glyph tile (the tab shows the glyph), and the sub-assembly form its grey box (the tab shows only the form); the packaging form keeps its box, above the packaging table. Spacing went from 16 to 8–12 px throughout. The recipe's tab strip starts about 150 px higher than before (with the header from the user's mockup, below), and the sub-assembly form needs little or no scrolling in a maximized 1080 p window.
    - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against a migrated copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo`: Cream Loaf's Recipe, Costing and Links & Clone tabs maximized, and at 1,100 px wide (the header's figures and the side column both stacked, nothing cut off); the Edit form in its tab; the Packaging tab, own and linked (badge, Add Packaging disabled, Edit and Remove hidden); the packaging link banner; a usage example worked out.
  - **Costing:** the five inputs are tiles (a coloured edge beside each, no box, one under another in `CostingCard`'s left column since 2026-09-19; a five-column row before), all percentages (Labor %, Overhead % and Administrative % since 2026-09-15, **of the sub-assemblies (ingredients) cost since 2026-09-20**; Markup % and VAT %), each showing what it comes to in ₱ per piece and its share of the selling price ("₱23.7729 · 11.9% of the price"). The breakdown lists sub-assemblies, packaging, labor, overhead and administrative (each labelled with its percentage, as in "Labor (25% of sub-assemblies)"), the subtotal (the base cost, highlighted), markup and VAT, then the total selling price, each with ₱ per piece and % of the price. It all comes from one `ProductCostSummary`, worked out from the form as it's edited, so unsaved changes show everywhere at once.
  - **One Save writes the whole product.**
    - Any open form is applied first, then `ProductValidator` and `CostingParametersValidator` run.
    - The form is compared with what was loaded (the baseline: the product's recipe, its packaging lines and its costing). `IProductRepository.SaveAsync(product, recipe?, packagingLines?, costing?)` gets only what changed: the whole `RecipeDraft` (its own sub-assemblies with their lines, and its links, each with its piece size) or null; the packaging lines or null; the costing or null. (Until 2026-09-14 it took each unlinked role whose usage set differed.)
    - One context and one transaction, as for Raw Materials: the product (added or updated from a copy of its fields, stamping `UpdatedAt`), its recipe, the packaging lines, the costing, then the commit. Each step goes through `internal static` write methods (`ProductRepository.AddAsync`/`UpdateAsync`, `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.SetRecipeAsync` — `SetUsagesForRoleAsync` until 2026-09-14 — `PackagingLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`, `CostingParametersRepository.SetAsync`), so every check (own and linked sub-assemblies, names, lines, piece sizes, duplicates, linked packaging, packaging type) runs, and a rejection anywhere rolls everything back — sub-assemblies included. The caller's entities are never attached, so a rolled-back save leaves no id on them. `ProductCloner` adds its product through the same static `AddAsync`.
    - Afterwards `LoadCommand` runs and a fresh copy of the product is selected (`ReloadSavedProductAsync`), so the form and its baseline are what was saved.
    - **Since 2026-09-23, Save waits for its reload**: it keeps each new section on the Sub-Assemblies screen, and until the reload shows them linked the form still has them as new, so a second Save in that moment would keep each twice. `ReloadSavedProductAsync` clears `AreDetailsReady`, which Save already waits on and the tabs' hit-testing follows, and the reload sets it again. Delete's confirmation now says the product's sub-assemblies stay on the Sub-Assemblies screen, and nothing but a packaging link refuses it.
    - Linking, copying and Make a Copy of a sub-assembly are recipe edits, saved by Save. The packaging link and Clone stay immediate: Link and Unlink Packaging reload only the link and the packaging lines with their baseline, so unsaved edits elsewhere survive. Clone copies the product as saved — its own copy of every sub-assembly. Delete's confirmation says the product's own sub-assemblies go with it, and it's refused while another product links one of them or its packaging.
    - Covered by `ProductSavePersistenceTests`, and by a `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests` test that fills in a new product and saves it with one Save.
  - **Three layout fixes came out of checking it in the app:**
    - **`MouseWheelForwarding`** (`controls:MouseWheelForwarding.IsEnabled="True"`): an `SfDataGrid` with no height shows every row and has nothing to scroll, yet its own ScrollViewer still takes the wheel, so the tab stopped scrolling whenever the pointer was over a section. It passes the wheel on to the grid's parent.
  - **Verified in the app** on the secondary monitor, against `TinapayanRMS_Demo` (restored from a `COPY_ONLY` backup and seeded with two products, one linked to the other). Each section's total equals its header. The doughnut's shares add up to 100%. The breakdown's total equals the header's price, administrative cost included. The wheel scrolls the tab over a table. A new product filled in, with its sub-assembly form still open, was saved by one Save. A usage added to a saved product was saved, Last Updated moved, and the packaging was left alone. A linked section is read-only with its badge. A raw material repriced on Raw Materials reached the sections while an unsaved labor cost was kept.
  - **Verified again after sub-assemblies became owned (2026-09-14)**, on the secondary monitor against a migrated copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo`, with real clicks and keystrokes:
    - the Sub-Assemblies tab is gone;
    - Cream Loaf Mini's former Dough-tab link shows as "Sweet Dough · From Cream Loaf", and its material cost matched the one recorded before migrating (₱10.3229);
    - Cream Loaf's Sweet Dough shows "Also used by Cream Loaf Mini, Ube Cheese Roll";
    - a new sub-assembly made in the form (a line added in grams, a yield of 100 typed, 50 g per piece worked out) was saved by Save as a typed yield, and the material cost rose by its ₱2.60;
    - removing the shared Sweet Dough and deleting Cream Loaf were both refused, naming the linking products;
    - Make a Copy on the Mini's linked dough saved it as the Mini's own at the same weight and cost;
    - linking and unlinking the Mini's packaging added and then kept Cream Loaf's box;
    - a role was added in Settings → Roles;
    - Butter Icing's BOM card shows Component and Grams only.
  - The small secondary monitor (1,366 × 768) leaves little height for the sub-assembly form under the header; it scrolls with the tab, but the lines grid's fixed height (200 px since the lines became typed into it) takes the wheel while the pointer is over it.
- **The Settings dialog holds the lookups every screen picks from: units of measure, categories, and (since 2026-09-14) sub-assembly roles** (2026-09-13, at the user's request: the embedded Manage categories panels were hard to find, and Units of Measure took a whole tab). Since 2026-09-17 it has two category tabs: **Material Categories** (the raw materials' list, `CategoriesView`, formerly just "Categories") and **Product Categories** (`ProductCategoriesView`/`ProductCategoriesViewModel`, the same shape over `IProductCategoryRepository`).
  - **It opens from the gear at the bottom of the navigation drawer.** Until 2026-09-22 that was a `FooterItems` entry with `ItemType="Button"` — Syncfusion's docs say a Button-type item "is not selectable and only ItemClicked and Command actions can be performed", so opening Settings left the current page highlighted, and Syncfusion's own `DisplayMode.xaml` demo puts Settings in `FooterItems` too. With the drawer now in `Default` mode, `FooterItems` isn't rendered at all: Settings is a `ButtonAdv` pinned below the menu's `ListBox`, **outside** it, which is what keeps it from taking the menu's selection — the same guarantee by a different route. Verified in the app: opening Settings closes the menu and leaves the highlight on the page showing.
  - **`SettingsWindow`** is a modal `Window` owned by `MainWindow`, with fixed `TabControlExt` tabs (like Products' sub-tabs: no close buttons or context menus) for `UnitsOfMeasureView`, `CategoriesView` and `SubAssemblyRolesView` (and `ProductCategoriesView` and `CostingDefaultsView` since), and a Close button (`IsCancel`, so Esc closes it too). It takes the main window's theme (`SfSkinManager.SetTheme(settings, SfSkinManager.GetTheme(this))`) and sets `SizeMode="Default"` itself (`Touch` until 2026-09-15), since a new window is a new visual tree.
  - It's **built afresh each time it opens, over singleton ViewModels** (`UnitsOfMeasureViewModel`, `CategoriesViewModel`), so a tab keeps its selection and unsaved edits between visits. Anything a view hooks onto its longer-lived ViewModel is undone with it: `SettingsWindow` registers the delete confirmations and disposes them when it closes (`RegisterNativeMessageBoxHandler` returns the registration for this), and each view's focus-the-Name-box subscription lives from `Loaded` to `Unloaded`.
  - Each opening loads both lists. When it closes, `MainWindow` refreshes the screen on show so its pickers include anything added or renamed; the other tabs pick changes up when they're next shown, as for any change.
  - **Both tabs reset their form after Save and Delete**, as New does, and title it "New Unit"/"New Category" or "Edit Unit"/"Edit Category". Before, Save and Delete cleared the list selection but left the form pointing at the last record, so the next Save silently renamed it (or failed, after a Delete) instead of adding one. The user ran into this while asking how to add a category.
- **Migrations that carry data over use hand-added SQL, each verified up and down on a restored copy of the dev database** — the three multi-unit migrations and the raw materials merge (`BACKUP … COPY_ONLY` / `RESTORE … WITH MOVE`, then `dotnet ef database update --connection` on the copy):
  - `IngredientAlternativeUnits`: each purchase unit + `PurchaseUnitQty` becomes an `IngredientUnit`, prices are linked to it, `CostPerGram` is recomputed from the true latest price (healing the same-day bug), then the old columns are dropped. The scaffold dropped the columns first; the operations were reordered so the SQL runs in between.
  - `RecipeLinesInAlternativeUnits`: `Quantity = Grams` for existing lines.
  - `PackagingMaterialAlternativeUnits`: rewritten by hand — the scaffold took `PurchaseUnitQty → CostPerBaseUnit` for a rename (both decimals). A purchase unit other than the base unit becomes a `PackagingMaterialUnit` with prices linked to it; a purchase unit equal to the base unit turns prices into per-base-unit prices; `CostPerBaseUnit` comes from the latest price. `Down` restates everything in the old one-purchase-unit shape, including packaging lines entered in an alternative unit.
  - `MergeRawMaterials` (the raw materials merge): rewritten by hand — the scaffold dropped the eight old tables before creating the four new ones. It creates `RawMaterialCategories`, `RawMaterials`, `RawMaterialUnits` and `RawMaterialPriceHistories` first, copies everything across in one SQL batch (table variables map old packaging ids to new ones), then drops the old tables. Ingredient rows keep their ids (`IDENTITY_INSERT`), so recipe lines, BOM lines and their units still point at them; packaging materials, units and prices get new ids after them, and packaging lines follow. A packaging category joins the ingredient category of the same name (case-insensitively, like the unique index), and a packaging material named like an ingredient gets " (packaging)" appended, since names are now unique across both. `Down` splits the tables back by type with every id kept, each category going to both old category tables. Verified on a restored copy of the dev database — the user's 4 ingredients, 5 categories, 5 units and 5 prices came through with identical ids and costs — then with packaging data seeded into the old schema (a category differing only in case, a material named like an ingredient, units, prices per unit and per base unit, and packaging lines in both): up, down and up again. The dev database was backed up (`COPY_ONLY`) before the migration was applied to it.
  - `AddCostBuffer`: adds `CostBufferPercent` and `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit` (both default 0), sets `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit = CostPerBaseUnit` for every material (no buffer yet, so no cost changes), then adds `CK_RawMaterials_CostBufferPercent`. Verified up, down and up on a restored copy (every row's buffered cost matched its cost; the constraint rejected 101%), then applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup.
  - `ProductDescriptionUpdatedAtAndAdministrativeCost` (2026-09-14) needed no hand SQL: it only adds columns, `Description` (`nvarchar(500)`) and `UpdatedAt` (`datetime2`), both nullable, on Products, and `AdministrativeCost` (`decimal(18,4) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`) on CostingParameters. Applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup.
  - `ProductCategories` (2026-09-17) needed no hand SQL: it adds the `ProductCategories` table (unique `Name`) and a nullable `Products.CategoryId` with a Set Null FK, so existing products stay uncategorized. Applied to the dev database.
  - `ProductCurrentPrice` (2026-09-19) needed no hand SQL: it only adds a nullable `Products.CurrentPrice` (`decimal(18,4)`), so existing products have none entered. Applied to the dev database.
  - `ProductOwnedSubAssemblies` (2026-09-14): rewritten by hand — the scaffold dropped `ProductTabLinks` outright and added `OwnerProductId` as 0. In order:
    1. **Role-tab links become sub-assembly links.** A recursive CTE follows each role-tab link (`TabKind = 0`) to the end of its chain; the linking product's own, hidden usages in that role are deleted, and the end product's usages in that role are copied to it at the same weights. Then the role-tab rows go.
    2. **Sub-assemblies no product uses are deleted** (with their lines): they can't have an owner.
    3. **Owners**: `OwnerProductId` is added nullable, set to the lowest product id using each sub-assembly, then made NOT NULL with its Cascade FK; the unique `Name` index becomes `(OwnerProductId, Name)`.
    4. **`ProductTabLinks` → `PackagingLinks`** (`RenameTable`, keeping the Packaging-tab rows): `TabKind`, `SubAssemblyRoleId`, their FK and the filtered indexes go; the PK and FKs are re-created under the new name, with a plain unique index on `ProductId`.
    5. **Grams-only lines**: `Quantity` and `UnitId` (FK, index) leave `SubAssemblyLines` and `IngredientBomLines`; `Grams` already held every line's amount.
    6. **Piece size**: `WeightPerPiece` is renamed `AuthoredValue` and `AuthoredField` added at 0 (weight per piece), so every usage keeps its weight; then `CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_AuthoredValue`, and later `ShrinkagePercent` at 0 with `CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_ShrinkagePercent` (`UsageShrinkagePercent`), so every existing usage keeps its yield.
    - `Down` resolves a typed yield back to a weight (`TotalWeight / yield`), gives lines `Quantity = Grams` with no unit, restores `ProductTabLinks` with every row a Packaging tab, and appends " (owner name)" to a sub-assembly name that another owner also uses before restoring the global unique index. It can't restore role-tab links (they stay the shared usages they became) or the units lines were entered in; the migration's comment says so.
    - Verified up, down and up on a restored copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo`: every product's sub-assembly cost per piece matched before and after each step (22.567141, 10.322941, 14.028705); Cream Loaf came to own Sweet Dough, Butter Cream, Custard Filling and Crumb Topping, and Cream Loaf Mini's Dough-tab link became a link to Sweet Dough at 60 g; the two lines entered in units kept their grams. Then, seeded into the new schema, a typed yield (100 pieces → 1 g a piece down), a sub-assembly name shared by two owners (renamed down) and a packaging link (kept both ways). Applied to the dev database (no products or sub-assemblies yet; its one BOM line kept its 500 g) after a `COPY_ONLY` backup.
  - `CostingPercentagesOfMaterialCost` (2026-09-15) needed no hand SQL: EF scaffolded three `RenameColumn`s on CostingParameters (`LaborCost` → `LaborPercent`, `OverheadCost` → `OverheadPercent`, `AdministrativeCost` → `AdministrativePercent`), same `decimal(18,4)`. **The numbers carry over unchanged, so a saved ₱4 labor cost reads as 4%.** Converting them would mean working out every product's material cost in SQL (sub-assembly piece sizes, buffered costs, packaging through its link chain), and the dev database had no costing rows to convert; only `TinapayanRMS_Demo`'s three products have old peso amounts. Verified up, down and up on a restored copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo` (the values survived each step), then applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup. In the app on that copy: Cream Loaf's ₱23.2754 material cost at 25%, 10% and 5% came to ₱5.8189, ₱2.3275 and ₱1.1638, a ₱32.5856 subtotal and ₱47.44 with a 30% markup and 12% VAT; Save stored 25, 10 and 5, and Reports showed the same ₱32.59 and ₱47.44.
  - `RawMaterialCostPrecision` (2026-09-19) needed no hand SQL: two `AlterColumn`s take `RawMaterials.CostPerBaseUnit` and `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit` from `decimal(18,4)` to `decimal(28,20)` (`HasPrecision` in `RawMaterialConfiguration`), so a buffered cost typed per bag or kilo is kept as typed once divided down to the base unit. EF warns of possible data loss because 8 integer digits replace 14; no cost per base unit comes near ₱100,000,000. 28 digits in all is what a .NET `decimal` holds, so any stored value reads back without overflowing (a larger scale, such as `decimal(38,28)`, would overflow on reading anything from ₱8 up). Existing values carry over unchanged. Applied to the dev database.
  - `StandaloneSubAssemblies` (2026-09-15), scaffolded by `dotnet ef migrations add` and left as it came (the user's instruction: never hand-write a migration): `OwnerProductId` becomes nullable (EF drops and re-creates `IX_SubAssemblies_OwnerProductId_Name` around it, unfiltered). **Its scaffolded `Down` can't run once a standalone sub-assembly exists**: it sets the missing owners to 0, which no product has, so the FK rejects it. Verified up, down and up on a restored copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo` (no standalone rows yet; the column came back nullable and the index unfiltered), then applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup.
  - `SubAssembliesAllStandalone` (2026-09-23), scaffolded by `dotnet ef migrations add` and left as it came: drops the owner FK, `IX_SubAssemblies_OwnerProductId_Name` and `OwnerProductId`, makes `Name` NOT NULL, and adds a unique `IX_SubAssemblies_Name`. It needs every owned row converted first (the one-off script under SubAssembly's Existing data): an unconverted one would come out nameless, and the unique index would refuse the migration, not lose it quietly.
  - `ProductOwnedRoles` (2026-09-23), scaffolded and left as it came: `SubAssemblyRoles.IsProductOwned` (bit, default 0, with `UpdateData` rows setting the seeded roles' to false), and `SubAssemblies.OwnerProductId` (nullable) with its index and Cascade FK to `Products`. No data step: what the flag does to existing sub-assemblies is `SubAssemblyRoleRepository.UpdateAsync`'s, done by ticking the roles in the app.
  - Always run `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes` after adding a migration, and rebuild Infrastructure before `database update --no-build` — a build from before `migrations add` doesn't contain the new migration, and EF then reports pending model changes.
- **Off-screen renders verify the card layouts without touching the user's monitor:** a file-based app (`#:project` the UI csproj) creates `new App()` + `InitializeComponent()` for the resources, builds the real ViewModel against a database (read-only), selects an item, and renders through a `VisualBrush` so the scrolled detail column comes out in full. Match `MainWindow`'s size mode: `Default` since 2026-09-15, which a new window already has (before that, `SfSkinManager.SetSizeMode(window, SizeMode.Touch)`, or inputs rendered at the compact height). For demo data, render against a restored copy, never the dev database.
- **After a command changes a row's server-computed fields (Phase 5: `DeletePriceCommand` changing `Ingredient.CurrentCost`/`CostPerGram`), refresh that one row rather than reloading the whole screen**: re-fetch it via the repository's `GetByIdAsync`, `_cache.AddOrUpdate(refreshed)` to update the grid, and reassign the `Selected*` property to the refreshed instance — `SfDataGrid` tracks selection by reference, so leaving `Selected*` pointing at the stale object silently desyncs the form/detail panel from what the grid now shows.
- **`UpdateAsync` must apply fields one by one onto a tracked, freshly-loaded entity — never `dbContext.Set<T>().Update(incoming)` on an object the caller built from scratch.** `Update()` marks *every* property Modified, so an `Ingredient`/`PackagingMaterial` built by a Save command's form fields (which never sets `CurrentCost`/`CostPerGram`, since those are server-owned) would silently overwrite them back to null/0 on every plain rename — a real bug found in Phase 6 while wiring `WeightLossPercent` through the same path, present in both `IngredientRepository` and `PackagingMaterialRepository` since Phase 4 and fixed in both at once. The fix is the same shape every time: load the tracked entity by id, copy across only the user-editable fields, leave every server-computed field untouched, then `SaveChangesAsync`. A related, easy-to-miss corollary: if a user-editable field feeds a server-computed one, the `UpdateAsync` that edits it must recompute that derived field too — it doesn't get another chance to until the next price entry. Originally `PurchaseUnitQty` feeding `CostPerGram`; since the multi-unit work it's an alternative unit's multiplier, which `RawMaterialUnitRepository.UpdateAsync` applies to the cost (and, for ingredients, to recipe-line grams).
- **`ExecuteDeleteAsync` doesn't wrap a failure in `DbUpdateException` the way `SaveChangesAsync` does** — it issues a raw `DELETE` outside the change tracker, so a foreign-key violation surfaces as the driver's own exception (`Microsoft.Data.SqlClient.SqlException` on SQL Server) instead. A test asserting `ThrowAsync<DbUpdateException>()` around an `ExecuteDeleteAsync` call needs `SqlException` instead (found in Phase 6, verifying `IngredientBomLine`'s Restrict-delete FK).
- **FluentValidation (Phase 13) is a thin, UI-facing pre-check layer added *alongside*, never in place of, the existing `ApplicationCore` Rules classes.** One `AbstractValidator<T>` per entity that actually has an editable Save form (`Ingredient`, `IngredientUnit`, `PackagingMaterial`, `PackagingMaterialUnit`, `SubAssemblyRole`, `SubAssembly`, `Product`, `CostingParameters`, `IngredientPriceHistory`, `PackagingPriceHistory` — ten total, the two unit validators added with the multi-unit work; read-only lookups like `IngredientCategory`/`PackagingCategory`/`UnitOfMeasure` deliberately got none, since there's no Save command to guard) mirrors the same rules the repository already enforces via `if/throw`. A Save/Add command runs the validator first and, on failure, sets `ErrorMessage` and returns *before* calling the repository — the repository's own checks are untouched and remain the authoritative last line of defense, so nothing about the already-tested defensive checks changes; FluentValidation only turns an invalid Save into an immediate, friendly on-screen message instead of a thrown exception the user never sees the text of. A "replace-all" set of lines or usages (the usages and packaging lines the Products Save sends, and the BOM lines the Raw Materials Save sends) gets no validator of its own — that cross-row validation already lives in the `ApplicationCore` Rules class the repository calls.
- **`ViewModelBase` (Phase 13)** — a shared `ReactiveObject` base class, introduced only once four CRUD ViewModels needed identical behavior (not before): `ErrorMessage`/`HasError` (an `ObservableAsPropertyHelper` derived from `ErrorMessage`, safe here specifically because `ErrorMessage` is itself a clean `RaiseAndSetIfChanged` property, not one driven by imperative collection mutations — see the OAPH lesson above), `IsBusy`, and a ReactiveUI `Interaction<string, bool> ConfirmDelete` the View fulfills with a real dialog. Two protected helpers wrap every command that touches the database: `RunSafelyAsync(Func<Task> action)` sets `IsBusy` for the call's duration and maps three exception types to a friendly `ErrorMessage` (`InvalidOperationException` → `ex.Message` verbatim, since every Rules class already writes a clear message; `DbUpdateException` → `"That name is already in use."`, the only scenario that type is ever thrown for anywhere in this app; `SqlException` → `"This item is currently in use elsewhere and can't be deleted."`, matching the `ExecuteDeleteAsync`-throws-`SqlException` behavior documented above) and lets any other exception type propagate uncaught, since that would be a real bug, not a user-facing condition to mask; `RunWithConfirmationAsync(message, action)` shows the confirmation dialog first and only calls `RunSafelyAsync` if the user agrees. Every command that calls a repository Delete method is wrapped in `RunWithConfirmationAsync`; every Add/Update-calling command is wrapped in `RunSafelyAsync` (with a validator pre-check, per the FluentValidation point above, when one exists for that entity); a Set-lines/Set-usages command is `RunSafelyAsync` only; a command that only mutates an in-memory edit list (`Add*Line`/`Remove*Line`/`Add*Usage`/`Remove*Usage`) or `NewCommand` is left unwrapped, since it's reversible pre-Save and carries no exception risk. `ProductsViewModel`'s tab-link commands (`LinkTabCommand`/`UnlinkTabCommand`) and `CloneCommand`/`CreateVariantCommand` are `RunSafelyAsync` only, not confirmation-gated — none of them destroy data (unlinking specifically *materializes* the tab's data rather than removing it, per the Phase 10 note above).
- **Serilog (Phase 13)** writes a daily rolling file to `logs/tinapayanrms-.log` under the app's base directory, 31-day retention, configured once via a static `Log.Logger` built in `App`'s static constructor (so even a licensing failure in that same constructor gets logged) and registered in DI as a `Serilog.ILogger` singleton. Each `ViewModelBase`-derived class calls `logger.ForContext(GetType())` once, in the shared base constructor, to get a per-class-named logger without needing a separate DI registration per ViewModel. `App.OnStartup` also hooks `DispatcherUnhandledException`/`AppDomain.UnhandledException` to log any exception that reaches those handlers.
- **Confirmation dialogs stay in the View, via a shared extension method, not baked into each View's constructor by hand.** `ConfirmDeleteHandlerRegistration.RegisterNativeMessageBoxHandler(this ViewModelBase)` wires a `ViewModelBase`'s `ConfirmDelete` interaction to a native `MessageBox.Show(..., MessageBoxButton.YesNo, MessageBoxImage.Warning)` in one place; every affected View's constructor calls `viewModel.RegisterNativeMessageBoxHandler();` once, right after setting `DataContext`.
- **A "replace-all" editor's referenced-entity validation must run *before* `ExecuteDeleteAsync`, never only implicitly via a dictionary lookup or a database FK constraint afterward** — a real, latent bug found and fixed in Phase 14's coverage audit. `PackagingLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` and `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.SetUsagesForRoleAsync` (both Phase 8) already got this right: they build a lookup of the referenced entities that actually exist and loop over the incoming rows checking each one, throwing a friendly `InvalidOperationException` immediately, all *before* the old rows are deleted. `SubAssemblyLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` (Phase 7) and `IngredientBomLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` (Phase 6) did not: both deleted the existing lines first and only discovered a bad reference afterward — the former via a plain dictionary indexer throwing a raw, unfriendly `KeyNotFoundException`; the latter via the database's own `Restrict` FK constraint throwing `DbUpdateException` at `SaveChangesAsync`. In both cases the existing, valid rows were already gone by the time the rejection happened, with no transaction to roll them back. Neither path had a test covering "the new set references something that doesn't exist," which is exactly why it went unnoticed since Phase 6/7. Fixed by moving the existence-check dictionary/set build (and a `foreach` loop throwing `InvalidOperationException` per missing id) to before the `ExecuteDeleteAsync` call in both repositories, matching the established correct shape, with a regression test in each asserting the *existing* lines survive a rejected replacement.
- **A native `ProgressBar`, not a Syncfusion busy-indicator control, for Phase 13's busy-during-save indicator** — deliberate: the app references no Syncfusion busy-indicator package, and adding one for one small piece of UX polish wasn't worth it. A plain `ProgressBar IsIndeterminate="True" Height="4"` bound to `IsBusy` via `BooleanToVisibilityConverter`, placed in a status-bar row above each screen's content alongside the `ErrorMessage` `TextBlock`, is the sanctioned native-control fallback per the Conventions section below.
  - **Retrofitting a status-bar row onto an existing `Grid` needs `Grid.Row` added to every pre-existing child, not just the new row itself.** WPF defaults an unset `Grid.Row` to `0`; inserting a new `RowDefinition="Auto"` above the existing content and giving only the *new* status-bar element `Grid.Row="0"` leaves every already-existing child (which never had an explicit `Grid.Row` before, back when there was only one row) silently defaulting to `Grid.Row="0"` too, overlapping the status bar. Caught and fixed in `IngredientsView.xaml` by re-reading the file after the first edit; applied correctly from the start in `PackagingMaterialsView.xaml` and `SubAssembliesView.xaml` afterward. `ProductsView.xaml` doesn't use a column-based `Grid` at all (its panels are a horizontally-scrolling `ScrollViewer` of `StackPanel`s, per the CRUD screen pattern note above) — for that shape, wrap the existing `ScrollViewer` in an outer two-row `Grid` (status bar in row 0, the untouched `ScrollViewer` in row 1) instead of touching every inner panel.
  - **Sending synthetic `WM_LBUTTONDOWN`/`WM_LBUTTONUP` directly to a window's `HWND` (via `SendMessage`/`PostMessage`, with screen-to-client-translated coordinates) doesn't reliably drive a Syncfusion `ButtonAdv`'s click, even though it targets the exact window and never touches the real screen cursor** (tried in Phase 13, verifying the new validation/confirmation wiring while the user had a game occupying the foreground — see the "can't win against a real foreground app" lesson above). Syncfusion's WPF controls also don't expose the standard UI Automation `InvokePattern` (`GetSupportedPatterns` on a `ButtonAdv` returns only `SynchronizedInputPattern`), so `AutomationElement`-based invocation doesn't work either. Both were dead ends for exercising a write-path command without either real OS focus or a real physical click — which matches, and reinforces, the existing Phase 5/7 guidance to rely on integration tests plus a line-by-line binding review for write paths when the app can't safely be given real focus.

## Reactive Costing Cascade

The core design problem is that a price change on any ingredient or packaging material must ripple up through everything that uses it, automatically. The cascade has two starting points, one per raw material type, and both end at the product:

```
Price entry added for an ingredient
  → purchased ingredient's CostPerBaseUnit (per gram) recomputed
    → every made ingredient that uses it recomputed, up every level of the BOM tree
      → every SubAssemblyLine using a changed ingredient recomputed
        → SubAssembly.TotalCost recomputed
          → every ProductSubAssemblyUsage of that sub-assembly recomputed ─┐
                                                                           ├→ Product total cost recomputed
Price entry added for a packaging material                                 │    → Product margin / selling price recomputed
  → packaging material's CostPerBaseUnit recomputed                        │
    → every PackagingLine using it recomputed ─────────────────────────────┘
```

**Quantity Required hangs off the end of that cascade** (2026-09-20), and adds nothing to it. It is a plain multiplier applied where the branches meet — `CalculateMaterialCost` multiplies the usages' cost per piece by it and adds the packaging once — so a product that sells in boxes of 8 moves whenever its piece does, with no propagator of its own. Nothing about it is stored derived, and no write path has to know it exists.

This replaced packs (2026-09-18 to 2026-09-20), which hung off the same end: a pack was priced from the product's material cost per piece by `PackMaterialCost = materialCostPerPiece × PiecesPerPack + that pack's own line costs`. The one cost that was a pack's alone was its own packaging lines (`PackagingLine.PackId` set), which rode the packaging branch exactly as the per-piece lines did, and `PackagingLineRepository.GetEffectiveForProductAsync` returned `PackId is null` lines only to keep the per-piece figure honest. With packs gone every packaging line is the product's, and that filter went with them.

The original idea was to model this with DynamicData `SourceCache<T>`s joined via `.Connect()`/`.InnerJoin()`/`.TransformMany()`, so derived observable collections update themselves. **That's not what got built.** Phase 6 established a different, simpler pattern first — a pure ordering function plus a stateful database orchestrator, described below — and it worked well enough that Phase 11 extended the *same* shape to the sub-assembly branch instead of introducing DynamicData joins partway through. EF Core stays the single source of truth throughout; nothing here lives only in an in-memory observable.

**Two costs go up the tree side by side** (since the buffered cost, 2026-09-14): the true `CostPerBaseUnit` and the `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit`. A purchased material's buffered cost is a fixed amount since 2026-09-19 (see Buffered cost under RawMaterial): `RawMaterialRules.ApplyBrandPrices` sets it only on a first price, and the Raw Materials Save writes it otherwise. Every write through the cascade sets both: `IngredientCostPropagator`, which runs `IngredientBomRules.CalculateCost` twice per made ingredient (true costs, then buffered costs — with nothing of its own on top since 2026-09-21, and its stored `CostBufferPercent` normalised to 0 in the same pass). Everything downstream reads the buffered one: `SubAssembly.TotalCost` (`SubAssemblyCostPropagator`, `SubAssemblyLineRepository`), sub-assembly line costs, and packaging line costs in Products and Reports. A buffer edit is one more way a cost changes: `RawMaterialRepository.UpdateAsync` reprices a purchased material's buffered cost itself and asks for the cascade for any ingredient whose buffer changed (packaging needs none, as below).

The one nesting exception is made ingredients: their BOMs nest to any depth, so that part of the cascade can't be a fixed one-hop join. An ingredient's cost can change from a new price entry, a BOM line edit, a `WeightLossPercent` edit, or a linked ingredient's link or share edit; when it does, the made ingredients that depend on it need recomputing in dependency order, components before the ingredients built from them. Sub-assemblies don't nest (a `SubAssemblyLine` only ever references an `Ingredient`, never another `SubAssembly` — Phase 7), so that branch needed no ordering logic at all, just "recompute every sub-assembly touched."

**Ingredient branch — implemented Phase 6** as `IngredientCostGraph.GetDependentsInOrder` (`ApplicationCore`, pure graph logic, no I/O — reverse-BFS from the changed ingredient to find every made ingredient that transitively depends on it, then a DFS-based topological sort restricted to that set) and `IngredientCostPropagator.RecomputeAndPropagateAsync` (`Infrastructure`, the orchestrator: loads every ingredient and every BOM line once, calls the graph function, walks the resulting order recomputing each ingredient's cost per gram (`CostPerBaseUnit`) from its own lines — reading each component's cost directly off the already-loaded, already-correct dictionary, never re-walking that component's own tree — then a single `SaveChangesAsync`). Every write path that can change an ingredient's cost calls it: `RawMaterialPriceHistoryRepository`'s add/update/delete (for an ingredient's price), `RawMaterialRepository.UpdateAsync` when `Source == Made` (a `WeightLossPercent` edit), `RawMaterialUnitRepository.UpdateAsync` when an ingredient unit's new multiplier reprices it (its latest price is in that unit), and `IngredientBomLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`. (Before the raw materials merge these were `IngredientPriceHistoryRepository` and `IngredientRepository`.) It loads ingredients only (`Type == Ingredient`), since packaging never takes part.

**Linked ingredients needed no new ordering** (2026-09-16). A linked ingredient's link is one more edge in the same graph: `IngredientCostPropagator` adds `linked → linked-to` beside every `made → component` before calling `IngredientCostGraph.GetDependentsInOrder`, which is unchanged, so egg white is recomputed after the egg and a made ingredient using egg white after egg white. In the walk, a linked ingredient is costed with `RawMaterialRules.LinkedCost` from its linked-to ingredient's already-fresh true cost, takes that ingredient's buffer percent, and its buffered cost is its cost with that percent on top, and a made one with the BOM rollup as before. A linked ingredient that's itself the changed one is recomputed first, as a made one is: `RawMaterialRepository` asks for the cascade when a linked ingredient is added or updated.

**Sub-assembly branch — implemented Phase 11** as `SubAssemblyCostPropagator.RecomputeAndPropagateAsync` (`Infrastructure`, taking the *set* of ingredient ids whose cost just changed — no per-ingredient ordering needed, since sub-assemblies don't nest: finds every `SubAssemblyLine` referencing any of them, groups by `SubAssemblyId`, and recomputes each affected sub-assembly's `TotalWeight`/`TotalCost` from *all* of its own lines' current ingredient costs — not just the changed line — in one loaded-once-saved-once pass). `IngredientCostPropagator` calls it at the end of its own run, passing `changedIngredientId` itself (whose own cost the caller already updated, for a Purchased ingredient) unioned with every made ingredient it just recomputed — so this one call, from the existing three ingredient-branch write paths, now reaches every sub-assembly transitively affected, at any BOM depth.

**Product and packaging branches needed no propagation code at all.** Neither `ProductSubAssemblyUsage`'s cost-per-piece, `PackagingLine`'s cost-per-piece, nor a product's total material cost is stored anywhere (Phase 8's "derived values are computed on read" decision) — they're plain `ProductRules` functions reading `SubAssembly.TotalCost` and the packaging material's `CostPerBaseUnit` fresh every time a screen asks. As long as those two columns are correct — which the ingredient and sub-assembly propagators now guarantee, and which the price history repository has guaranteed for packaging since Phase 5 (`RawMaterialPriceHistoryRepository` since the raw materials merge, which skips the ingredient cascade for a packaging price) — a product's displayed cost is automatically correct too, with nothing to propagate into. Verified end-to-end (`ReactiveCostCascadeTests.cs`): a price change several BOM levels deep reaches a sub-assembly's `TotalCost` with no direct edit to that sub-assembly, and a product's live-computed cost-per-piece reflects it immediately, with zero product-level or packaging-level code involved. Since 2026-09-14 a usage's weight per piece is itself computed on read (`ProductRules.ResolvePieceSize`, from the typed figure and the current `TotalWeight`), so a product that typed a yield sees its piece weight, and so its cost per piece, move when the owner changes the batch — with nothing to propagate either. Sub-assembly ownership didn't change the cascade at all: it still runs per sub-assembly, and a linked product reads the same row.

**Add-ins needed no propagation either, for the same reason** (2026-09-16). A `UsageAddInLine` stores only `UsageId`, `IngredientId` and `Grams`; its cost is `Grams × the ingredient's BufferedCostPerBaseUnit`, read fresh, and no derived total about it is stored anywhere. So the two propagators are untouched, and `SubAssemblyCostPropagator` deliberately still recomputes a sub-assembly from `SubAssemblyLines` alone — an add-in must never move the batch of a sub-assembly another product owns. `ReactiveCostCascadeTests` pins both halves: repricing an add-in's ingredient moves the linking usage's cost per piece, and leaves `SubAssembly.TotalCost` exactly where it was.

This does mean a product **already on screen** only reflects a price change made from a *different* screen once the user reselects it (or navigates back to it) — there's no cross-screen live push. That's the one piece of the original DynamicData-joins vision this phase deliberately didn't build; revisit only if that specific UX gap is ever reported as a real problem, not preemptively.

## Development Environment

- Development happens directly on a local Windows machine that has the .NET 10 SDK, SQL Server Express, and SQL Server LocalDB installed.
- Running the app (not just building it) requires the `SYNCFUSION_LICENSE_KEY` user environment variable to be set (see Syncfusion UI Framework below). The solution builds fine without it, but `App`'s static constructor throws on startup if it's missing.
- **Docker is not used anywhere in the project**: not for development, testing, or deployment.
- EF Core migrations are generated with `dotnet ef migrations add`, not written by hand, and applied to the local database with `dotnet ef database update`. Run both from the repo root with `--project src/TinapayanRMS.Infrastructure`; that project's design-time `TinapayanRMSDbContextFactory` supplies the DbContext.
- **A phase is done** when the solution builds without errors, all tests pass, and any new migration applies cleanly to a local database. Mark it Done in the Build Plan table; there are no separate per-phase documents.
- **Integration test database**: tests never touch the developer's own `TinapayanRMS` database. `tests/TinapayanRMS.Tests/Fixtures/DatabaseFixture.cs` runs against a separate `TinapayanRMS_Test` database (same server, connection string's `Initial Catalog` overridden), migrated once per test run and reset with Respawn before every test via `[Collection(DatabaseCollection.Name)]` + `IAsyncLifetime`. Only EF's own `__EFMigrationsHistory` table is excluded from the Respawn reset (`DatabaseFixture.TablesExcludedFromReset`). `UnitsOfMeasure` used to be excluded as well while migrations seeded it, and that exclusion went when the seed rows did (see the Units of Measure notes under Application Architecture). Add a table to that list if a future phase adds seeded lookup data that tests depend on.
  - **Every Respawn-backed integration test class inherits `TinapayanRMS.Tests.Fixtures.PersistenceTestBase(fixture)`** instead of retyping the `CreateDbContext`/`CreateDbContextFactory`/nested `TestDbContextFactory`/`IAsyncLifetime` plumbing per file (that scaffolding was identical across 8 test classes before being extracted). `[Collection(DatabaseCollection.Name)]` still has to be repeated on every concrete test class, though — it's deliberately **not** on the base, since xUnit's collection/fixture discovery isn't guaranteed to honor an attribute declared only on a base class, and getting that wrong would silently break the very Respawn-reset isolation this hierarchy exists to guarantee.
- **Code coverage (Phase 14): `coverlet.collector` never actually worked in this repo and was removed.** It only integrates through the classic VSTest collector pipeline, but `global.json`'s `test.runner: Microsoft.Testing.Platform` setting (needed for `dotnet test` at all under the .NET 10 SDK — see Tech Stack above) runs tests through native MTP instead, where `dotnet test --coverage`/`--collect:"XPlat Code Coverage"` silently fails ("Zero tests ran") with `coverlet.collector` present. Replaced with `Microsoft.Testing.Extensions.CodeCoverage`, the MTP-native coverage extension — `dotnet test --solution TinapayanRMS.slnx --coverage --coverage-output-format cobertura --coverage-output coverage.cobertura.xml` now works and writes a real Cobertura report to `TestResults/` (gitignored). Measured line coverage: `ApplicationCore` 99.4%, `Domain` 100%, `Infrastructure` excluding the auto-generated `Migrations` folder 97.2% (the raw tool number for `Infrastructure` reads ~32% because EF Core's generated migration `Up`/`Down`/`BuildTargetModel` methods — over 4,000 of the folder's ~5,300 lines — never execute under Respawn's reset-an-already-migrated-database model and aren't meaningfully unit-testable; exclude that folder by eye when reading the raw report, don't chase it). `UI` reads ~5% and is expected to, per the standing no-ViewModel-tests convention below.
